# 第1周

## 06月01日

### 01

Tom loved to watch the sky. He wanted to study **<abbr title="n. 气象学">**meteorology**</abbr>** when he grew up. For the school science fair, he decided to show how clouds can tell us the weather. But first, he needed to take photos from the top of Blue Hill.

Early Saturday morning, Tom was ready to **<abbr title="v. 长途步行">**tramp**</abbr>** up the hill. He asked his best friend Lisa to come. “Sorry, Tom! I must practice my new **<abbr title="n. 手风琴">**accordion**</abbr>** for the music show,” Lisa said and waved. Tom felt a little sad, but he went alone.

The long walk was hot, and Tom’s feet hurt. When he came back, Lisa didn’t even ask about his trip. This small problem **<abbr title="v. 使加剧">**exacerbated**</abbr>** the feeling between them. Slowly, the two friends started to **<abbr title="v. 使疏远">**estrange**</abbr>**. They stopped sitting together at lunch.

Tom’s grandpa saw that Tom was unhappy. “Let me tell you about my old circus days!” Grandpa said with **<abbr title="adj. 无节制的">**unbridled**</abbr>** joy. He showed Tom a picture of himself flying on a **<abbr title="n. 高空秋千">**trapeze**</abbr>**. Tom looked at the photo, but his mind was still on science.

That afternoon, Tom tried to make a rain gauge. He took some old **<abbr title="n. 陶器">**crockery**</abbr>** from the kitchen, like a big bowl and a cup. By accident, he broke a plate. “Tom! Be careful with my dishes, and clean up this mess. **<abbr title="n. 卫生">**Sanitation**</abbr>** is important!” Mom said.

Tom’s dad, Mr. Smith, is the **<abbr title="n. 系主任">**dean**</abbr>** of the science department at the local college. He came to help Tom fix the broken display board. “Let me **<abbr title="v. 加固">**bolster**</abbr>** this with some strong tape,” Dad said. He always showed great **<abbr title="n. 尊重">**esteem**</abbr>** for Tom’s hard work.

“Dad, I need to buy a special thermometer. Can I have some money?” Tom asked.

“Sorry, son. The family **<abbr title="n. 分配">**allocation**</abbr>** for fun projects is gone this month. You used your pocket money already. Your piggy bank has an **<abbr title="n. 透支">**overdraft**</abbr>**!” Dad said with a smile.

“Then maybe I can help you clean the lab after school. Professor White needs a student for an **<abbr title="n. 助教职位">**assistantship**</abbr>**. The **<abbr title="n. 报酬">**remuneration**</abbr>** is ten dollars a week,” Dad said. Tom agreed happily.

Tom’s new rain gauge was so clever. Dad said, “This idea is special. You should get a **<abbr title="n. 专利">**patent**</abbr>** for it!” Tom painted a cloud **<abbr title="n. 图案">**motif**</abbr>** on the side of the gauge. It looked very nice.

On the day of the fair, Tom stood by his project. Lisa came to his table. “Tom, I am sorry I was so busy with my music. I saw that our friendship was getting weak. I felt a deep **<abbr title="n. 失望">**disenchantment**</abbr>** when you stopped talking to me,” she whispered.

Suddenly, Lisa held her stomach. “It hurts so much!” she cried. The pain was **<abbr title="adj. 急性的">**acute**</abbr>**. Tom held her hand and shouted for the school nurse.

Later, at the nurse’s office, Lisa felt better. “Thank you, Tom. You are a true friend,” she said. Tom smiled. The weather project was great, but getting his friend back felt much better.

### 02

My family went on a big <abbr title="n. 乘船游览">**cruise**</abbr> last summer. The ship was like a floating town. On the second day, the captain said, “We are crossing the <abbr title="n. 赤道">**equator**</abbr> now.” He pointed to the line that runs around the middle of the Earth. Everyone cheered. I looked out at the blue sea and felt excited.

The ship was full of <abbr title="n. 多样性">**diversity**</abbr>. You could hear many languages – Spanish, Chinese, and Arabic. In the dining room, there were foods from ten different countries. Mom said it was the best way to learn about the world.

Near the pool, there was a small <abbr title="n. 零售">**retail**</abbr> shop. It sold hats, toys, and keychains. A sign on the wall read: “No extra <abbr title="n. 关税">**tariff**</abbr> on these items! You don’t need to pay more tax.” The shopkeeper said, “But if you hide items and don’t tell the officers later, they can <abbr title="v. 处罚">**penalize**</abbr> you.” I bought a little dolphin toy and threw the paper bag into a <abbr title="n. 垃圾桶">**bin**</abbr>.

One morning, I visited the science corner. A teacher was showing the <abbr title="n. 结构；解剖">**anatomy**</abbr> of a fish. She had a big plastic fish and opened it to show the heart and bones. Some kids covered their eyes. “That’s <abbr title="adj. 野蛮的">**barbaric**</abbr>!” one boy cried. “Why cut it open?” But the teacher smiled. “It’s just a model, and we learn by looking inside.” I thought for a moment and then <abbr title="v. 认为">**deemed**</abbr> it not scary at all. Learning felt like an adventure.

Later, I went to a room with a historical <abbr title="n. 回顾展">**retrospective**</abbr>. Old black-and-white photos showed the first cruise ships. A corner told a sad story: an old sailor was buried at sea, his last resting place a deep, silent <abbr title="n. 坟墓">**grave**</abbr> under the waves. I shivered a little and walked away.

The most exciting thing on the ship was a giant water slide. It was shaped like a <abbr title="n. 螺旋形">**helix**</abbr>, a shiny yellow tube that twisted around and around like a big spring. I stared at it, but my legs felt weak. I am usually a <abbr title="adj. 胆小的">**timid**</abbr> girl – new things often scare me. But I remembered I am the <abbr title="adj. 唯一的">**sole**</abbr> child in my family, and I wanted my parents to be proud. So I took a deep breath and climbed up.

At the top, I closed my eyes and pushed off. Whoosh! I twisted down the **helix** faster and faster. At the bottom, everyone cheered. I stood up, brushed water from my hair, and gave a small <abbr title="v. 鞠躬">**bow**</abbr>. “I did it!” I shouted.

After that, I walked to the deck and saw a boy I knew from the kids’ club. He was holding a small <abbr title="n. 包裹">**parcel**</abbr> wrapped in brown paper. He was a <abbr title="n. 门徒；追随者">**disciple**</abbr> of the ship’s insect expert. He opened the **parcel** carefully, and a big green <abbr title="n. 蚱蜢">**grasshopper**</abbr> jumped out! It landed on my arm, and we both laughed.

At dinner, my little baby brother was crying. Mom looked tired. “I’m trying to <abbr title="v. 使戒掉">**wean**</abbr> him off the bottle,” she said. “He needs to drink from a cup now.” She handed him a sippy cup, and soon he was quiet.

That night, I lay in my bunk and thought about the **cruise**. I had seen the **equator**, faced my fear on the **helix**, learned about fish **anatomy**, and even met a **grasshopper**. It was the best trip ever.

### 03

Leo woke up on a Saturday morning. His legs were still <abbr title="adj. 酸痛的">**sore**</abbr> from yesterday’s football game. It hurt to walk. He remembered his grandpa’s wise <abbr title="n. 忠告">**counsel**</abbr>: “Always check your tools before you start.” Today he would <abbr title="v. 开始">**embark**</abbr> on his big science project for school. It was the third <abbr title="n. 学期">**trimester**</abbr>, and Invention Day was near. The teacher had decided to <abbr title="v. 委托">**commission**</abbr> Leo to build a working model of a solar-powered car. Leo felt proud, <abbr title="conj. 虽然">**albeit**</abbr> a little nervous.

He went downstairs. Mom said, “Eat your oatmeal; it has <abbr title="n. 纤维">**fiber**</abbr> that helps your body.” She also added, “You need to <abbr title="v. 接种疫苗">**vaccinate**</abbr> later at the clinic. The nurse gives flu shots.” After breakfast, Leo went to the backyard. He picked up the long green <abbr title="n. 软管">**hose**</abbr> to water the dry plants. As water sprayed, he thought, “I could use a small pump like this to move water, maybe even <abbr title="v. 利用">**harness**</abbr> the power of water flow!” That gave him an idea.

Back inside, he looked at his backpack. It had a secret <abbr title="n. 隔间">**compartment**</abbr> where he kept a tiny screwdriver and his lucky marble. Then the phone rang. It was his friend Amy. She said, “Leo, if you don’t help me with my project, you aren’t a real friend.” Leo knew Amy was often <abbr title="adj. 操纵的">**manipulative**</abbr> like that. He said he was busy, and she grew very <abbr title="adj. 易怒的">**irritable**</abbr>, shouting about how unfair he was. Leo sighed. This was a <abbr title="adj. 令人沮丧的">**dispiriting**</abbr> start to his day.

Later, Leo went to the community center for a special workshop. Only five kids were invited; it was an <abbr title="adj. 专属的">**exclusive**</abbr> event. He took his notebook, <abbr title="conj. 以免">**lest**</abbr> he forget any important details. At the workshop, an old engineer taught them how to <abbr title="v. 校准">**calibrate**</abbr> a sensor. He showed a small dial and said, “Turn it until the needle points to zero. That’s how you calibrate it to get the right reading.”

After the workshop, Leo saw a poster about a drive for <abbr title="adj. 贫困的">**impoverished**</abbr> families. The poster explained that some families had almost no money for food or clothes. Leo decided to <abbr title="v. 赋予">**endow**</abbr> some of his old toys to the collection box. He felt good about that.

Monday came. At school, the science teacher played a speech by a famous professor. The speech was too <abbr title="adj. 高雅的">**highbrow**</abbr> for Leo; the professor used many long words and ideas that Leo could not follow at all. He wished for a simple explanation. During lunch, Tim, a boy from another class, was very **irritable**. His project from the first **trimester** had failed, and he was still angry about it. He pushed his tray away and frowned. Leo thought maybe Tim needed some kind **counsel** too.

After school, Leo finally started to build his solar car. He remembered the **counsel** from his grandpa and the workshop. He carefully **calibrated** the solar panel angle so it faced the sun just right. He used a motor and a belt to **harness** the spinning power. The car was ready. On Invention Day, his model worked perfectly. The teacher smiled and said, “Leo, you did a great job **embarking** on this challenge. You even helped collect toys for **impoverished** kids.” Leo felt his hard work was worth it, **albeit** the journey had some **dispiriting** moments. And he never forgot to eat enough **fiber**!

## 06月02日

### 01

The school basketball <abbr title="n. 锦标赛">**tournament**</abbr> was just one day away. All students were excited. Mike was the most <abbr title="adj. 精英的">**elite**</abbr> player on our team. But he also had a bad <abbr title="n. 脾气">**temper**</abbr>. He often yelled at us when we missed a shot.

I was part of the setup team. Our job was to build a small fence around the court. We needed wooden <abbr title="n. 尖桩">**pickets**</abbr> to make the fence. Coach handed me a <abbr title="n. 锯子">**saw**</abbr> and some long wood sticks. "Cut the wood into **pickets**," he said. I started working.

Mike walked by and watched. He did not offer to help. "You are too slow," he said with <abbr title="n. 恶意">**spite**</abbr> in his voice. I knew he was still angry because I scored more points than him in the last practice game. I ignored him and kept cutting. Then, a heavy <abbr title="n. 大块">**chunk**</abbr> of wood fell off the table. It hit my right <abbr title="n. 小腿">**shin**</abbr>. A sharp pain shot up my leg. I cried out.

Mike laughed. "Stop pretending. You just want to skip the work." He did not believe me. His words <abbr title="v. 欺骗">**deceived**</abbr> everyone nearby; they thought I was just acting. But then someone saw blood. The pain was real. The **picket** I was holding had a pointed tip. When I fell, the tip <abbr title="v. 刺">**stabbed**</abbr> into my leg just below the knee. The cut was deep. Blood was <abbr title="adj. 大量的">**profuse**</abbr> — it soaked my sock quickly.

Coach ran over. "Someone call the nurse! We need to <abbr title="v. 优先处理">**prioritize**</abbr> this cut right now." Nurse Kim arrived and used a cloth to <abbr title="v. 覆盖">**shroud**</abbr> the wound. Then she placed a <abbr title="n. 夹板">**splint**</abbr> on my leg to keep it still. "You must go to the hospital," she said. I felt tears in my eyes. The **tournament** would start soon, and now I could not play.

Mike's smile faded. He realized he had been wrong. It was not a trick. He had acted out of **spite**, and now his **temper** and **spite** had hurt someone. His face turned pale.

Coach looked at the team. "We need someone to <abbr title="v. 平衡">**counterbalance**</abbr> Mike's scoring. Who can play in Tom's place?" Nobody answered. Then a quiet girl named Lucy stepped forward. She had always been shy. But she had a <abbr title="adj. 潜在的">**latent**</abbr> talent for shooting. She practiced alone almost every day, <abbr title="adv. 察觉不到地">**imperceptibly**</abbr> getting better and better. Nobody really noticed her skills. But Coach had seen her once. "Lucy, you're in," he said.

That evening, the <abbr title="n. 泛光灯">**floodlights**</abbr> above the court were turned on. The bright **floodlights** made the whole place shine. The final game <abbr title="v. 达到高潮">**culminated**</abbr> — our team against the Lions. Mike played hard, but he also tried to control his **temper**. Lucy's shooting was amazing. She moved and shot **imperceptibly**, like a quiet storm. Her points helped **counterbalance** the other team's star player.

In the end, we won by two points. The **tournament** was over. We gathered around Lucy and cheered. Mike came to the hospital to see me. "From <abbr title="adv. 从今以后">**henceforth**</abbr>, I promise to control my temper and never act out of **spite** again," he said. I smiled. My leg still hurt, but I knew our team was stronger now. Even a **splint** could not stop my happiness. And a **chunk** of ice on my **shin** felt nice.

### 02

Tom was a young <abbr title="n. 少年犯">**delinquent**</abbr>. He often skipped school and got into fights. One cold morning, a judge gave him a strange choice: go to an <abbr title="adj. 南极的">**Antarctic**</abbr> <abbr title="n. 团体">**corps**</abbr> or stay in a small room for a year. Tom chose the **corps**. He did not know what to expect.

The ship sailed south for many days. Tom stared at the gray sea and felt a deep <abbr title="n. 孤独">**solitude**</abbr>. There were no friends, no warm streets. Just water and wind. When they reached the **Antarctic**, everything was white. The cold air bit his skin.

The **corps** was a group of young people like him. They worked to study the ice and stop pollution. Tom was a <abbr title="n. 新手">**novice**</abbr>, so he had to learn everything. His first task was to seal a crack in a storage hut. The leader, Ms. Lee, handed him a tube of <abbr title="n. 硅胶">**silicon**</abbr>. “This will stop the cold air from getting in,” she said. Tom’s fingers became <abbr title="adj. 僵硬的">**stiff**</abbr> from the cold. The **silicon** would not come out easily. He pushed hard. The next morning, his hands started to <abbr title="v. 肿胀">**swell**</abbr>. They hurt a lot. Ms. Lee gave him warm water to soak them and <abbr title="v. 赞扬">**commended**</abbr> him for trying his best.

But not everyone was kind. A boy named Ben tried to <abbr title="v. 欺负">**victimise**</abbr> Tom. He told everyone a <abbr title="n. 捏造">**fabrication**</abbr>: that Tom had wasted the **silicon** on purpose. Tom did not understand why Ben would use such an <abbr title="n. 诡计">**artifice**</abbr>. Luckily, Ms. Lee had an <abbr title="n. 才能">**aptitude**</abbr> for seeing the truth. She asked to see the work log and the <abbr title="n. 规格说明">**specification**</abbr> of the task. The **specification** listed how much **silicon** was needed. The numbers matched exactly. She said, “We must <abbr title="v. 教导">**edify**</abbr> each other, not tear down.” Her words **edify**ed Tom. He learned that honesty was important.

That evening, the group ate a meal together. For dessert, the cook had made a <abbr title="n. 糕点">**pastry**</abbr>. It was a sweet, flaky treat with jam inside. Tom thought of his mother’s kitchen. A deep <abbr title="n. 后悔">**regret**</abbr> filled him. He **regret**ted all the trouble he had caused back home.

After dinner, a scientist showed them a test tube. “This is <abbr title="n. 甲烷">**methane**</abbr> gas,” she said. “When ice melts, **methane** goes into the air and makes the Earth warmer.” Tom looked at the tube. The clear gas seemed like nothing, but it was a big problem.

The days passed. Tom woke up each morning with **stiff** muscles. The **swell** in his hands went down as he became stronger. As a **novice**, he made mistakes, but he learned from them. The wind sometimes blew snow so hard that everything turned into a <abbr title="n. 模糊">**blur**</abbr>. Once, an <abbr title="adj. 奇异的">**exotic**</abbr> bird waddled close — an emperor penguin with bright yellow feathers. Tom sat still and watched. The **solitude** he once felt was now a peaceful quiet. He was no longer the angry **delinquent** from the city. The **fabrication** and **artifice** of others did not matter anymore.

When the ship came to take them home, Tom looked back at the **Antarctic**. He knew he would never forget this place. He had found something true in himself. He was ready to start a new life.

### 03

Tom is a 12-year-old boy. He loves <abbr title="n. 击剑运动">**fencing**</abbr>. In **fencing**, he wears a white suit and holds a thin sword. His main <abbr title="n. 竞争者">**competitor**</abbr> is a girl named Lily. They practice hard every week.

One day, Tom’s dog Bella had three <abbr title="n. 后代">**offspring**</abbr>. The puppies were very small. But one puppy had a problem. Its <abbr title="n. 大腿">**thigh**</abbr> looked red and big. The leg had a <abbr title="n. 缺陷">**defect**</abbr>—it was bent and weak. Tom felt worried. He stayed home to care for the puppy. This caused <abbr title="n. 缺勤">**absenteeism**</abbr> from school. He missed four days. His mom kept <abbr title="n. 不停唠叨">**harping**</abbr> on him, “Tom, you must go to school! Stop staying home!” She said it again and again.

Tom took the puppy to the vet. The vet said, “We must look at the puppy <abbr title="adv. 整体地">**holistically**</abbr>. We check his leg, his food, and even his <abbr title="n. 胰腺">**pancreas**</abbr>.” The **pancreas** helps digest food, but the puppy’s was weak. So the puppy could not eat well. The vet gave an <abbr title="n. 抗生素">**antibiotic**</abbr> to stop the sickness. Then he put a <abbr title="n. 石膏">**plaster**</abbr> cast on the puppy’s hurt **thigh**. “Keep this **plaster** dry for two weeks,” said the vet.

Tom bought a soft <abbr title="n. 垫子">**mat**</abbr> for the puppy to sleep on. But the next morning, he found tiny bugs in the room. They were <abbr title="n. 白蚁">**termites**</abbr>! The **termites** liked wood, so they chewed the floor. They also ate part of the **mat** and some <abbr title="n. 纺织品">**textile**</abbr> things, like an old rug. Tom’s mom had to call a bug man to stop them.

Tom gently held the puppy’s small paw. The puppy playfully bit his finger. Even so tiny, it had a strong little <abbr title="n. 紧握">**grip**</abbr>. Tom smiled.

At school, Tom faced a <abbr title="n. 制裁">**sanction**</abbr> for his **absenteeism**. The school’s **sanction** was detention after class. His classmates felt that was not fair. They showed strong <abbr title="n. 团结">**solidarity**</abbr>. They wrote a letter together: “Please do not give Tom a **sanction**. He was helping a sick puppy.” The principal saw their **solidarity** and said, “Okay, no detention this time.”

Tom’s big <abbr title="n. 志向">**aspiration**</abbr> is to become a vet. He wants to help all animals. For the school science fair, he made a project about how **termites** damage **textile** items. He built a small house model. He also made a <abbr title="n. 复制品">**duplicate**</abbr> of the model, just in case one got broken. Lily was his **competitor** again. She showed a project about sunflowers.

The special food for the puppy had to come from a far city. It was sent by <abbr title="n. 货物">**freight**</abbr> train. The **freight** arrived on Monday. Tom was so happy. Soon, the puppy’s **thigh** got stronger with the **plaster**. Tom still went to **fencing** practice and tried to beat his **competitor**, but he never missed school again. His mom also stopped **harping**.

## 06月03日

### 01

Tom loved digging in the backyard. His grandpa gave him a small shovel and a brush. “This shovel has a one-year <abbr title="n. 保修单">**warranty**</abbr>,” Grandpa said. Tom smiled and started to dig.

His little sister, Lily, sat under a tree, looking very <abbr title="adj. 懒惰的">**slothful**</abbr>. “Why don’t you help?” Tom asked. Lily just yawned. She was always **slothful** on sunny days.

Tom dug and dug. The ground was hard. Grandpa said, “This is the top of the <abbr title="n. 岩石圈">**lithosphere**</abbr>.” Tom asked, “What is the **lithosphere**?” “It is the hard, rocky skin of the earth,” Grandpa explained. “Be careful not to <abbr title="v. 推翻">**overturn**</abbr> the water bucket.”

Suddenly, Tom’s shovel hit something hard. He <abbr title="v. 擦">**wipe**</abbr>d the dirt away. “Is this an <abbr title="adj. 考古的">**archaeological**</abbr> find?” Grandpa said, “Maybe. We find **archaeological** treasures <abbr title="adv. 零星地">**sporadically**</abbr>.” Tom guessed **sporadically** meant “not often.”

Tom dug more carefully. He found a round, white object. “A <abbr title="n. 头骨">**skull**</abbr>!” he yelled. Lily ran over, forgetting her **slothful** mood. “Is it a dinosaur **skull**?” she asked. Grandpa studied it. “No, it is a human **skull**. Very old.”

Grandpa began to <abbr title="v. 哼唱">**hum**</abbr> a soft tune. Then he stopped **hum**ming and said, “I will tell you a story full of <abbr title="n. 激起兴趣">**intrigue**</abbr>.” He spoke in a <abbr title="adj. 冷静的">**dispassionate**</abbr> voice. “Long ago, a brave <abbr title="n. 传教士">**missionary**</abbr> came here. A fierce <abbr title="n. 敌人">**foe**</abbr> attacked him. The battle was <abbr title="adj. 喧闹的">**raucous**</abbr>—full of loud shouts. But in the end, peace did <abbr title="v. 获胜">**prevail**</abbr>. The **missionary**’s words made the **foe** stop fighting. The **raucous** noise turned into quiet. When peace **prevail**s, people are happy.”

Tom was **intrigue**d. “How do you know?” Grandpa’s voice stayed **dispassionate**. “I read old books. A **dispassionate** mind learns much.” Tom felt more **intrigue**.

They dug deeper. Tom’s shovel hit a rock with strange marks. “These marks are <abbr title="adj. 未分化的">**undifferentiated**</abbr>,” Grandpa said. The **undifferentiated** scratches looked like messy letters.

Then Tom hit the water bucket. It **overturn**ed! Water splashed on the rock. “Oh no!” Tom **wipe**d the rock with a cloth. As he **wipe**d, the **undifferentiated** marks became clearer. “Look!” He **wipe**d more.

Lily began to <abbr title="v. 嘀咕">**mutter**</abbr>, “I’m bored.” She **mutter**ed this <abbr title="adv. 总是">**invariably**</abbr> every ten minutes. And **invariably**, Tom said, “Just wait!”

Their neighbor Mr. Green walked over. He was in a <abbr title="adj. 手术后的">**postoperative**</abbr> recovery after leg surgery. “Can I join your **archaeological** dig? This is my **postoperative** exercise.” He sat down and helped **wipe** the rock. But when he stood up, he accidentally **overturn**ed his chair. Tom helped him.

Grandpa said, “The **warranty** card says this shovel can dig into the **lithosphere** without breaking.” They all laughed.

At last, they found a clay box. “This might <abbr title="v. 给...权利">**entitle**</abbr> us to name it,” Grandpa said. “The law **entitle**s finders to give a name.” Tom thought. “Let’s **entitle** it ‘Peace Skull’ because of the **missionary** and the **foe** who found peace.” Everyone agreed.

**Raucous** laughter filled the yard. Even **slothful** Lily was excited. She forgot to **mutter**. Grandpa **hum**med again. Tom learned that digging can be full of **intrigue** and joy.

### 02

Tim and Sara were working on a science project. They wanted to study the **<abbr title="n. 成分">**composition**</abbr>** of dirty water from the river. Their teacher would **<abbr title="v. 主持">**host**</abbr>** a school science fair next week, and the winner could visit a big lab.

First, they collected a bottle of **<abbr title="n. 废水">**effluent**</abbr>** from a pipe near the old factory. The water smelled bad and looked brown. “This **effluent** must be cleaned before it flows into the river,” Sara said. Tim nodded. He added some tiny **<abbr title="n. 酶">**enzyme**</abbr>** powder into the bottle. The **enzyme** could break down the dirt in the water. They left the bottle overnight.

The next morning, they came to the school **<abbr title="n. 附属建筑">**annex**</abbr>**, a small building behind the playground. It was their lab for the week. Tim turned on the lamp and the room was **<abbr title="adj. 照亮的">**lit**</abbr>** brightly. They saw the **effluent** was now clearer. “The **enzyme** works!” Tim said. But they still needed to filter it. Sara took a **<abbr title="n. 果壳">**husk**</abbr>** from a coconut and filled it with small **<abbr title="n. 卵石">**pebble**</abbr>**s. She also used a **<abbr title="n. 螺丝钉">**screw**</abbr>** to fix the **husk** onto a stand. When they poured the water through, the **pebble**s caught the big bits. The water’s **<abbr title="n. 质地">**texture**</abbr>** became smooth, not slimy. They felt the **texture** with their fingers.

But then a boy named Leo came into the **annex**. He also had a project. He wanted to **<abbr title="v. 侵犯">**encroach**</abbr>** on their space. “This table is mine,” he said, pushing their bottle. Tim, an **<abbr title="adj. 勇敢的">**intrepid**</abbr>** boy, did not back away. “Don’t **encroach** on our work,” he said calmly. Leo laughed but left. Tim’s **intrepid** words saved their project.

They needed to **<abbr title="v. 汇编">**compile**</abbr>** all their notes and numbers. Sara wrote down how much cleaner the water got. They also made a quick **<abbr title="n. 民意调查">**poll**</abbr>** among classmates, asking what color clean water should be. Most kids said blue or clear. They used the **poll** results in their poster.

Just before the fair, they went to the **annex** to **<abbr title="v. 排练">**rehearse**</abbr>** their talk. Tim held the bottle while Sara explained the steps. They had a good opening **<abbr title="n. 策略">**gambit**</abbr>**: “Have you ever seen a river cry?” Their **gambit** made people stop and listen. They **rehearse**d three times until everything was perfect.

But on the fair day, trouble came. As Tim carried the water sample, he bumped into Leo. The two bottles **<abbr title="v. 碰撞">**collide**</abbr>**d and fell. Dirty water spilled over their clean sample! Tim saw the brown color mix back in. This could be an **<abbr title="adj. 不可逆的">**irreversible**</abbr>** mistake. Once mixed, the change was **irreversible**. They had no time to clean it again. The damage would not **<abbr title="v. 停止">**cease**</abbr>** worrying them. However, Sara did not let her hope **<abbr title="v. 受煎熬">**languish**</abbr>**. She grabbed a spare filter with fresh **pebble**s and a new **husk**. Quickly, they poured the spilled water through it. The color did not fully **cease** to be brown, but it got lighter. Sara did not let the project **languish** in failure; she acted fast.

Their **host**, Mrs. Chen, came to their table. Tim explained what happened, and then showed how their **composition** of **enzyme** and filter could still clean some dirt. The **intrepid** pair answered every question. Mrs. Chen smiled. “Good **gambit** and quick thinking,” she said. They did not win first place, but they got a special prize for bravery. As they left the **annex**, the **lit** windows shone on their happy faces. They knew no **effluent** problem was too big if you were **intrepid** and worked together.

### 03

Today was the school Fun Fair. Tom and his sister Lucy were very <abbr title="adj. 热衷的">**keen**</abbr>. They woke up early and could not wait to go.

When they arrived, they saw the school hall. It had real <abbr title="n. 宏伟">**grandeur**</abbr>. There were gold curtains, bright lights, and a big stage. Lucy's eyes opened wide. "It's like a king's palace!" she said.

First, they went to the voting area. The school was going to pick a new student <abbr title="n. 学生代表">**senator**</abbr>. Max, a well-known <abbr title="n. 改革者">**reformer**</abbr>, was one of the boys who wanted the job. He wanted to change the old lunch rules. He had a clear <abbr title="n. 立场">**stance**</abbr>: every child should have more fruit and less junk food. Another boy wanted to be senator too. He tried to <abbr title="v. 歪曲">**skew**</abbr> Max's ideas. He said, "Max wants to stop all sweets!" But that was not true. Max was ready. He could <abbr title="v. 证实">**substantiate**</abbr> his plan with a paper from the health teacher. He showed it to everyone. The crowd looked at the other boy with <abbr title="n. 蔑视">**contempt**</abbr>. Many students shook their heads. The teacher then said, "All students, from big to small, are <abbr title="v. 给予选举权">**enfranchised**</abbr> to vote. Your voice matters." For many, the <abbr title="n. 决定因素">**determinant**</abbr> was trust. Max's honest **stance** won the day.

Then, Tom and Lucy went to the talent show. A <abbr title="n. 剧团">**troupe**</abbr> of dancers and singers came on stage. The actors had colorful <abbr title="n. 化妆品">**makeup**</abbr> on their faces. One girl sang <abbr title="adv. 抒情地">**lyrically**</abbr>. Her voice was soft and sweet, like a bird. Lucy was also **keen** on singing. She listened with all her heart. She dreamed of singing **lyrically** too.

After that, they visited the art room. There were white candles. A sign next to them said: "<abbr title="n. 蜡">**Wax**</abbr> comes from honeybees." Next to it, a parent showed metal art. He talked about <abbr title="n. 熔炼">**smelting**</abbr>—heating metal until it melts. Tom thought the hot, red liquid was amazing.

Outside, some dads helped <abbr title="v. 割草">**mow**</abbr> the school lawn. The grass had grown fast. Tom's father was pushing a loud machine. The cut grass flew into the air and smelled fresh. Lucy covered her ears but smiled.

Near the school nurse's room, the nurse had a small table. She held up a small bottle. "This is a <abbr title="n. 麻醉剂">**narcotic**</abbr>," she said. "It is a strong pain medicine. Only a doctor can give it. You must never touch it alone." Tom remembered when Jack, his friend, had a bad fall and needed a **narcotic** from the hospital.

Tom then felt sad. Jack could not come to the fair. He had a <abbr title="n. 停学">**suspension**</abbr> from school. He drew on the hallway wall and got in trouble. But Tom knew Jack was sorry. Tom planned to visit Jack after the fair and help him with his math.

Back at the games, Tom tried the ring toss. He was usually good at it. But his <abbr title="n. 自负">**ego**</abbr> grew too big. He thought, "I'm the best; I don't need to try hard." So he threw the rings without care. He missed every one. The prize went to a younger boy. Tom learned a lesson: too much **ego** can make you lose.

Finally, the whole fair was <abbr title="v. 资助">**subsidized**</abbr> by the parents' group. All the games and treats were free. Tom and Lucy ate popcorn and laughed. At the end of the day, they walked home under the orange sunset, tired but happy. They had learned many new things, and Tom had learned to keep his **ego** small.

### 04

On a small island in <abbr title="n. 大洋洲">**Oceania**</abbr>, there was a sunny beach with an old wooden <abbr title="n. 码头">**dock**</abbr>. Every day, Lily the rabbit and her friend Spike the hedgehog sat on the **dock** and watched the blue sea. Spike was a little <abbr title="adj. 带刺的">**spined**</abbr> animal, but he was very kind.

One morning, they found a shiny <abbr title="n. 陶瓷">**ceramic**</abbr> bottle floating by the **dock**. Inside was a piece of paper. It was a <abbr title="adj. 地质的">**geological**</abbr> map! The map showed a secret <abbr title="n. 喷泉">**fountain**</abbr> deep in the island. Next to the **fountain**, there was a strange rock called <abbr title="n. 铀">**uranium**</abbr>. The note said the **uranium** could make things warm without fire.

Lily was excited, but she was a little <abbr title="adj. 怀疑的">**sceptical**</abbr>. “Can a rock really do that?” she asked. Spike, who was very <abbr title="adj. 天真的">**ingenuous**</abbr>, said, “Let’s go and see! It sounds so fun.” His **ingenuous** smile made Lily agree. They decided to follow the map.

They walked <abbr title="adv. 朝陆地">**landward**</abbr> from the beach. Soon they came to a tall green <abbr title="n. 树篱">**hedge**</abbr>. Behind the **hedge** was a small <abbr title="n. 住所">**dwelling**</abbr>. An old turtle lived there. The turtle looked worried. “Be careful,” she said. “Beyond my **dwelling**, there are <abbr title="adj. 恶性的">**malignant**</abbr> vines. Those vines make the land <abbr title="adj. 贫瘠的">**sterile**</abbr> and nothing can grow.”

Spike felt <abbr title="adj. 矛盾的">**ambivalent**</abbr>. He wanted to see the **fountain**, but he was scared. “Don’t worry,” Lily said. “If the vines try to hurt you, I will <abbr title="v. 干预">**intervene**</abbr>!” Spike nodded. His **spined** back could also help cut the vines.

They walked on. The land became **sterile** and grey. Then they saw a fox standing near a trap. The fox was trying to <abbr title="v. 部署">**deploy**</abbr> a net. “Aha!” said the fox. “I know your plan. I need the **uranium** for my <abbr title="n. 推销">**merchandising**</abbr> business. I sell **ceramic** cups at the **dock**, and hot cups sell better!” The fox had a clever <abbr title="n. 计划">**scheme**</abbr> to take the **uranium**. His **scheme** was bad because the **uranium** belonged to the whole island.

Lily was angry. Spike was frightened and blamed Lily for trusting the map. They had a short <abbr title="n. 破裂">**breakup**</abbr> right there. Spike ran off. But the fox’s net caught Spike! The fox laughed. Lily knew she had to **intervene**. She used a stick to break the net. Spike used his **spined** body to scratch the fox’s paw. The fox cried and ran away.

Lily and Spike said sorry to each other. Their **breakup** ended. They reached the **fountain**. Water splashed up, and the **uranium** rock glowed warmly. They poured some **fountain** water onto the **sterile** land. At once, the **malignant** vines turned brown and died. Soon green shoots appeared. The **landward** area became alive again.

They carried a small piece of **uranium** back to the village. The villagers used it to warm the **dwelling** of the old turtle. The fox learned his lesson and stopped his **scheme**. He helped with real **merchandising**, selling **ceramic** art from the island. Spike was no longer **ambivalent** about adventures, and his **ingenuous** trust in friends stayed. All was well in **Oceania**.

### 05

Tom and his friends were helping at the town’s annual summer fair. The fair was always held in a <abbr title="adj. 乡村的">**rural**</abbr> area, with big green fields and a few old barns. A <abbr title="adj. 公共的">**communal**</abbr> garden near the main tent needed work, so the kids took their tools and got started.

First, they had to trim the <abbr title="n. 灌木">**shrub**</abbr>s that grew too tall. Tom used a small knife to <abbr title="v. 切开">**incise**</abbr> a line on a thick branch before snapping it. That made the job easier. Leo tried to do the same, but he was not careful. A <abbr title="n. 意外">**contingency**</abbr> happened—the knife slipped and cut his finger deeply. Everyone stopped. The bleeding would not stop, so they quickly took Leo to the small <abbr title="n. 医务室">**infirmary**</abbr> by the parking lot.

While Leo was gone, Mia worked on the <abbr title="n. 灌溉">**irrigation**</abbr> system for the tomato plants. An old engine pumped water through long tubes, but it made thick black <abbr title="n. 烟雾">**fumes**</abbr> that smelled terrible. Mia coughed and pointed to her throat. “My <abbr title="n. 喉">**larynx**</abbr> hurts from that smoke,” she whispered. Later, she tried to sing for the talent show practice, but her voice sounded rough and <abbr title="adj. 平庸的">**mediocre**</abbr>. She could barely hit the notes because of the pain in her throat.

After the garden work, the baking contest tent became very noisy. Anna and Ben both <abbr title="v. 声称">**professed**</abbr> that the prize‑winning chocolate cake recipe was their own idea. “I invented it last week!” Anna shouted, but Ben said the same. This created a <abbr title="n. 分裂">**schism**</abbr> in their friendship group. Some kids stood by Anna, others by Ben. The tent was split in two. The fair organizer shook her head. “Using someone else’s recipe without asking is like a <abbr title="n. 侵权">**tort**</abbr>,” she explained. “You cannot just take it.” She wanted to check their recipe cards at home, but without a <abbr title="n. 授权令">**warrant**</abbr>, no one could enter their houses to search.

To solve the problem, the judges set up an <abbr title="n. 淘汰">**elimination**</abbr> round. Each baker had to make one small cake on the spot, and the worst would be sent home. That would leave the true winner.

Tom sat on a bench to watch. A little <abbr title="n. 麻雀">**sparrow**</abbr> hopped onto the table and pecked at some sugar. Tom smiled at the <abbr title="n. 自发性">**spontaneity**</abbr> of the visit—the bird came without any warning or plan, just because it wanted to.

Meanwhile, Ben pulled out his worn recipe paper. He pointed to a <abbr title="n. 括号">**bracket**</abbr> he had drawn around the dry ingredients: flour, sugar, cocoa. But the paper was so old and folded that it felt stiff—it was completely <abbr title="adj. 无弹性的">**inelastic**</abbr>. When he tried to flatten it, the paper cracked. Anna saw this and said, “That’s a very <abbr title="adj. 传统的">**conventional**</abbr> way to write a recipe, but it doesn’t prove you created it.”

The baking began. Anna made a simple vanilla cake. Ben made a huge chocolate cake with thick cream and gold sprinkles. Everyone called it <abbr title="adj. 奢侈诱人的">**decadent**</abbr>—it was so rich and sweet that you could only eat one tiny slice. The judges loved it. Ben won, but he immediately cut a big piece and shared it with Anna. That kind act slowly mended the **schism**, and the friends smiled again.

As the sun set, Tom looked around the **rural** fairground. Even a cut finger, a sore throat, and a recipe fight could not ruin a day full of teamwork and good cake.

### 06

Anna and Ben were playing on the beach. The sun was warm, and the waves made soft sounds. Ben pointed to a dark hole in the cliff. “Look, a cave! Let’s go inside,” he said. Anna looked at the sign that said “No Entry”. She felt a little <abbr title="adj. 担心的">**apprehensive**</abbr>. “But Ben, we should not go in there. It’s against the rules.” Ben just smiled. “It’s just a cave. Nothing bad will happen.” He started to climb. The cave was at a high <abbr title="n. 高度">**elevation**</abbr>, and the rocks were slippery. Anna followed him slowly. They knew they were making an <abbr title="n. 闯入">**intrusion**</abbr> into a place they should not be. And they were also doing a <abbr title="n. 违反">**breach**</abbr> of the sign’s warning. But their curiosity was too big.

Inside the cave, the air was cool and wet. The walls sparkled because they had <abbr title="n. 硅石">**silica**</abbr> in the rock. Water dripped from the ceiling, and every <abbr title="n. 小滴">**droplet**</abbr> made a “plink” sound. Anna looked around and saw strange little plants on the ground. Each plant had a small, balloon-like <abbr title="n. 囊">**sac**</abbr> that <abbr title="v. 像">**resemble**d</abbr> tiny water balloons. When Anna touched one, the **sac** burst and water wet her hand. The cave also had a light, sweet smell. It was very <abbr title="adj. 芳香的">**fragrant**</abbr>, like flowers. Ben said, “This cave is so strange and beautiful. It seems to <abbr title="v. 代表">**epitomise**</abbr> the magic of nature.”

Then they heard a rustling noise. In a corner, a seagull was pecking at some old food. The bird looked sick. Its leg had a big lump, like a <abbr title="n. 肿瘤">**tumor**</abbr>. It was acting like a <abbr title="n. 食腐者">**scavenger**</abbr>, eating leftovers. Anna felt sad. “That poor bird does not belong in a dark cave. It looks so <abbr title="adj. 不协调的">**incongruous**</abbr> here.” Ben agreed. They saw a nest with three eggs. Suddenly, a boy’s head appeared at the cave entrance. It was their friend Jack. But Jack was not acting like a friend. He grabbed one egg and laughed. “I can sell this for money!” he shouted. He was a <abbr title="n. 叛徒">**traitor**</abbr> to the bird and to them.

Anna and Ben were shocked. They ran after Jack, but he was fast. They had to be <abbr title="adj. 顽强的">**dogged**</abbr> and not give up. They chased him down the cliff and onto the beach. Jack tripped and dropped the egg. Luckily, the egg only had a small crack, not a big **breach**. Jack ran away, and they carefully picked up the egg. Anna said, “We must be as **dogged** as detectives to save this egg.” They used some wet seaweed to <abbr title="v. 浸透">**impregnate**</abbr> the egg with moisture so it would not dry out. They did not want any <abbr title="n. 浪费">**wastage**</abbr> of water from the seaweed. Then they climbed back up the high **elevation** with great care.

When they returned the egg to the nest, the mother seagull came back. She looked at them and then gently sat on her eggs. Anna and Ben felt a deep sense of <abbr title="n. 满足">**fulfillment**</abbr>. Their day had a <abbr title="adj. 大量的">**substantial**</abbr> adventure and a happy ending. As they left the cave, Ben said, “You know, this cave is just a <abbr title="adj. 世俗的">**secular**</abbr> place, not magic. But it still teaches us to respect nature.” Anna smiled. She was glad they did the right thing. And the **fragrant** smell of the cave plants seemed to follow them all the way home.

That evening, when they got home, their mother noticed the **fragrant** smell on their clothes. “You two smell like flowers! Where did you go?” she asked. Anna and Ben just smiled. They did not tell her about the **intrusion** and the **breach** of the rule, but they knew they had done something good. Their **dogged** efforts had saved a life.

## 06月04日

### 01

Tom lived on a farm with his mom and dad. Every morning, he woke up to the steady **cadence** of his father’s boots on the wooden floor. The sound was like a clock: step, step, step. But this farm was not an easy place. His father ran it like a strict <abbr title="n. 管理体制">**regime**</abbr>. There were rules for everything, and Tom had to follow them all.

The land around the farm was **tillable**. That meant it was good for growing wheat and corn. Tom spent long days in the fields under the hot sun. When he came home, he often had to fix the <abbr title="n. 管道系统">**plumbing**</abbr> in the old house. The pipes made strange noises, but Tom knew how to calm them with a wrench and some tape.

One afternoon, dark clouds rolled over the farm. Suddenly, a storm of <abbr title="n. 冰雹">**hail**</abbr> began. Small balls of ice hit the roof like angry fists. Tom ran inside and saw that the **hail** had broken a window. His father just shook his head. “Go get the **cord** from the shed,” he said. “We must tie down the loose cover over the chicken coop.” Tom found the strong <abbr title="n. 绳子">**cord**</abbr> and rushed out. The cold **hail** still stung his skin, but he secured the cover.

Later, when the storm passed, Tom looked toward the hills. A group of <abbr title="n. 叉角羚">**pronghorn**</abbr> ran across the distance, their white tails flashing. His father pointed at them. “Speed is their best <abbr title="n. 特点">**trait**</abbr>,” he said. “No wolf can catch a healthy **pronghorn**.”

Tom dreamed of becoming an animal doctor, but his father would only <abbr title="v. 嘲笑">**mock**</abbr> him. “A vet? That is not a real <abbr title="n. 职业">**vocation**</abbr> for a farmer’s son,” he said with a cold laugh. Tom felt hurt but said nothing.

Not far from their fields stood an old, empty building. It was a <abbr title="adj. 废弃的">**derelict**</abbr> factory. Long ago, people made <abbr title="n. 石油">**petroleum**</abbr> products there. Then a big company took over, and after a <abbr title="n. 合并">**merger**</abbr>, they closed it down. Now it was used as a <abbr title="n. 垃圾填埋场">**landfill**</abbr>—a place to bury trash. Tom’s father often used the **derelict** factory as a <abbr title="n. 借口">**pretext**</abbr> for a lesson. “You see? If you don’t work hard, you end up like that—forgotten and useless.”

One night, a terrible cry woke Tom. He ran to the sheep pen and saw a bloody scene. It was a <abbr title="n. 大屠杀">**massacre**</abbr>. Several sheep lay dead. Tom’s hands shook. He knew a wolf had done this. The next day, he set a trap using a strong **cord** and some wooden poles. His father watched but said nothing. That evening, the wolf came again. Tom pulled the **cord**, and the net fell, trapping the animal without hurting it. The next morning, he and his father took the wolf far away and let it go free.

After that, Tom’s idea of protecting animals with kindness began to get <abbr title="n. 吸引力">**traction**</abbr>. Even his father started to listen. The old man had been worried about his own <abbr title="n. 死亡率">**mortality**</abbr> because the doctor had warned about his <abbr title="adj. 心血管的">**cardiovascular**</abbr> health. He began to see that Tom’s gentle way had value.

One day, Tom’s father took out an old watch. “Do you know its <abbr title="n. 出处">**provenance**</abbr>?” he asked. “It came from your great-grandfather. He was not a farmer. He was a healer of horses. Maybe that’s where your dream began.” Tom looked at the watch and smiled.

When Tom finally started his training as a vet, the whole village **hailed** him. His father was the loudest of all. He finally understood that Tom’s **vocation** was not a weakness but a gift. And the steady **cadence** of farm life went on, but now with a softer, kinder **regime**.

### 02

The Smith family planned a weekend trip to Blue Sea Town. Dad showed everyone the <abbr title="n. 行程表">**itinerary**</abbr>. “Look, on Saturday we visit the beach, and on Sunday we go shopping,” he said. Their dog Max, a bit <abbr title="adj. 胖乎乎的">**plump**</abbr> from too many treats, jumped into the car happily.

They drove to a small house near the water. It was a holiday <abbr title="n. 租赁">**rental**</abbr>. Dad had bought a new camera last month. He didn’t pay all at once; he used an <abbr title="n. 分期付款">**instalment**</abbr> plan. That means he pays a little each month.

After lunch, they walked to the beach. Tim saw a big wall of rocks in the sea. “That’s a <abbr title="n. 防波堤">**breakwater**</abbr>,” Dad said. “It stops big waves from hitting the boats.”

Near the sand, they found a small pool. Tiny creatures were swimming. “These are crab <abbr title="n. 幼虫">**larva**</abbr>,” Dad explained. “They will grow into crabs one day.”

Tim wanted to show Mum. He ran back to the house and took a small <abbr title="n. 茶碟">**saucer**</abbr> from the kitchen. He put the **larva** and some water in it.

Mum was looking through her old suitcase. She pulled out something white and tight. “What is that?” asked Lily. “It’s my old <abbr title="n. 紧身胸衣">**corset**</abbr>,” Mum laughed. “I wore it long ago to make my waist look thin.”

By the afternoon, Mum’s face had brown spots from the sun. “It’s <abbr title="n. 色素沉着">**pigmentation**</abbr>,” she said. “Too much sun can give you these spots.”

Tim and Lily built a sandcastle. It was tall and beautiful. But when a wave came, it made the castle <abbr title="v. 破碎">**crumble**</abbr>. The walls fell down quickly.

Dad took out his guitar. He played a song and taught Tim a <abbr title="n. 和弦">**chord**</abbr>. “Put your fingers here and here,” he said. Tim tried and it sounded nice.

The sun made the sand very hot. Dad pointed to the air above the sand. “See how the hot air goes up? That’s <abbr title="n. 对流">**convection**</abbr>.” The warm air moved up, and cooler air came down.

For tea, they opened a can of peaches. The can was made of <abbr title="n. 马口铁">**tinplate**</abbr>. It was shiny and didn’t break when it fell.

Now Tim had a problem. There was only one piece of cake left, but both he and Lily wanted it. He faced a <abbr title="n. 两难困境">**dilemma**</abbr>. “I want it, but Lily also wants it,” he thought. At last, he broke it into two halves.

The next day, they followed the **itinerary** and went to the shopping street. The town was full of <abbr title="n. 商业">**commerce**</abbr>. Shops sold shells, hats, and toys.

They entered a big shop. A smiling <abbr title="n. 收银员">**cashier**</abbr> said, “Welcome!” Mum bought a bag for Lily. It had a long, colorful <abbr title="n. 带子">**strap**</abbr>, so Lily could carry it on her shoulder.

On the way back, they saw a pretty garden with a thick green <abbr title="n. 树篱">**hedge**</abbr> around it. Suddenly, Lily’s new bag strap broke, and juice spilled on the path. She looked at Max. “Max did it!” she said. But Dad shook his head. “Don’t make the dog a <abbr title="n. 替罪羊">**scapegoat**</abbr>. I saw you drop it.” Lily turned red.

Then, she wasn’t looking and tripped. She hurt her ankle. Dad had to <abbr title="v. 固定">**immobilize**</abbr> it with a cloth strip until they got home.

At the end of the day, Tim said, “I love the sea. I love the **convection** that makes warm air, and even the **dilemma** about cake.” Everyone laughed.

### 03

Tom was getting ready for the school art show. He had a strong <abbr title="n. 信念">**conviction**</abbr> that his drawing would win first prize. His little sister, Lily, had lent him her best colored pencils. So Lily was his <abbr title="n. 债权人">**creditor**</abbr>. She said, "You owe me a big painting of a cat!" Tom agreed.

He opened his large <abbr title="n. 作品集">**portfolio**</abbr>. Inside were many drawings. He needed to <abbr title="v. 筛选">**sift**</abbr> through them to pick the best one. He looked at each picture carefully. Some were good, some were messy. Finally, he chose a picture of a dragon. Then he used scissors to <abbr title="v. 修剪">**trim**</abbr> the edges so it would fit the frame perfectly. He was <abbr title="adj. 节俭的">**sparing**</abbr> with the glue because the bottle was almost empty. But he still got some <abbr title="adj. 残留的">**residual**</abbr> glue on his fingers.

He put the drawing into the **portfolio**. The sky outside turned dark. The window became <abbr title="adj. 不透明的">**opaque**</abbr> with fog, so he could not see out. He decided to hurry. He loaded the **portfolio** onto his old red <abbr title="n. 四轮车">**wagon**</abbr>. The wagon was full of stuff and very <abbr title="adj. 笨重的">**unwieldy**</abbr>. It was hard to pull. As he pulled, the handle hurt his <abbr title="n. 手腕">**wrist**</abbr>. He rubbed the sore part between his hand and arm.

He wore his favorite <abbr title="n. 运动鞋">**sneaker**</abbr>s, but one shoelace was untied. He bent down and tied it. Then he walked with <abbr title="n. 轻快">**briskness**</abbr>, moving quickly and full of energy. He had to <abbr title="v. 经历">**undergo**</abbr> a very bumpy ride down the sidewalk. The wagon shook. He did not want to <abbr title="v. 屈服">**succumb**</abbr> to tiredness. He said to himself, "I will not give up!"

On the way, he saw a tiny <abbr title="n. 小斑点">**speck**</abbr> of black paint on his shoe. It was so small, like a dot. He passed a gray wall that looked <abbr title="adj. 单色的">**monochromatic**</abbr>. There was only one color—gray, no other shades. It felt boring.

Suddenly, the playground was a <abbr title="n. 喧闹">**riot**</abbr> of sound. Kids were shouting and running everywhere. The noise was loud and wild. A stray dog was chewing on an old stick. The dog began to <abbr title="v. 啃咬">**gnaw**</abbr> on the **wagon**’s wheel! Tom shouted, "Stop!" The dog ran away. Luckily, the wheel was okay. Tom didn’t want to <abbr title="v. 使遭受">**inflict**</abbr> any more damage on his sore **wrist**, so he pulled the **wagon** with his other hand.

Finally, he arrived at school. He felt proud. His **conviction** that he could win stayed strong. He showed his work to the teacher. She smiled. "You did a great job," she said. Tom was happy. He remembered he still owed Lily a cat picture because she was his **creditor**. He would use his **residual** energy to draw it later. The **briskness** of his walk returned as he went to find Lily. The **opaque** fog had cleared. The **monochromatic** wall now had a colorful poster on it. A **speck** of glitter stuck to his nose, making Lily laugh. The **unwieldy** **wagon** was now empty and easy to pull. He had survived the journey and his **wrist** felt better. At home, he had to **trim** the cat drawing again. He was **sparing** with the blue paint to avoid more **residual** mess. He didn't want to **inflict** any trouble on his tired dad. His **conviction** about being a good artist never changed.

### 04

Tom and Amy were excited to spend summer at Uncle Ben’s farm. It was not a normal farm. A big paper hung on the kitchen wall. The paper was an <abbr title="n. 认证">**accreditation**</abbr>. It said “Certified Organic.” That meant the farm did not use bad chemicals. Uncle Ben was proud of it.

Uncle Ben said, “I have an important job. I will <abbr title="v. 委托">**entrust**</abbr> you with it. You must put the <abbr title="n. 马鞍">**saddle**</abbr> on Brownie the horse.” Tom and Amy felt very proud. Uncle Ben showed them the leather seat that goes on a horse’s back. It was the saddle. They learned how to put it on Brownie.

They walked to the field. Some <abbr title="adj. 青少年的">**juvenile**</abbr> boys from the village were there. They were about 12 years old. The boys started to <abbr title="v. 嘲笑">**ridicule**</abbr> Tom and Amy. “City kids! You can’t even ride a horse!” they laughed. Amy did not have any <abbr title="n. 顾虑">**scruple**</abbr> about trying. She just jumped in to help. She felt no worry at all. She used <abbr title="n. 谨慎">**discretion**</abbr> when she got close to Brownie. She moved very slowly and spoke in a soft, calm voice. The horse stayed calm and let her touch it.

Later, they all went to the kitchen. A string of <abbr title="n. 大蒜">**garlic**</abbr> hung by the window. Uncle Ben also had a bottle of <abbr title="n. 橄榄">**olive**</abbr> oil. He made it from his own olive trees. The oil was green and smelled fresh. “Let’s <abbr title="v. 使脱水">**dehydrate**</abbr> some apple slices,” he said. They used a small machine to pull the water out of the fruit. After a few hours, the wet slices became dry, sweet snacks. It was like magic.

In the afternoon, Uncle Ben took them to a small pond. They looked at a drop of pond water under a microscope. Tiny creatures swam around very fast! Each one had a long, whip-like tail. “Those tails are called <abbr title="n. 鞭毛">**flagella**</abbr>,” Uncle Ben explained. The creatures used the flagella to push through the water, just like little boats with oars.

A <abbr title="adj. 相互的">**mutual**</abbr> friendship started to grow between the city kids and the village boys. They shared the dried apple snacks. They laughed together and forgot the earlier teasing. But suddenly, the <abbr title="n. 开始">**onset**</abbr> of a storm surprised everyone. Dark clouds rolled in fast. Lightning <abbr title="v. 闪耀">**flare**</abbr>d across the sky. The bright light made everyone jump. The grass was now wet and slippery. They had to <abbr title="v. 放慢">**slacken**</abbr> their speed so they would not fall. They walked slowly and carefully toward the house.

The <abbr title="n. 猛攻">**onslaught**</abbr> of wind and rain was very strong. It hit the farm all at once. The wind blew down some <abbr title="adj. 杂乱的">**haphazard**</abbr> piles of branches that were stacked in a messy way near the shed. The damage was <abbr title="adj. 明显的">**stark**</abbr>. A big branch lay across the shed roof. Everyone could see the hole clearly.

Uncle Ben looked up at the broken roof. “The repair will have a <abbr title="n. 附加费">**surcharge**</abbr>,” he said. “It will cost extra money because it is an emergency. But do not worry. Your help today was a great <abbr title="n. 促进">**facilitation**</abbr> – you made all the work much easier.” Tom and Amy smiled. They knew they had helped and learned so many new things on the farm.

## 06月05日

### 01

Tom arrived at Grandpa’s farm near the sea. The air was thick with <abbr title="n. 潮湿">**humidity**</abbr>, and his shirt stuck to his back. Grandpa waited under a <abbr title="adj. 宽边的">**broad-brimmed**</abbr> hat and smiled. He drove an old green <abbr title="n. 拖拉机">**tractor**</abbr>, and Tom jumped on for a bumpy ride to the barn.

Inside the barn, a goat blocked their way. It was a <abbr title="adj. 不听话的">**recalcitrant**</abbr> goat that never wanted to move. Grandpa called it the boss of the farm. Tom laughed and pushed the **recalcitrant** goat gently aside. Then he saw a huge wooden <abbr title="n. 大箱子">**trunk**</abbr> in the corner. It was covered in dust and spider webs.

Tom opened the **trunk** and found strange things: a mask, a tube, and heavy tanks. Grandpa said it was his old <abbr title="n. 水肺潜水">**scuba**</abbr> gear. His eyes lit up. “In my <abbr title="n. 全盛期">**heyday**</abbr>, I explored a sunken ship with this **scuba**,” he said. That was his golden time. But an <abbr title="n. 小病">**ailment**</abbr> in his knee now kept him from diving. Still, just talking about his **heyday** made him walk taller.

Grandpa took Tom to the beach. The sunset left a soft pink <abbr title="n. 淡色调">**tint**</abbr> on the clouds. Grandpa recited a short <abbr title="n. 韵文诗节">**verse**</abbr> about the ocean. Tom didn’t understand every word, but it sounded like waves. Then a huge bird with wide wings flew over them. “A <abbr title="n. 秃鹰">**condor**</abbr>,” Grandpa whispered. Tom watched the **condor** glide until it was a dot in the sky.

They wanted to go out on a boat. Grandpa needed to <abbr title="v. 征求">**solicit**</abbr> help from the harbor <abbr title="n. 工作人员">**personnel**</abbr>. A friendly woman in a blue jacket gave them a form. After a few calls, they finally <abbr title="v. 敲定">**clinched**</abbr> a plan for the next morning. Tom felt so excited he almost forgot the cold wind. Grandpa said the sea could be an <abbr title="n. 折磨；考验">**ordeal**</abbr>, but together they would be safe.

Back at the farmhouse, Tom helped Grandpa <abbr title="v. 拧干">**wring**</abbr> out wet towels. He twisted them hard until no water dripped. Then Grandma brought a pie from the oven. The smell was <abbr title="adj. 美味的">**delectable**</abbr>. Tom took a bite and felt warm inside. “We all <abbr title="v. 勉强维生">**subsist**</abbr> on simple things here,” Grandma said, “but we are happy.” Tom nodded. A little bread and a **delectable** pie were enough.

Later, Tom found a shiny <abbr title="n. 便士">**penny**</abbr> on the floor. He put it in a small box under his bed, a secret <abbr title="n. 藏匿物">**stash**</abbr> of treasures. He decided to use that **penny** to buy Grandma a new spoon. His **stash** grew with a pretty shell and a feather from the **condor**. He liked having a hiding place.

That night, a storm hit. Tom woke to the sound of thunder. He ran to help Grandpa cover the **tractor** with a big cloth. They had to **wring** the cloth after it fell in a puddle. The **humidity** before the rain made everything damp. The **ordeal** of the storm passed, and the morning was clear. Tom saw the bright **tint** of blue sky again.

They carried the **scuba** gear to the boat. Grandpa’s knee **ailment** did not stop him from smiling. The harbor **personnel** waved as they left. Tom remembered the **verse** about the sea and felt brave. The trip was not a great **ordeal** but a small adventure. And the **recalcitrant** goat? It ate a bit of Tom’s **delectable** pie that Grandma gave him for luck. Tom just laughed. Even with only a **penny** in his pocket, life felt rich.

### 02

Tom was a <abbr title="adj. 好学的">**studious**</abbr> boy. Almost every day, he would read something. One sunny morning, he sat at his desk reading an <abbr title="n. 摘录">**excerpt**</abbr> from a big book. The book was all about musical instruments from around the world. The excerpt focused on the <abbr title="n. 竖琴">**harp**</abbr>. It said, “The harp has many strings and makes a soft, beautiful sound.” Tom loved how the sound was described.

Just then, his mother called from the kitchen. “Tom, I need your help. Please go to Grandma’s old shed. That shed is part of our family <abbr title="n. 遗产">**heritage**</abbr>. Inside, you will find an old <abbr title="n. 螺丝刀">**screwdriver**</abbr>. Your dad is down by the river. He needs it to fix the wooden fence on the <abbr title="n. 堤岸">**embankment**</abbr>.”

Tom put on his shoes and walked outside. The sun felt warm on his arms. He followed the path that ran next to the big cornfields. He could see tall <abbr title="n. 玉米">**maize**</abbr> plants. Their green leaves were waving in the soft wind. The field looked so peaceful.

Then, Tom saw a yellow sign just ahead. It was a serious warning. The sign said: “Warning! By court <abbr title="n. 禁令">**injunct**</abbr>, no one may enter the field beyond this point. <abbr title="n. 疯草">**locoweed**</abbr> grows there. This plant can <abbr title="v. 刺激">**stimulate**</abbr> the brain and make cattle act very strange. Keep out!” Tom remembered his dad talking about locoweed. It was dangerous. He quickly walked past that area and stayed far away.

He soon reached the old shed. It smelled of wood and dust. On a shelf, he found the screwdriver. He picked it up and hurried to the river. Back at the **embankment**, his dad was waiting. He was glad to see the tool. “Good job, Tom. Let’s fix the fence now. The water will be safe again.” They worked together, putting the **screwdriver** to good use.

While they worked, Dad said, “School will <abbr title="v. 恢复">**resume**</abbr> next week. You can tell your teacher about how you helped today.” Tom smiled. He liked learning, and he was actually excited for the new <abbr title="n. 学期">**semester**</abbr>. He thought about his friends and the classroom.

Dad continued, “Did you hear the school’s new <abbr title="n. 宣言">**declaration**</abbr>? They said there will be no <abbr title="adj. 身体的">**corporal**</abbr> punishment anymore. Also, teachers must try to stay <abbr title="adj. 中立的">**neutral**</abbr> when kids argue. That sounds fair, right?” Tom nodded. But then he thought about a boy in his class named Jim. Jim was often very <abbr title="adj. 自命不凡的">**patronizing**</abbr>. He would say things like, “Oh, that’s so easy. You are too slow, let me do it for you.” This always made Tom feel small, even angry.

Tom made a decision. This semester, he would tell Jim <abbr title="adv. 直率地">**outright**</abbr>, “I don’t like it when you talk to me that way. Please stop.” He felt braver just thinking about it.

The next day, Tom practiced on his grandmother’s old **harp**. It was the very **harp** from the book’s excerpt! He played a simple tune. His sister filmed a short video with her phone. Later that evening, they posted it online. To everyone’s surprise, the video went <abbr title="adj. 病毒式传播的">**viral**</abbr>! Hundreds of people watched it, and many left nice comments.

When school finally started, Tom felt proud. In English class, the teacher taught the past <abbr title="n. 分词">**participle**</abbr>. She explained how some verbs change, like “eat” to “eaten.” Tom listened carefully. Then she gave a writing task. Tom started to write a long story about his adventure. But the teacher came by and said, “Tom, your story is very good, but we only have a short time. Could you please <abbr title="v. 缩短">**curtail**</abbr> it a little?” Tom nodded. He cut out a few sentences, and it became a perfect short story.

### 03

During winter break, Tom visited Grandpa’s farm by the sea. One morning, he looked out and saw white <abbr title="n. 霜">**frost**</abbr> on the grass. It sparkled in the sun. Grandpa was already feeding the animals. Tom ran to help. Grandpa smiled. “Good boy. This is <abbr title="n. 畜牧业">**husbandry**</abbr>. We care for sheep, cows, and chickens.” Tom learned how to pour grain into a big bucket.

After breakfast, Grandpa needed to start the tractor. It used <abbr title="n. 柴油">**diesel**</abbr>. Tom could smell the strong fuel. Grandpa said, “Always move slowly near the sheep to <abbr title="v. 避免">**avert**</abbr> scaring them.” Tom nodded and tiptoed past the sheep.

Then they walked down to the beach. Near the old lighthouse, only a pile of stones and broken bricks remained. “That’s <abbr title="n. 碎石">**rubble**</abbr>,” Grandpa said. “The storm knocked it down last year.” A friendly <abbr title="n. 服务员">**attendant**</abbr> from the farm shop brought them cold drinks. Tom’s sister Mary ran up with a small black box. “Look, Tom! I brought my <abbr title="n. 光度计">**photometer**</abbr>. It tells how bright the light is!” She pointed it at the sky and the number showed.

At lunch, Mom did not feel well. She had a terrible <abbr title="n. 偏头痛">**migraine**</abbr>. The pain was so bad that she had to lie down in a dark room. Everyone stayed very quiet. Dad whispered, “We believe in <abbr title="n. 非暴力">**nonviolence**</abbr>. That means no shouting or fighting, even when things go wrong.” Tom and Mary nodded and played a quiet game with paper and pencils.

In the kitchen, Tom saw many big bags. “What’s in those?” he asked. Grandma opened one. “There’s a <abbr title="n. 大量">**plethora**</abbr> of <abbr title="n. 麦芽">**malt**</abbr> in here! So much that we can bake bread for a whole year.” Tom tasted a little. It was sweet.

After lunch, Mary picked up her photo album. She wrote a funny <abbr title="n. 说明文字">**caption**</abbr> under a picture: “Tom with cookie crumbs on his nose.” They all laughed. Then Dad called them to the big map on the wall. “See, this blue shape is a <abbr title="n. 大陆">**continent**</abbr>—Asia. And this is Africa.” Tom traced the shapes with his finger.

Dad looked excited. “We have a <abbr title="adj. 未来的">**prospective**</abbr> plan. Next year, we might move here to <abbr title="v. 居住">**dwell**</abbr> near the sea forever.” Tom’s heart beat fast. He imagined living in a little house near the water. That evening, Mary sat on the floor and began to <abbr title="v. 摆弄">**fiddle**</abbr> with the old radio. She turned the buttons this way and that until music played.

The next day, they went to the shore. Far out in the water stood big white windmills. “Those are <abbr title="adj. 离岸的">**offshore**</abbr> windmills,” Grandpa explained. “They make power from the wind.” The blades turned slowly. Suddenly, a small fishing boat stopped moving. Seaweed had wrapped around its engine and <abbr title="v. 使无力">**incapacitated**</abbr> it. The boat could not go anywhere. A man on the boat threw a rope to the dock. But the rope was old and started to <abbr title="v. 磨损">**fray**</abbr>—tiny threads came loose. Tom and Grandpa hurried to help. They caught the rope and pulled slowly. The boat came safely to shore. After the rescue, Tom felt a burst of <abbr title="n. 兴奋">**exhilaration**</abbr>. He hugged Grandpa and cheered. It was the best winter break ever.

### 04

The <abbr title="n. 魅力">**glamour**</abbr> of the old castle was hard to see at first. It was buried under ice for hundreds of years. But now the **glacial** ice was melting, and Lucy’s team could finally work. Lucy wanted to **unearth** its secrets. She arrived on a small **jet** plane with her team. The plane landed on a frozen lake. The cold wind was **pungent** with a strange, sharp smell, like old fire and metal.

The team set up camp near the castle’s outer wall. A deep, wide <abbr title="n. 护城河">**moat**</abbr> surrounded the castle. The water in the **moat** was half frozen. They used a wooden bridge to cross it. Lucy’s helper, Ben, had to <abbr title="v. 驯养">**domesticate**</abbr> a wild dog that lived nearby. At first the dog was afraid, but Ben gave it food every day. Soon the dog was **domesticate**d and even helped them find things.

Inside the castle, Lucy found a strange old machine. It was a <abbr title="n. 地震仪">**seismograph**</abbr> made of stone and metal. The **seismograph** could still record shakes in the ground. Ben lit a fire with <abbr title="n. 木炭">**charcoal**</abbr> to keep them warm. The **charcoal** burned bright and hot. While they worked, they found old writings. The words were full of <abbr title="adj. 语义的">**semantic**</abbr> puzzles. The **semantic** meaning of some signs was still a mystery.

Lucy sent a <abbr title="adj. 季度的">**quarterly**</abbr> report to the <abbr title="n. 议会">**parliament**</abbr> back home. The **quarterly** report had to be on time, or she would get into trouble. The **parliament** gave money for her work. But last month, Lucy made a big mistake. She was on <abbr title="n. 试用期">**probation**</abbr> because she lost a key map. The **probation** meant she could lose her job if she failed again. The team leader, Mr. Stone, gave her a strong <abbr title="v. 严厉批评">**censure**</abbr> for that. The **censure** made Lucy very sad, but she did not give up.

One day, a tall man came to the camp. His great <abbr title="n. 身高">**stature**</abbr> made everyone look small. His **stature** was almost two meters. He said his name was Dr. Hill. He was sent to <abbr title="v. 遣返回国">**repatriate**</abbr> Ben. Ben’s visa had ended. So Ben had to leave. Lucy felt bad. Before Ben left, he pointed to a blocked door. “You must find what is behind it,” he said. The thought made Lucy feel <abbr title="v. 使兴奋">**exhilarate**</abbr>d. A new discovery! That feeling of being **exhilarate**d kept her awake all night.

The next morning, Lucy found an old mark on the door. It warned: “Open not, or you will <abbr title="v. 丧失">**forfeit**</abbr> your greatest love.” Lucy thought hard. What would she **forfeit**? Maybe it meant her work. But she had to know. She placed a small charge. “Stand back!” she yelled. Then she pushed the button to <abbr title="v. 引爆">**detonate**</abbr> the door. The charge **detonate**d with a loud noise. Stones flew <abbr title="adv. 向前">**forth**</abbr> from the wall. Dust flew **forth**, and a powerful **pungent** smell of old spices rushed out. When Lucy stepped through, she saw a room full of gold and ancient books. The **glamour** of the treasure was now clear and bright. She had not **forfeit** anything—she found her greatest success.

## 06月06日

### 01

The Summer Camp Discovery

Last summer, Tom and Lily went to a mountain camp. The place was beautiful. A fast <abbr title="n. 小溪">**creek**</abbr> ran between big trees. The rocks were mostly white <abbr title="n. 石灰岩">**limestone**</abbr>, which sparkled in the sun. It looked like a painting – so <abbr title="adj. 田园诗般的">**idyllic**</abbr>! But the paths were <abbr title="adj. 粗糙的">**crude**</abbr> and full of stones, so we walked carefully.

Dr. Hill, our <abbr title="n. 内科医生">**internist**</abbr>, told us not to drink from the **creek**. “The water may carry <abbr title="adj. 水传播的">**water-borne**</abbr> illnesses,” he said. So we only drank bottled water.

On the first day, Mr. Green, our history teacher, said, “We will visit a <abbr title="adj. 中世纪的">**medieval**</abbr> village. Long ago, <abbr title="n. 犹太人">**Jew**</abbr> traders lived here. They used a special <abbr title="n. 货币">**currency**</abbr> made of sea shells.” We thought that was interesting. Lily asked, “Is that <abbr title="adj. 真实的">**authentic**</abbr>?” Mr. Green nodded. “Yes, we found many shells in the ground. It proves they really used them as money.”

We walked to the old village. There, we saw old walls made of **limestone**. The stones looked very old. Mr. Green said, “Finding these things has great <abbr title="n. 价值">**merit**</abbr>. It helps us understand the past. It also shows <abbr title="adj. 人文主义的">**humanistic**</abbr> values – how people lived and helped each other.” Tom yawned. He didn’t care about history. He liked to build things.

Then Mr. Green gave us a job. “Please <abbr title="v. 统计">**tally**</abbr> all the small pieces you find. Write down the numbers.” So we started counting. Tom found a strange thing. It was a dark, soft material. When he pressed it, it bounced back. “It feels <abbr title="adj. 有弹性的">**rubbery**</abbr>!” he said. He pushed it with his hand. He used <abbr title="n. 压缩">**compression**</abbr> – he squeezed it hard – and when he let go, it returned to its shape. He had an <abbr title="adj. 巧妙的">**ingenious**</abbr> idea. He made a simple clamp by placing the **rubbery** material between two flat **limestone** pieces. When he pressed the stones together, they held a piece of paper. “Look, I made a clip using compression!” Everyone thought it was very clever. Mr. Green said, “With ideas like that, you could get into a <abbr title="adj. 有声望的">**prestigious**</abbr> university someday.”

Later, we climbed a small hill. Suddenly, we saw a hawk flying low. It was hunting. Its <abbr title="n. 猎物">**prey**</abbr> was a little mouse. The hawk dived fast and caught it. We watched in silence.

Then we heard a loud noise from the far mountain. Rumble! “It’s a small <abbr title="n. 雪崩">**avalanche**</abbr>,” Dr. Hill said calmly. “Don’t worry, we are safe here.” We saw snow sliding down a distant peak. It was exciting but a bit scary.

Back at camp, we sat around the fire. Tom talked about his dream to be an inventor. Another boy, Jack, said, “I want to be a <abbr title="n. 裁判">**referee**</abbr> for soccer games. I like being fair.” Dr. Hill smiled, “It’s good to have dreams. Just remember to stay healthy.” As an **internist**, he always cared about our health.

That night, Lily wrote in her diary. She wrote about the **medieval** village, the **Jew** traders, and the **authentic** shell **currency**. She thought history had **merit** and taught **humanistic** lessons. She also wrote about the **creek**, the **limestone**, and the **idyllic** view. And she didn’t forget the **rubbery** material and the **ingenious** clamp. The **tally** work was fun. She even drew a picture of the hawk and its **prey**.

The camp was a great adventure. We learned about nature and history, and we all had dreams for the future.

### 02

Tom ran through the forest, and soon he began to <abbr title="v. 喘气">**pant**</abbr>. His chest moved up and down fast because he was tired. He stopped by a big rock and heard the sound of water. He walked closer and found a small waterfall. The water fell in a beautiful <abbr title="n. 小瀑布">**cascade**</abbr> over the rocks. It looked like a white, shiny curtain under the sun.

Under the cascade, there was a very small man, much shorter than Tom. He was a <abbr title="n. 矮人">**dwarf**</abbr>. The dwarf was eating some purple fruits from a tree. They were small and juicy. “Try one,” he said with a smile. “These <abbr title="n. 桑葚">**mulberries**</abbr> are very sweet.” Tom tasted one, and it was like candy from nature.

The dwarf then took Tom to his home near the trees. A rope hung from a big branch, with a wooden seat. “This is my <abbr title="n. 秋千">**swing**</abbr>,” said the dwarf. Tom sat on it and moved back and forth under the green leaves. After that, they went inside the little house. In the corner, there was a small instrument like a piano. The dwarf called it a <abbr title="n. 小型钢琴">**spinet**</abbr> and played a soft little song. Tom noticed the dwarf had long, dark hair on his upper lip — a fine <abbr title="n. 胡子">**moustache**</abbr> that curled up at the ends.

The dwarf put on a special hat and jacket. “Long ago, I was an <abbr title="n. 飞行员">**aviator**</abbr>,” he said. “I flew my own plane. It was a <abbr title="adj. 混合的">**hybrid**</abbr> — it could use sun power and oil together. One day, I had to fly through a big, dark storm. I was scared, but I had to <abbr title="v. 面对">**confront**</abbr> it. I used a clever <abbr title="n. 策略">**tactic**</abbr>: I flew around the worst clouds, not into them. But the air was so thin that my body had a <abbr title="adj. 生理的">**physiological**</abbr> reaction. I began to **pant** hard, and my hands became sweaty. The ropes in my hand were old and rough — they felt <abbr title="adj. 粗糙的">**abrasive**</abbr> against my skin.”

That flying <abbr title="n. 一段时间">**stint**</abbr> lasted ten years, and he quickly gained <abbr title="n. 名望">**prominence**</abbr> as a brave sky explorer. People everywhere loved him, and he became a <abbr title="adj. 平民英雄的">**populist**</abbr> hero — even simple farmers and children knew his name.

Now the dwarf had a quiet life. He showed Tom a strange plant in his garden. “This plant has a special <abbr title="adj. 繁殖的">**reproductive**</abbr> way,” he said. “A single leaf can grow into a whole new plant. But touch it softly — the leaf feels **abrasive** , like sandpaper.” He made tea from the leaves and gave it to Tom. “Drink this. It will <abbr title="adv. 极大地">**substantially**</abbr> increase your imagination.”

Tom drank the warm tea, and at once he felt a bright, new <abbr title="n. 灵感">**inspiration**</abbr>. The **prospect** of flying like the dwarf someday filled his heart with joy. He looked at the **aviator** with big eyes and said, “Maybe I can be like you!”

Before Tom went home, he swung on the **swing** one more time, ate a handful of **mulberries**, and watched the sun shine through the **cascade**. He would never forget the little **dwarf** with the **moustache** and the quiet music from the **spinet**.

### 03

Tom and Lily were excited. They watched a <abbr title="n. 重播">**rebroadcast**</abbr> of the Food Festival on TV. Last year they missed it, so they watched the **rebroadcast** this morning. In the show, a famous food <abbr title="n. 大亨">**tycoon**</abbr> was the judge. Mr. Rich, the **tycoon**, owned many restaurants. He was very <abbr title="adj. 独裁的">**autocratic**</abbr>. He never listened to others. He just yelled, “No, this dish is bad!” No one could change his mind. His **autocratic** ways made the cooks sad.

The show had food from many countries. The **tycoon** said, “Good <abbr title="n. 烹饪">**cuisine**</abbr> needs love.” The **cuisine** looked delicious. One cook made garlic <abbr title="n. 虾">**shrimp**</abbr>. The big **shrimp** smelled wonderful. Another cook used a special <abbr title="n. 调味品">**seasoning**</abbr> — a mix of salt and herbs. He said, “This **seasoning** is my secret.” On the table, there was an <abbr title="n. 什锦">**assortment**</abbr> of dishes. The **assortment** included soup, meat, and bread. It was a true <abbr title="n. 盛宴">**feast**</abbr> for the eyes. Tom’s mouth watered. Lily said, “I want to go to a **feast** like that.”

Then a funny cook brought fried <abbr title="n. 蚱蜢">**grasshopper**</abbr>. The **tycoon** made a face. “Yuck! Bugs!” he shouted. He did not even try the **grasshopper**. Lily whispered, “That’s not fair.
