He should not <abbr title="v. 歧视">**discriminate**</abbr> against strange food.” Tom agreed. “You should have an <abbr title="n. 喜好">**affinity**</abbr> for new things,” he said. A real food lover has an **affinity** for all tastes. The cook looked sad because of the **discriminate** behavior.

Tom’s dad came in. “Time to go,” he said. “Today’s outdoor Food Festival starts at <abbr title="n. 正午">**meridian**</abbr>.” So at exactly **meridian**, they arrived at the park. It was winter and snow covered the ground. The festival had <abbr title="adj. 繁茂的">**luxuriant**</abbr> decorations — colorful flags and green pine branches. The **luxuriant** display made everyone smile.

Near a food tent, Tom saw a big fish on ice. It was a <abbr title="adj. 有鳞的">**scaled**</abbr> fish. Its shiny **scaled** skin sparkled. The seller said, “You must remove the **scaled** skin before you cook it.” Tom nodded. Lily pulled him to a live animal corner. There was a small <abbr title="n. 豪猪">**porcupine**</abbr>! The **porcupine** had sharp spikes. It curled into a ball. “Don’t touch the **porcupine**,” warned a keeper.

Tom wanted to try fried **grasshopper** at a stall. He walked to the seller but felt nervous. He tried to ask, but he started to <abbr title="v. 结巴">**stammer**</abbr>. “I-I-I want some g-grasshopper,” he **stammer**ed. The seller smiled kindly. Tom bought the **grasshopper**. It was crunchy. He bit into it and loved the taste <abbr title="adv. 其">**thereof**</abbr> — the taste **thereof** was nutty.

Suddenly, Tom stepped back and his foot landed in a cold <abbr title="n. 水坑">**puddle**</abbr>. The **puddle** was from melting snow. He slipped and fell! People looked. Tom felt so <abbr title="adj. 尴尬的">**embarrass**</abbr>. His face turned red. Lily helped him up. “Don’t be **embarrass**ed,” she said. “Everyone falls.”

After eating, they saw a big hill. Kids were sliding down on a <abbr title="n. 雪橇">**sledge**</abbr>. Tom and Lily borrowed a wooden **sledge**. They climbed up and zoomed down the snowy hill on the **sledge**. So much fun!

Later, the **tycoon** from TV arrived at the festival. He was not so **autocratic** today. He tried a fried **grasshopper** and smiled. “I should not **discriminate**,” he said. “My **affinity** for bug food is growing.” Tom heard this and felt happy. The **rebroadcast** of this new festival would be great.

The family enjoyed the **feast** of **cuisine**, from **shrimp** to spiced meat. The **seasoning** was just right. The **assortment** of desserts was amazing. As the sun passed **meridian**, they went home, tired but happy. Tom even wrote a story about the **porcupine**, the **scaled** fish, and the **puddle**. He did not **stammer** one bit when he read it aloud.

### 04

Every summer, our small town by the river held a very old <abbr title="n. 仪式">**rite**</abbr>. This rite was to <abbr title="v. 纪念">**commemorate**</abbr> the day our town was born, long ago. All our <abbr title="n. 亲属">**kin**</abbr>—aunts, uncles, cousins—would come back home. They would gather under the big <abbr title="n. 橡树">**oak**</abbr> tree in the town square. The oak tree was hundreds of years old and gave cool shade.

My Uncle Jack ran the <abbr title="n. 渡船">**ferry**</abbr>. The ferry carried people and carts from one side of the river to the other. Uncle Jack was a good man, but he was a bit <abbr title="adj. 自夸的">**boastful**</abbr>. "No one can run a ferry like me!" he often said. He also liked to <abbr title="v. 说教">**preach**</abbr> to anyone who would listen. "Keep the river clean! It gives us life," he would say. I worked with him as an <abbr title="n. 助手">**adjunct**</abbr>, helping to tie the boat and take tickets.

This year, however, something was very wrong. The <abbr title="n. 小麦">**wheat**</abbr> fields outside town looked sick. The long green leaves had turned yellow and <abbr title="adj. 枯萎的">**blighted**</abbr>. Farmers were upset. Also, water from the river started to become <abbr title="adj. 稀缺的">**scarce**</abbr>. There was less and less clean water for drinking and cooking. People grew worried. Even the river looked dull.

One morning, I was on the **ferry** with Uncle Jack when I saw a truck stop near the river bank. A big wooden <abbr title="n. 板条箱">**crate**</abbr> fell from the back of the truck. The crate broke open, and a dark, thick liquid started to leak into the water. I knew that this liquid could <abbr title="v. 污染">**contaminate**</abbr> the river and kill the fish. "Uncle, look!" I shouted. But Uncle Jack just <abbr title="v. 没精打采地坐">**slouched**</abbr> in his chair and waved his hand. "It is probably nothing. I will fix it later," he said. He did not move.

The next day, the problem grew worse. Strange weeds began to <abbr title="v. 激增">**proliferate**</abbr> along the river bank. They spread fast, covering the mud and rocks. Dead fish floated on the water. The smell was bad. I felt scared. A famous musician had just come to town. He brought a beautiful <abbr title="n. 羽管键琴">**harpsichord**</abbr> with him. He was invited to play a <abbr title="n. 协奏曲">**concerto**</abbr> at the rite to **commemorate** our town. But if the river was dirty and water was **scarce**, the visitors would have no clean water. The festival could be ruined.

I decided I had to do something myself. I wanted to <abbr title="v. 使认识">**acquaint**</abbr> myself with the source of the pollution. So I took a small boat and rowed up the river. I followed the bad smell. Soon, I found a large pipe near an old factory. The pipe was pouring dirty <abbr title="n. 污水">**sewage**</abbr> directly into the river! Black water and bits of trash filled the place. The sight would <abbr title="v. 诱惑">**tempt**</abbr> anyone to turn away and go home, but I stayed. I saw another broken **crate** there, just like before.

I went back and told my uncle everything. This time, Uncle Jack got up. "You are right," he said, with no boast. He called the town leaders. They blocked the pipe and cleaned the river. They took out the **crates**. After many days, the water turned clear. The ugly weeds stopped growing. The **wheat** in the fields began to heal. It was green once more, not **blighted**. And water was no longer **scarce**.

On the day of the **rite**, we all met under the **oak** tree. The musician sat at his **harpsichord** and played a sweet **concerto**. It was a fine way to **commemorate** our town and river. Uncle Jack listened. He still liked to **preach**, but now he also knew the value of action. And I learned that even a **boastful** person can change and help.

## 06月07日

### 01

Lily had a special <abbr title="n. 天赋">**flair**</abbr> for finding interesting things. One sunny day, she and Sam were in Grandpa’s barn. The <abbr title="n. 背景">**backdrop**</abbr> of the barn was a golden field and blue sky. Lily’s **flair** led her to a dusty old box in a corner. “Let’s open it!” she said.

Inside, there was a lovely <abbr title="n. 石印画">**lithograph**</abbr>. The **lithograph** showed a colorful, swirling <abbr title="n. 星云">**nebula**</abbr>. “Wow, a **nebula** is where stars are born,” said Sam. He put the **lithograph** carefully aside.

Next, they pulled out a heavy <abbr title="n. 法规书">**statute**</abbr> book. The **statute** book was full of old laws. Then they found a thick <abbr title="n. 目录">**catalogue**</abbr>. The **catalogue** had pictures of tools and toys. There was also a glass <abbr title="n. 水银">**mercury**</abbr> thermometer. “Be careful, that has **mercury** inside,” Lily said. A small metal <abbr title="n. 喷瓶">**spray**</abbr> bottle was at the bottom. Lily used the **spray** to mist a little plant. The plant had a bright green <abbr title="n. 茎">**stem**</abbr>. She touched the **stem** gently.

“Let’s go to the <abbr title="n. 小溪">**brook**</abbr>!” Sam cried. They ran to the **brook** behind the field. Near the water stood a tall <abbr title="n. 棉白杨">**cottonwood**</abbr> tree. The **cottonwood** released white fluffy seeds into the air. Under the tree, a broken water <abbr title="n. 出水口">**spout**</abbr> dripped into a puddle. A frog sat by the **spout**. Lily pointed, “Look, a three-<abbr title="adj. 有趾的">**toed**</abbr> frog!” The three-**toed** frog jumped away.

Sam wanted to measure the water temperature with the **mercury** thermometer. But as he pulled it out, it slipped from his hand and broke on a rock. The shiny **mercury** scattered into little balls. Sam’s heart pounded. He knew he had to make a <abbr title="n. 坦白">**confession**</abbr> to Grandpa. He ran back to the house. “Grandpa, I need to make a **confession**,” he said. “I broke the **mercury** thermometer at the **brook**.” Grandpa listened and said, “Thank you for your honest **confession**. But you must always <abbr title="v. 优先考虑">**prioritize**</abbr> safety. Before you play with old things, **prioritize** thinking about what could go wrong. And did you hurt your back? Your <abbr title="adj. 脊椎的">**spinal**</abbr> column is important.” Sam checked and said, “My **spinal** area doesn’t hurt. I’m okay.”

Later, Sam’s older brother Tom was working in his room. Tom had to finish his university <abbr title="n. 学位论文">**dissertation**</abbr>. The **dissertation** was about how plants <abbr title="v. 吸收">**assimilate**</abbr> minerals from water. “A plant’s **stem** helps **assimilate** nutrients,” Tom explained. Sam thought that was cool. Tom also said, “If you break a **statute** from that old book, you might have no <abbr title="n. 求助途径">**recourse**</abbr> but to pay a fine. No **recourse** means you can’t get legal help.”

The next morning, Sam and Lily looked at the **catalogue** again. They wanted to find a new toy. But Grandpa said, “After our last move, there was a big <abbr title="n. 移位">**displacement**</abbr> of all the boxes. The **displacement** means things are not in their usual spots.” Lily’s doll was lost because of the **displacement**. They searched and finally found it under a pile of books. Sam smiled. He learned that an honest **confession** and learning to **prioritize** carefully could fix many problems. And he would never forget the beautiful **lithograph** of the **nebula** and the three-**toed** frog by the **brook**.

### 02

One sunny morning, a cheerful <abbr title="n. 女孩">**lass**</abbr> named Lily and her brother Tom went to visit their grandfather. Grandpa lived in a small house by the river. He was a very <abbr title="adj. 和蔼的">**genial**</abbr> man. His warm smile always made them feel at home. He was also a <abbr title="n. 鉴赏家">**connoisseur**</abbr> of old books and strange machines. Grandpa loved both art and science.

He took them to his workshop. On the table was a machine with a long <abbr title="n. 绳子">**cord**</abbr>. The **cord** linked a metal box to a big paper drum. “This is a <abbr title="n. 波动记录仪">**kymograph**</abbr>,” said Grandpa. “It can draw waves from the <abbr title="n. 电离层">**ionosphere**</abbr>.” Lily looked at the wavy line on the paper. “Last night, the signal from the **ionosphere** became <abbr title="adv. 千倍">**thousandfold**</abbr> stronger,” Grandpa said. “I need to <abbr title="v. 评估">**appraise**</abbr> this data to find the <abbr title="adj. 因果的">**causal**</abbr> reason.”

Then Grandpa pointed to a painting on the wall. It showed a hill and a river, but the shapes were all cubes and sharp angles. “This is my <abbr title="n. 立体主义">**cubism**</abbr> work,” he said. “It is a <abbr title="n. 描绘">**rendering**</abbr> of the <abbr title="n. 地形">**terrain**</abbr> near the river <abbr title="n. 连接点">**junction**</abbr>.” The most <abbr title="adj. 突出的">**prominent**</abbr> shape in the **rendering** was an old stone tower. The **connoisseur** Grandpa had even marked the <abbr title="n. 圆周">**circumference**</abbr> of that tower. He pulled out a heavy old book, a <abbr title="n. 巨著">**tome**</abbr> about local history. “This **tome** tells about a <abbr title="adj. 腐败的">**corrupt**</abbr> businessman long ago. He used a <abbr title="n. 驳船">**barge**</abbr> to hide stolen gold in the mud at the very **junction**,” Grandpa said. “The gold was never found.”

Grandpa smiled. “I will <abbr title="v. 策划">**mastermind**</abbr> a small dig. We can <abbr title="v. 挖掘">**dredge**</abbr> the mud by the old **barge** and see if the treasure is still there.” Lily and Tom jumped with joy.

Early next morning, they walked to the **junction**. The **terrain** was bumpy and wet, but Grandpa’s **genial** jokes kept them going. They saw the rotten wood of an old **barge** half sunk in the water. “Now we must **dredge** with care,” Grandpa said. They used shovels and their hands. The mud was deep. After a long while, Lily felt something hard. Tom tied a **cord** around a heavy iron box, and together they pulled it out. The box was **corrupt** with rust. Inside, they found gold coins and a paper. The paper showed the **corrupt** man’s secret plan.

“I **appraised** the story from the **tome**, and I was right!” Grandpa said. He called the police. The news quickly spread. The **connoisseur** Grandpa was proud. Lily thought about how a **causal** clue—the **thousandfold** signal from the **ionosphere**—had started their adventure. Maybe the signal was just a funny coincidence, but it felt like fate. Back home, Grandpa painted a new **cubism** picture of their day. In the **rendering**, the **kymograph**, the little **lass**, and the old **barge** were all mixed in bright squares. Lily looked at the **circumference** of the painted sun and smiled. Grandpa had **masterminded** the best summer hunt ever.

### 03

Peter was a thin boy of thirteen. He worked as an apprentice for Mr. Grim, the village blacksmith. Mr. Grim knew much about <abbr title='n. 冶金学'>**metallurgy**</abbr>, the art of working with metals. But his <abbr title='n. 偏狭'>**insularity**</abbr> was strong. He did not like new ideas or people from other places. He often <abbr title='v. 虐待'>**maltreat**</abbr>ed Peter, giving him too much work and very little food. Peter’s <abbr title='n. 津贴'>**stipend**</abbr> was just a few pennies a week. It was hardly enough to buy bread.

After a long day, Peter liked to read a <abbr title='n. 平装书'>**paperback**</abbr> book called “The Dream-Seller.” The title had a little <abbr title='n. 连字符'>**hyphen**</abbr> in it. He read at night because he was <abbr title='adj. 夜间的'>**nocturnal**</abbr>—he felt awake when the moon was up. Because of this, the village children gave him a <abbr title='n. 绰号'>**sobriquet**</abbr>: “Night Owl.” Peter did not mind.

In the village, people were worried. The price of bread and milk was going up fast. Old Farmer Joe said it was <abbr title='n. 通货膨胀'>**inflation**</abbr>. Someone was making fake coins. Peter had a kind friend, old Martha. She was a <abbr title='adj. 慈祥的'>**benign**</abbr> lady with a gentle smile. She was very <abbr title='adj. 见多识广的'>**cosmopolitan**</abbr> because she had traveled to big cities when she was young. She loved to <abbr title='v. 刺绣'>**embroider**</abbr> beautiful cloth with flowers and spiders. Her spiderwebs looked like <abbr title='adj. 网状的'>**reticular**</abbr> nets of silver thread.

Mr. Grim was afraid people would find out his secret. He tried to <abbr title='v. 预先阻止'>**forestall**</abbr> any trouble. He started a <abbr title='n. 诉讼'>**lawsuit**</abbr> against Martha. He said she was a thief and tried to <abbr title='v. 诽谤'>**smear**</abbr> her good name. “She hides fake coins in her embroidery!” he shouted. Martha was very sad.

Peter knew the truth. One night, he saw Mr. Grim making fake coins in the dark forge. He also heard Grim talking in a <abbr title='adj. 好战的'>**militant**</abbr> voice about driving Martha out of the village. Peter ran to the town judge. He told everything. The judge sent soldiers. They caught Mr. Grim. Because his crime was very serious, he was <abbr title='v. 绞死'>**hanged**</abbr> in the town square. It was a sad but just end.

After that, the king made a new law, an <abbr title='n. 修正案'>**amendment**</abbr> to protect young workers. No one could **maltreat** an apprentice again. Peter felt free. His love for learning began to <abbr title='v. 萌芽'>**burgeon**</abbr>. He started to read more books and even opened a small bookshop one day.

### 04

The morning sun was warm, and a soft <abbr title="n. 微风">**breeze**</abbr> moved the leaves. At the nature camp, Mr. Brown used a shiny <abbr title="n. 咖啡壶">**percolator**</abbr> to make coffee. The smell woke everyone up. He held a paper in his hand and smiled. “This is a <abbr title="n. 入围名单">**shortlist**</abbr> of our best bird helpers,” he said. “We need a leader today. The camp has a simple <abbr title="n. 等级">**hierarchy**</abbr> — the leader decides where we go, and the rest follow.”

Lily and her friend Tom looked at the map. “There is a <abbr title="n. 捷径">**shortcut**</abbr> through the woods,” Tom said. “It can save half an hour.” Lily felt a little <abbr title="adj. 犹豫的">**hesitant**</abbr>. “Is it safe? We might get lost.” But the <abbr title="n. 多数">**preponderance**</abbr> of the kids wanted to try it — ten kids said yes, and only two said no. So they took the shortcut.

They walked past a small old house with a <abbr title="n. 茅草">**thatch**</abbr> roof. Inside, there were papers and pens — some <abbr title="n. 文具">**stationery**</abbr> for nature notes. Suddenly, Lily stopped. She saw two men behind a big tree. They talked in low voices. She heard one say, “We take the eggs tonight. No one will know.” The other nodded. Lily understood — it was <abbr title="n. 共谋">**complicity**</abbr>. They planned a crime together.

The men looked <abbr title="adj. 不道德的">**unscrupulous**</abbr> — they did not care about rules or hurting animals. They spread a hidden net on the ground and covered it with leaves. It was an <abbr title="adj. 隐伏的">**insidious**</abbr> trap; a bird could walk into it and get caught slowly, never seeing the danger. Lily was a <abbr title="adj. 坦率的">**candid**</abbr> girl. She did not hide the truth. She ran to Mr. Brown and told him everything.

“We must check the <abbr title="n. 周边">**perimeter**</abbr> of our camp,” Mr. Brown said. They walked around the whole edge of the open area. Near a tall tree, they found a beautiful <abbr title="n. 犀鸟">**hornbill**</abbr> with a big curved beak. But it was hurt! Mr. Brown called the animal <abbr title="n. 诊所">**clinic**</abbr>. A van came quickly to help the bird.

Tom had a <abbr title="adj. 复杂的">**sophisticated**</abbr> camera — it had many buttons and could zoom very far. He took photos of the men and the trap as proof. While they waited, the children sat down to <abbr title="v. 消耗">**consume**</abbr> their lunch. Lily ate an apple, but her mind was full of <abbr title="n. 全神贯注">**preoccupation**</abbr> — she could only think about the **hornbill** and whether it would be okay. She did not even hear Tom call her name.

Soon the police arrived and caught the **unscrupulous** men. The **hornbill** got better at the **clinic**, and the rangers said it could fly back home. When it was time to leave, Lily felt a strong <abbr title="n. 倾向">**inclination**</abbr> to protect birds forever. She took one of the **stationery** pens and wrote down everything she had learned. The late afternoon **breeze** felt cooler, and everyone smiled. The **shortlist** of helpers became the “Hornbill Heroes,” and Lily’s name was on top.

# 第2周

## 06月08日

### 01

Tim was happy because he won a <abbr title="n. 证书">**certificate**</abbr> at the school science fair. His project showed how <abbr title="adj. 易延展的">**ductile**</abbr> copper wire can be pulled into a long string. He also used a tool to measure <abbr title="n. 扭矩">**torque**</abbr>, which is the force that turns things. On another board, he drew the stomach and wrote about <abbr title="n. 分泌素">**secretin**</abbr>, a special helper that makes your body ready to digest food when you smell something yummy. He even had a <abbr title="adj. 盆栽的">**potted**</abbr> plant to show how roots grow in circles. Everyone said it was a great <abbr title="n. 壮举">**feat**</abbr> for a young student.

The next day, Tim's family went on a trip. Mom put her <abbr title="n. 工资单">**payroll**</abbr> paper in her bag – she needed to take it to her office later. They drove past a tall <abbr title="n. 树篱">**hedge**</abbr> cut into a maze. Through a window, Tim saw an old church with a beautiful <abbr title="n. 壁画">**fresco**</abbr> on the wall. It showed angels flying.

At the beach, the sea looked <abbr title="adj. 波涛汹涌">**choppy**</abbr>. Big waves crashed on the sand. Suddenly, they saw a big <abbr title="n. 卡车">**lorry**</abbr> stuck in the wet sand. The driver looked worried. "I need a tool to give more **torque** to the wheel," he said. Tim's dad had a **torque** wrench in his car, so he helped. They pulled the **lorry** free. Dad said, "That was a team **feat**."

After lunch, which was a bit <abbr title="adj. 淡的">**bland**</abbr> because they forgot the salt, they went to climb a hill. The path was <abbr title="adj. 弯弯曲曲的">**tortuous**</abbr> – it went left, then right, then up and down. It took a long time, but they reached the <abbr title="n. 山顶">**peak**</abbr> at last. From the top, they saw fields below in <abbr title="adj. 同心的">**concentric**</abbr> circles, like a big target.

Suddenly, they heard a little dog barking. The <abbr title="n. 出现">**emergence**</abbr> of a small puppy from behind a rock surprised them. It was tied to a post, like a poor <abbr title="n. 人质">**hostage**</abbr>. Then a man with a <abbr title="n. 鞭子">**whip**</abbr> came out. "Stay away from my dog!" he shouted. Tim felt scared.

Dad stepped forward. He tried to talk, but the man lifted the **whip**. Dad quickly grabbed the man's arm and <abbr title="v. 制服">**subdued**</abbr> him. The man fell down. Mom called the police. Soon, the police arrived and began the <abbr title="n. 事件处理">**proceedings**</abbr> to take the man away. The little **hostage** puppy was safe. Tim untied the rope and the dog licked his hand.

Walking down the **tortuous** path again, Tim thought about the day. From his **certificate** to the rescue, it was a day full of **feats**. He was proud that Dad **subdued** the bad man and saved the **hostage** puppy. The **emergence** of a hero in his own dad was wonderful. He would not forget the **fresco**, the **concentric** fields, or the **choppy** sea.

### 02

Lily woke up late on the day of the school fair. The fair was to raise <abbr title="n. 收益">**proceeds**</abbr> for the library. The library had a big <abbr title="n. 赤字">**deficit**</abbr> – it did not have enough money to buy new books. So, all the **proceeds** from the fair would help cover the **deficit**.

Lily rushed out. Because she was <abbr title="adj. 迟到的">**tardy**</abbr>, she ran all the way. When she arrived, her friend Tom was also **tardy**. “Sorry I’m **tardy**,” Tom said.

The fair was in the <abbr title="adj. 古雅的">**quaint**</abbr> town square. The **quaint** old buildings had red roofs and small windows. It looked like a storybook.

First, Lily went to the <abbr title="n. 口味">**flavour**</abbr> booth. You could taste ice cream and guess the **flavour**. Lily tasted one. “This **flavour** is strawberry!” she said. She was right because the sweet taste was just like strawberries.

Next to it was the <abbr title="adj. 嗅觉的">**olfactory**</abbr> game. You had to close your eyes and use your nose to smell things. The **olfactory** test was fun. Lily smelled a flower, and her **olfactory** sense told her it was a rose.

Near the pond, Lily saw a pretty <abbr title="n. 蜻蜓">**dragonfly**</abbr>. It had long, thin wings and flew fast over the water.

Then, Lily met her pet <abbr title="n. 猎犬">**hound**</abbr>, Max. Max was a friendly **hound** with long ears. Tom brought his cat, but Max the **hound** just wagged his tail.

At the <abbr title="n. 笔迹学">**graphology**</abbr> booth, a man looked at your handwriting. He used **graphology** to tell things about you. Lily wrote her name, and the man said, “Your **graphology** shows you are a kind person.”

Inside the old <abbr title="n. 房间">**chamber**</abbr> of the town hall, there was a <abbr title="adj. 触觉的">**tactile**</abbr> box. You put your hand in and felt a thing without looking. The **tactile** feeling told you what it was. Lily felt a soft, round object. She used her **tactile** sense and guessed, “A wool ball!”

Nearby, a model train showed its <abbr title="n. 运动">**locomotion**</abbr>. The train’s **locomotion** was smooth as it went around the track. A sign above the train had an <abbr title="n. 头韵">**alliteration**</abbr>: “Tiny Train’s Terrific Travels.” An **alliteration** is when words begin with the same sound.

Lily needed a prize from a stuck box. She used a stick to <abbr title="v. 撬开">**pry**</abbr> it open. With a strong push, she managed to **pry** the lid up.

The <abbr title="n. 光学">**optics**</abbr> booth had a glass of water and a pencil. The **optics** experiment showed that the pencil looked bent in the water. It was a trick of light, or **optics**.

Then, Lily saw a goat at the pet area. The goat had a small <abbr title="n. 寄生虫">**parasite**</abbr> on its ear – a tick. The tick was a **parasite** that lived on the goat’s blood. The goat was very <abbr title="adj. 厚颜无耻的">**brazen**</abbr>. It was **brazen** enough to steal a sandwich right out of a boy’s hand! Everyone laughed, but the **brazen** goat just chewed happily.

At the <abbr title="n. 注册">**enrolment**</abbr> table, people could sign up for the pet show. Lily did the **enrolment** for Max the **hound**. She filled in the **enrolment** form.

The farmer sold eggs from his <abbr title="n. 家禽">**poultry**</abbr>. The **poultry** included chickens and ducks. The eggs were fresh.

While running to see the pet show, Lily stepped on a sharp stone and hurt her <abbr title="n. 脚后跟">**heel**</abbr>. Her **heel** was sore, but she walked on.

At the end of the day, the head teacher counted the **proceeds**. The **proceeds** from the fair were enough to cover the library’s **deficit**. Everyone cheered. Lily smiled, even with her sore **heel**.

### 03

Lily’s dog Max did not want to play. He lay on the floor with heavy eyes. Max showed deep <abbr title="n. 疲劳">**weariness**</abbr> after his morning walk. Lily got worried and called the local <abbr title="n. 兽医">**veterinarian**</abbr>. She put Max into a small box and walked to the animal clinic.

On the way, she passed a neighbor’s kitchen. A sweet smell of <abbr title="n. 杏仁">**almond**</abbr> cookies came through the window. Lily remembered that **almond** was bad for dogs. She made sure Max did not eat any.

The **veterinarian** checked Max’s ears and found a tiny <abbr title="n. 螨虫">**mite**</abbr> inside. “This little **mite** is making him itch and tired,” she said. She gave Lily a special <abbr title="n. 食谱">**recipe**</abbr> for soft food, because Max needed only safe meats and rice. The **recipe** did not use any nut or milk.

On the walk home, Lily saw a poster for the school <abbr title="n. 抽奖">**raffle**</abbr>. The **raffle** was to help fix the old <abbr title="adj. 殖民风格的">**colonial**</abbr> building. That **colonial** building had tall white columns. Lily wanted to bake cookies for the cake sale. She used a <abbr title="n. 新月">**crescent**</abbr>-shaped cutter from her new baking <abbr title="n. 工具包">**kit**</abbr>. Her **kit** also had tiny star and dog molds.

Her little brother Tom ran into the kitchen and stepped on the cookie tray. He **crescent** cookies. Lily almost cried. The first batch became a mess, but she did not give up. She checked the **recipe** again and saw a **redundant** step—it said to chill water twice for no reason. She skipped that **redundant** part and mixed the dough again.

The next morning, the sky looked <abbr title="adj. 阴沉的">**dismal**</abbr> and gray. Lily put the new cookies in a tin and took her <abbr title="n. 水瓶">**flask**</abbr> of water. She carried a poster she drew about the **raffle**. The **raffle** table was busy and full of **momentum**. More and more kids bought tickets. Her teacher asked her to be a **delegate** for the **automotive** design club, because Lily had made a small car model. As a **delegate**, she would show her work at a big **automotive** fair next week.

At the corner of the bake sale, Lily saw a tiny <abbr title="adj. 家养的">**domestic**</abbr> cat hiding under a chair. It was not a wild cat; it was a clean **domestic** pet with a blue collar. Lily reached out her hand, but the cat was <abbr title="adj. 不屈服的">**unyielding**</abbr>. It stayed stiff and **unyielding** for a long minute, not moving forward. Lily sat still and waited. At last the cat relaxed and let her read a tag on its **peripheral** edge of the collar. The tag was turned to the side, almost in a **peripheral** spot that was hard to see. It had a phone number.

Later, Lily found the owner and gave the cat back. She felt proud. On the way home, she passed a box of her old dresses. She had **outgrown** them over the summer. She decided to give the **outgrown** clothes to a charity shop. Max, now full of new **momentum**, ran to Lily at the door, his tail wagging fast. His **weariness** was all gone. Lily smiled and gave him a gentle hug.

### 04

It was a sunny day. Our class went to an old manor house near a <abbr title="n. 稻田">**paddy**</abbr> field. The house was a place of <abbr title="n. 古迹">**antiquity**</abbr>. It was very old and full of stories. Inside, the big hall was <abbr title="adj. 豪华的">**sumptuous**</abbr> with colorful <abbr title="n. 挂毯">**tapestry**</abbr> on the walls. We all stopped and looked up. Our teacher, Mrs. Green, told us we would learn to <abbr title="v. 搅拌">**churn**</abbr> butter later. She also said the butter had no <abbr title="n. 添加剂">**additive**</abbr>, just fresh cream.

As we walked outside, Tom slipped on a wet stone and hurt his <abbr title="n. 脚踝">**ankle**</abbr>. He sat down with a cry. Mrs. Green saw this and said, “Let us <abbr title="v. 延期">**adjourn**</abbr> our walk for ten minutes.” Everyone waited. Some friends came to see Tom. Lily took the <abbr title="n. 主动性">**initiative**</abbr> to bring Tom a cold drink. Tom smiled and said thank you. He felt much better. He sat under a tree to rest.

While resting, Tom noticed a statue in the garden. It had a small <abbr title="n. 缺陷">**flaw**</abbr> – a crack on its arm. Mrs. Green explained that even old things have flaws. Tom thought that was interesting. Then Tom saw a wooden <abbr title="n. 箱子">**chest**</abbr> nearby. It was very old. He opened it and touched the dusty inside. His fingers became <abbr title="adj. 弄脏的">**begrimed**</abbr> with black dirt. He was <abbr title="adj. 习惯的">**accustomed**</abbr> to getting dirty because he often helped his grandpa in the garden. He just wiped his hands on his trousers. He did not mind the dirt.

Soon, we went to make butter. We took turns to **churn** the cream in a big jar. The cream was thick and white. Tom’s arms felt <abbr title="adj. 松弛的">**flabby**</abbr> at first, so the churning was hard. We worked until we almost <abbr title="v. 耗尽">**depleted**</abbr> our energy. Finally, the butter formed. We tasted it on bread. It was <abbr title="adj. 极好的">**terrific**</abbr>! No **additive**s, just pure taste. We all wanted more.

After lunch, we walked near the **paddy** field. The rice plants were green and tall. Farmer Lee told us about an <abbr title="adj. 入侵的">**invasive**</abbr> plant that took water from the rice. This could **deplete** the water in the field. A boy said we should pull out the plant. But Lily had an idea to use a machine. Her idea soon <abbr title="v. 推翻">**overturned**</abbr> the first plan because it was faster. Farmer Lee nodded. It was an <abbr title="adj. 明显的">**overt**</abbr> choice; everyone agreed it was better. We helped Farmer Lee mark the plants. We were proud of our work.

Later, Mrs. Green pointed to the old manor. She smiled and said, “Look at that **tapestry** again. See its bright colors? The makers used no **additive** colors long ago.” We saw the **sumptuous** hall once more. Our guide, an old man, had a healthy <abbr title="n. 面色">**complexion**</abbr> from working outdoors. He told us the manor was a true **antiquity** and even the **chest** in the garden was a treasure. He said many visitors missed it but Tom was clever to find it.

As the trip ended, Tom’s **ankle** felt better. He thanked Lily for her **initiative**. The whole day was **terrific**. We learned so much – from butter churning to **invasive** plants. On the bus, Mrs. Green said, “The meeting about next trip will **adjourn** until Monday.” Everyone cheered. Tom looked out the window and thought about the fun day. It was a day full of fun and learning. Everyone wanted another trip like that.

## 06月09日

### 01

Our summer holiday started with a long bus ride. When we got off, I smelled the sea right away. Last summer, I went with my family to a small <abbr title="adj. 沿海的">**maritime**</abbr> town. There were many boats at the dock, and the air smelled of salt. We stayed in an old, tall building with many small flats. The walls were white, and we climbed many stairs to our room. It was a <abbr title="n. 廉价公寓">**tenement**</abbr>, but it felt cozy. Our next-door neighbor was an old man named Tom. He wore soft, comfortable shoes inside the house – one had a hole at the toe. He shuffled his feet when he walked. They were his favorite <abbr title="n. 拖鞋">**slipper**</abbr>.

Tom worked for the weather station. He had many weather charts on his wall. He watched the sky and told people if it would rain. He was a <abbr title="n. 气象学家">**meteorologist**</abbr>, a true weather expert. When we met him, his first <abbr title="n. 话语">**utterance**</abbr> was, “The sea will sing for you today.” It sounded strange, <abbr title="conj. 尽管">**albeit**</abbr> a little poetic. We all smiled at his funny way of speaking.

The next morning, a light gray <abbr title="n. 薄雾">**haze**</abbr> floated over the water. I could not see the end of the pier. Tom showed us his small wooden house by the shore. It was not a real house; it was a tiny <abbr title="n. 棚屋">**shack**</abbr> where he kept his fishing tools. “Don’t <abbr title="v. 贬低">**abase**</abbr> yourself because you live in a small place,” my dad said, but Tom just smiled and shook his head.

Later, we planned a picnic on the sand. Mom put sandwiches and cold juice into a big basket with a lid. That was our <abbr title="n. 野餐篮">**hamper**</abbr>. But when we opened it, some hungry seagulls had flown in and <abbr title="v. 弄坏">**despoil**</abbr>ed our food! Bread was everywhere, and the juice bottle was broken. We were so sad, but Mom laughed. So we had to <abbr title="v. 光顾">**patronize**</abbr> the little bakery down the street to buy fresh bread and warm cakes.

In the afternoon, dark clouds came. The wind blew hard, and the waves crashed against the rocks. But Tom pointed at the sky and said the storm would soon <abbr title="v. 平息">**subside**</abbr>. And he was right; after an hour, the wind stopped, and the sea became calm again. The sun came out, and children flew kites on the beach.

We decided to fix his old wooden boat. We used a shiny liquid that protected the wood from water. Tom called it <abbr title="n. 清漆">**varnish**</abbr>. We all worked together, holding the boat steady and painting with small brushes. Tom told us stories as we worked. Our <abbr title="n. 团结">**cohesion**</abbr> made the job quick and fun.

A young man in a blue uniform arrived on a bicycle. He handed Tom a package with a bright stamp. He was the <abbr title="n. 快递员">**courier**</abbr> from the post office.

In the evening, we went to a concert at the big church. At the entrance, we left our coats in a small room with many hooks. The sign on the door said <abbr title="n. 衣帽间">**cloakroom**</abbr>. Inside, the choir sang a long, beautiful piece about a story from the Bible. There was no acting, just singing and music. Tom told me it was an <abbr title="n. 清唱剧">**oratorio**</abbr>. I looked at the words in the program and saw many small words like ‘in’, ‘on’, and ‘under’. My teacher had said these are called <abbr title="n. 介词">**preposition**</abbr>s, and their <abbr title="n. 普遍">**prevalence**</abbr> in English is very high. You find them in almost every sentence.

That night, I felt happy. The little maritime town and its people had given me a wonderful summer day.

### 02

Lily felt <abbr title="adj. 矛盾的">**ambivalent**</abbr> as she stared at the piano keys. Part of her wanted to play the song perfectly, but another part wanted to run back home and hide under her bed. Her mother, sitting beside her, smiled. “I have a strong <abbr title="n. 信念">**conviction**</abbr> that you will shine today. Just breathe.”

It was the day of the big piano <abbr title="n. 独奏会">**recital**</abbr> at the school hall. A boy named Jake walked up to her. “I’m playing a song with ten fast scales in a row,” he said loudly. “It’s really hard.” Lily knew Jake was just trying to <abbr title="v. 虚张声势">**bluff**</abbr> because he often forgot to practice. She could tell he was nervous, too.

<abbr title="adv. 迄今为止">**Hitherto**</abbr>, Lily had only played her little keyboard at home for her cat, Mr. Whiskers. The cat always purred, giving her a quiet “good job.” But now, the rows of chairs and the bright lights made her legs feel shaky.

She tried to smooth her favorite blue <abbr title="n. 棉">**cotton**</abbr> dress, but a tiny tag at the back of her neck began to <abbr title="v. 发痒">**itch**</abbr> terribly. She scratched and scratched, wishing she had cut it off. The annoying feeling made it hard to think about the notes.

Soon it was her turn. She played every note she had practiced. When she finished, the three judges whispered together. Their looks were <abbr title="adj. 不确定的">**inconclusive**</abbr> —no big smiles, but no frowns either. Lily stepped off the stage feeling that her performance was a little <abbr title="adj. 缺乏的">**deficient**</abbr> in the strong feeling the song needed.

Later, her dad came to pick her up. He was a tired <abbr title="n. 通勤者">**commuter**</abbr> who drove his car a long way every morning. “The engine’s <abbr title="n. 燃烧">**combustion**</abbr> is not smooth today,” he sighed. “And the traffic was pure <abbr title="n. 烦恼">**aggravation**</abbr>!” Lily held his hand, understanding his tired face.

At dinner, Mom told a funny story from her work. She studied <abbr title="n. 人类学">**anthropology**</abbr>, learning about people from long ago and their interesting ways of life. Lily loved hearing tales of ancient festivals and songs.

Back at the **recital**, the stage had been decorated with bright <abbr title="adj. 装饰的">**ornamental**</abbr> flowers made of paper. Lily had <abbr title="v. 假设">**postulated**</abbr> that if she played without a single mistake, she would surely win a gold star. But the judges gave <abbr title="adj. 定量的">**quantitative**</abbr> scores on a card—just numbers from one to ten. Her score was good, but not the best.

Jake came over holding a rubbery octopus toy. One of its long <abbr title="n. 触手">**tentacle**</abbr> parts dangled and wiggled. “Look, it has eight **tentacles**,” he said, trying to make Lily laugh. She giggled and forgot about her **itch** for a while.

Then Lily saw a smaller girl crying because she had dropped her cookie. Without thinking, Lily gave the girl her own chocolate chip treat. It was a small, <abbr title="adj. 利他的">**altruistic**</abbr> act that made the little girl smile again. Lily felt a warm glow inside.

Standing outside the hall, Lily looked up at the hill behind the school. The top, a small green <abbr title="n. 顶峰">**summit**</abbr>, seemed to shine in the afternoon sun. She imagined herself climbing right up to that **summit**, and suddenly, her **ambivalent** feelings melted away. Her **conviction** grew—not just about music, but about trying her best in everything.

And any little sting of <abbr title="n. 嫉妒">**jealousy**</abbr> she might have felt about Jake’s cool toy or someone else’s high score was completely gone. The day had been full of surprises, but Lily walked home feeling strong and happy.

### 03

Tom’s grandpa sat in his favorite chair, but he coughed a lot. The doctor came and gave a clear <abbr title="n. 诊断">**diagnosis**</abbr>. “His breathing trouble is <abbr title="adj. 呼吸的">**respiratory**</abbr>,” the doctor said softly. “It is linked to an old sickness he had as a boy—<abbr title="n. 小儿麻痹症">**poliomyelitis**</abbr>.” Grandpa’s legs had been weak ever since.  

That afternoon, Grandpa felt a little sad. He began a quiet <abbr title="n. 回顾">**retrospective**</abbr>, telling Tom about his younger days. “I was an <abbr title="n. 电工">**electrician**</abbr>,” he said, showing his old toolbox. “I could fix any broken wire. I used a hot iron to **<abbr title="v. 焊接">**solder**</abbr>** tiny metal parts together so lights would work again.” Tom watched as Grandpa picked up a small red <abbr title="n. 四轮运货车">**wagon**</abbr> toy. “I had a real one, big enough to carry all my tools. The wooden bottom had a long <abbr title="n. 接缝">**seam**</abbr> down the middle, but I **solder**ed a metal strip over the **seam** to make it strong.”  

Grandpa looked at Tom. “Sickness can slowly <abbr title="v. 侵蚀">**erode**</abbr> your strength, just like water **erode**s stone. That’s why you must always take care of your body. Hold your health in high <abbr title="n. 尊重">**esteem**</abbr>.” Tom nodded. He really did **esteem** his grandpa’s words.  

At school, Tom’s science teacher announced a project. “You will each make a <abbr title="n. 一批">**batch**</abbr> of five small water filters. The first filter must use a material that is <abbr title="adj. 可渗透的">**permeable**</abbr>—water can pass through it.” Tom brought a piece of cloth that was very **permeable**. From the <abbr title="n. 开始">**inception**</abbr> of the project, Tom worked with great care. He cut a small <abbr title="n. 刻痕">**notch**</abbr> on each filter’s side, like a mark to show the water level. **Even** his friend said, “You **even** put a **notch** on all of them? That’s so neat.”  

Tom also added something special. For the class show, he did an <abbr title="n. 模仿">**impersonation**</abbr> of a famous scientist. His **impersonation** was so funny and clever that everyone laughed and clapped. The teacher smiled at the <abbr title="n. 新颖性">**novelty**</abbr> of his idea—no one else had acted like a scientist! This **novelty** helped his project **<abbr title="v. 产生">**yield**</abbr>** top marks. The project’s success **yield**ed a big gold star.  

After school, a friendly school <abbr title="n. 职员">**clerk**</abbr> came to the classroom. The **clerk** said, “Your project has been chosen for the town science fair. The fair is in an <abbr title="adj. 边远的">**outlying**</abbr> area, near the old farm on the edge of town.” Tom was excited. He had never been to that **outlying** place before. He ran home to tell Grandpa the good news.  

That evening, Tom gave Grandpa the little red **wagon** toy. “Thank you for the story about your **wagon** and your **solder**ing,” Tom said. “I learned that even old things have strong **seam**s if you fix them well. And I will always **esteem** my health, so sickness doesn’t **erode** it.” Grandpa coughed a little, his **respiratory** problem still there, but he smiled. The **diagnosis** was serious, but love and memories made everything feel a little better.

### 04

Tom was a **promising** boy in the town. Everyone **deemed** him smart. But that summer, he did an <abbr title="adj. 大胆的">**audacious**</abbr> thing: he knocked on the door of the old house at the end of the road. The man who lived there was Mr. Griggs, an <abbr title="adj. 古怪的">**eccentric**</abbr> old man with long, <abbr title="adj. 卷曲的">**curly**</abbr> white hair that looked like cotton candy. People said strange things about him, but Tom thought those rumors were <abbr title="adj. 无根据的">**unsubstantiated**</abbr>.

Mr. Griggs opened the door. “Come in, boy,” he said. His <abbr title="n. 措辞">**diction**</abbr> was very clear and old-fashioned, like a teacher from a hundred years ago. Tom stepped inside. The room was almost empty. Only a chair and a table. “I like a <abbr title="adj. 极简的">**minimalist**</abbr> space,” said Mr. Griggs. “Few things, but each one has a story.”

He pointed to a metal box on the table. “That’s a <abbr title="n. 分发器">**dispenser**</abbr>,” he said. “I used to put candy in it, but now it just holds air.” Tom saw a trumpet-like thing in the corner. “Is that a trumpet?” Tom asked. “No, it’s a <abbr title="n. 短号">**cornet**</abbr>,” said Mr. Griggs. “It looks like a trumpet but has a softer sound. I played it long ago.” He sighed.

Tom noticed a crack on the wall. “Why is there a <abbr title="n. 裂缝">**fissure**</abbr> here?” he asked. “Oh, that’s from the big storm last year,” Mr. Griggs said. “Water came in, but I didn’t fix it. It reminds me of time.”

Later, Mr. Griggs showed Tom a book. “I only read <abbr title="n. 非虚构作品">**nonfiction**</abbr>,” he said. “This one is about animal bodies.” He opened it to a picture of a long, twisted tube. “This is the <abbr title="n. 肠子">**intestine**</abbr> of a pig,” he said. “It helps the pig digest food. Very important, but not pretty to look at.” Tom made a face.

Then Mr. Griggs pulled out an old <abbr title="n. 钞票">**banknote**</abbr>. It was yellow and had big numbers on it. “See the date?” he said. “It’s <abbr title="v. 填迟日期">**postdated**</abbr>—someone wrote a future date on it, so it could not be used until then. But that day never came.” He laughed.

Tom decided to <abbr title="v. 吐露秘密">**confide**</abbr> in Mr. Griggs. “I want to be a musician, but my parents say it’s not a real job,” he said. Mr. Griggs nodded. “People **deem** many things unacceptable, but you must follow your heart. When I was young, I was a **promising** **cornet** player. The <abbr title="adj. 盛行的">**prevailing**</abbr> belief back then was that music was only a hobby. So I stopped. Now I’m just a strange old man with a **cornet**.”

“You’re not strange,” Tom said. “Just different.”

Suddenly, they heard a loud voice outside. It was the mayor, a <abbr title="n. 平民主义者">**populist**</abbr> who always promised to make the town better but did little. He was shouting about cleaning up “useless old houses.” Mr. Griggs frowned. “He uses <abbr title="adj. 不分青红皂白的">**indiscriminate**</abbr> words—he attacks everyone without reason. But his words are **unsubstantiated**.”

“Why is he so angry?” Tom asked. “Because I refused to give him my **banknote**,” Mr. Griggs said. “He claimed it was valuable for the town museum. But it’s mine.”

Tom looked at the **banknote** again. “Maybe we should hide it,” he said **audaciously**. Mr. Griggs smiled. “You are a smart <abbr title="n. 婴儿">**infant**</abbr>—no, not an infant, a young man. Sorry, my **diction** confuses me sometimes.”

They hid the **banknote** inside the **dispenser**. Then they waited until the shouting stopped.

As Tom left, Mr. Griggs gave him the **cornet**. “It’s yours,” he said. “You have a **promising** future. Don’t let the **prevailing** opinion stop you.”

Tom walked home, holding the **cornet** carefully. He thought about the **intestine** picture, the **fissure**, and the **minimalist** room. He felt like he had gained a secret friend. From that day on, he practiced the **cornet** every evening, and he no longer **deemed** himself too young to dream.

## 06月10日

### 01

Emma and Jack were in the attic when they found an old paper. The <abbr title="n. 标题">**caption**</abbr> at the top said: “Time Travel Kit.” It was a <abbr title="n. 专利">**patent**</abbr> from 1900. “Let’s <abbr title="v. 制作">**fabricate**</abbr> it!” Jack said. They gathered old <abbr title="n. 陶器">**crockery**</abbr> plates, wires, and a big clock. When they finished, the machine made a loud hum. Then, everything went dark.

They opened their eyes next to a big stone castle. A deep <abbr title="n. 护城河">**moat**</abbr> surrounded it. The water was cold and dark. A guard shouted, “Bring the <abbr title="n. 烈酒">**liquor**</abbr> for the king!” He carried a bottle with a strong smell. Emma and Jack hid behind a bush. They saw a man sitting and writing. “I’m writing a new <abbr title="n. 作品">**composition**</abbr>,” he said. “It’s a poem about the moon.” Suddenly, the man cut his hand on a sharp rock. Red blood ran out fast. Emma saw the <abbr title="n. 动脉">**artery**</abbr> in his wrist. She quickly tied a cloth to stop the blood. The man smiled and pointed to a small horse. “Take my <abbr title="adj. 驯养的">**domesticated**</abbr> horse. She’s very calm and gentle.”

They climbed on the horse, and the machine buzzed again. This time, they landed on a wide, sunny prairie. A <abbr title="n. 自耕农">**homesteader**</abbr> was building a wooden house. “Hello! Are you tired? Drink some water,” he said. He handed them a **crockery** cup. The water was cool and fresh. Emma looked at a big green garden behind the house. The homesteader said, “These plants use <abbr title="n. 光合作用">**photosynthesis**</abbr> to grow. The sun gives them power to make food.”

Then, the machine took them to a busy city near the <abbr title="n. 赤道">**equator**</abbr>. The sun was so strong that the air felt like a hot blanket. They met a man in a white coat. “I am a <abbr title="n. 神经学家">**neurologist**</abbr>,” he said. “I study how the brain works and helps us think.” He pointed to the crowded streets. “A big <abbr title="n. 涌入">**influx**</abbr> of people came here to find jobs—whole families searching for a better life.” He looked at the time machine. “You must <abbr title="v. 隔离">**sequester**</abbr> this machine in a quiet room to fix it. Do not <abbr title="v. 忽视">**neglect**</abbr> this step, or things will go wrong.” They found a small, empty room and began to work. Jack stayed <abbr title="adj. 坚定的">**firm**</abbr> and did not stop, even when his hands got tired.

While fixing the machine, they saw that the <abbr title="n. 年表">**chronology**</abbr> inside it was broken—the dates were all mixed up. They needed help from someone who knew history. Suddenly, the machine glowed and sent them to a big hall. A tall man stood on a stage. “I am an <abbr title="n. 废奴主义者">**abolitionist**</abbr>,” he said with a strong voice. “I fight to end slavery and make all people free.” He gave them an old book with correct dates. They fixed the machine quickly. The man patted their shoulders. “Your long trip is a true <abbr title="n. 好事">**boon**</abbr>. You learn so much from seeing different times!”

With the machine finally working, Emma and Jack returned home. The old **crockery** and wires were still on the floor. “What an adventure!” Emma said, looking at the **caption** on the patent. Jack smiled and said, “I will never **neglect** a chance to learn something new!”

### 02

It was the day of the school talent show. The sky was <abbr title="adj. 阴沉的">**overcast**</abbr>. Tom looked in the mirror. He saw a big red <abbr title="n. 青春痘">**pimple**</abbr> on his nose. He felt very <abbr title="adj. 胆小的">**cowardly**</abbr>. How could he go on stage now?

“Your act is <abbr title="adj. 无与伦比的">**peerless**</abbr>,” said Lily. She was always <abbr title="adj. 直言不讳的">**plainspoken**</abbr>. “Forget the silly **pimple**.”

Tom still wanted to hide. They walked to the school yard. Near the stage, a small <abbr title="n. 小屋">**hut**</abbr> was built for their play. A lemonade <abbr title="n. 售货亭">**booth**</abbr> stood next to it. Tom’s brother Jack was there. Jack had a <abbr title="adj. 先天性的">**congenital**</abbr> heart problem. But he always smiled. He liked to <abbr title="v. 提倡">**advocate**</abbr> for chasing dreams. “Don’t let fear become your <abbr title="n. 厄运">**doom**</abbr>,” he said. “Even with my **congenital** condition, I choose joy.” He took a pill every morning to help his <abbr title="adj. 新陈代谢的">**metabolic**</abbr> rate. The medicine made his **metabolic** system work better.

Tom nodded. He tried to be brave. Suddenly, he hit his <abbr title="n. 胫骨">**shin**</abbr> on a wooden box. He almost <abbr title="v. 流下">**shed**</abbr> tears from the pain. Lily helped him up. He rubbed his **shin** and took a deep breath.

The group had a <abbr title="n. 分裂">**schism**</abbr> earlier. Half wanted to change the story. The other half said no. But they talked and fixed the **schism**. Now they were ready.

Their play was a <abbr title="adj. 虚构的">**fictional**</abbr> tale. Tom played an <abbr title="n. 杂技演员">**acrobat**</abbr>. The evil king wanted to <abbr title="v. 砍头">**decapitate**</abbr> him. But the **acrobat** used a trick. The king’s rope was <abbr title="adj. 无弹性的">**inelastic**</abbr>. It could not stretch at all. The **inelastic** rope helped the **acrobat** escape. The fake **decapitate** scene made everyone laugh. Tom forgot his **cowardly** feeling. He **shed** happy sweat instead.

Lily was <abbr title="adj. 熟悉的">**conversant**</abbr> with every line. She prompted anyone who forgot. She was so **conversant** that even the teacher praised her.

After the show, the audience began to <abbr title="v. 狂热赞扬">**rave**</abbr>. “That was **peerless**!” they said. The **rave** reviews made Tom smile. He went to the **hut** to rest. The **booth** sold all its lemonade. The sky was still **overcast**, but no one cared. Jack continued to **advocate** for hope. Tom looked in the mirror again. The **pimple** was still there, but it did not matter. The **doom** feeling was gone. He loved the **fictional** world of the stage. Lily’s **plainspoken** words had been right. Now Tom felt like a true **acrobat** of his own life.

### 03

Lucy pushed open the wooden door of the chicken coop. A mother hen sat proudly with her little <abbr title="n. 一窝幼崽">**brood**</abbr> of five fluffy chicks. But the chicks looked strange. They were so <abbr title="adj. 无精打采的">**lethargic**</abbr> that they didn't even move when Lucy put grain in front of them. They just lay there, eyes half-closed. Lucy felt worried. “Are they sick?” she wondered. Then she saw their water bowl was empty. “Oh, you're just hot and thirsty!” She quickly filled the bowl with cool water. Soon, the chicks drank and started peeping happily. They were not **lethargic** anymore!

After feeding the chicks, Lucy’s dad called her. “Lucy! I need you to take this bag of <abbr title="n. 核桃">**walnuts**</abbr> to the dock. I’ll load them onto my <abbr title="n. 单桅帆船">**sloop**</abbr>. Later, I might <abbr title="v. 以物易物">**barter**</abbr> some **walnuts** for fresh fish from the fishermen.” Dad was the <abbr title="n. 业主">**proprietor**</abbr> of a small nut shop. Lucy took the heavy bag and walked out.

The path to the village was muddy from last night’s rain. Lucy had to jump over puddles of sticky <abbr title="n. 泥浆">**slurry**</abbr> that looked like chocolate milk. “Yuck, I don’t want to step in that,” she said. Near the village square, she saw a crowd around the notice board. Someone had put up a note with no <abbr title="n. 标点符号">**punctuation**</abbr> at all. It read: “THE OLD BAKER USES BAD FLOUR HIS BREAD IS NOT SAFE DO NOT EAT IT.” Without **punctuation**, the message was hard to understand and seemed scary. A man was loudly explaining, “This is just a mean trick! Someone wants to <abbr title="v. 取代">**supplant**</abbr> our kind baker. A city <abbr title="n. 老手">**sophisticate**</abbr> arrived yesterday and wants to open a fancy bakery. He’s trying to spread this fake news for the <abbr title="n. 传播">**dissemination**</abbr> of fear.” Everyone nodded. Lucy thought the **dissemination** of lies was awful.

She continued to the dock, where the blue **sloop** bobbed on the water. Dad was fixing a rope. “Thanks, Lucy! These **walnuts** will make a good trade.” Lucy waved goodbye and headed back.

On the way home, she passed her sister May’s beauty shop. Through the window, Lucy saw May working. A lady was getting her nails done, and she kept saying sweet things to May. “You are the best <abbr title="n. 美甲师">**manicurist**</abbr> in the whole town! Your hands are magic!” It was clear the lady was trying to <abbr title="v. 奉承">**flatter**</abbr> May, maybe to get a discount. May just smiled politely.

When Lucy reached her house, she noticed the back door was <abbr title="adj. 微开着的">**ajar**</abbr>. She pushed it open and gasped. Her little brother Tom was sitting on the floor, squeezing a tube of <abbr title="n. 胶水">**glue**</abbr> onto his hands. The **glue** was everywhere—on the floor, on the cat, even on his hair! Lucy felt a wave of hot anger rise inside her. She started to <abbr title="v. 怒火中烧">**seethe**</abbr>. “Tom! What are you doing?” she shouted. She was so angry, she could almost see steam coming out of her ears, just like a pot boiling. Tom got so scared by her **seething** face that his stomach turned, and he suddenly <abbr title="v. 呕吐">**vomited**</abbr> on the floor.

Mom hurried in. “Oh dear! First the **glue**, now this!” She cleaned up the **vomit** and then said, “The smell is also coming from the drain. I think there is a problem with the <abbr title="n. 下水道系统">**sewerage**</abbr>.” Dad would have to fix it later. Lucy calmed down, stopped **seething**, and helped mop the floor.

In the afternoon, Dad came back from the sea. He said the waves had started to **seethe** on the horizon, so he came back early. “The water was bubbling like hot soup,” he explained. That was another kind of **seethe**.

To cheer everyone up, Dad took Lucy and Tom to the <abbr title="n. 山脊">**ridge**</abbr> behind the farm. The sun was warm on the grassy **ridge**. Tom brought a ball and hit it high towards Lucy. She hit it back quickly before it touched the ground—a perfect <abbr title="n. 截击球">**volley**</abbr>. “Nice **volley**!” Dad shouted. They played until the sky turned orange. From the **ridge**, Lucy could see Dad’s **sloop** returning to the harbor from another quick trip, and she felt happy. All bad moments were gone, like a puddle drying up in the sun.

### 04

Leo was an <abbr title="n. 乐观主义者">**optimist**</abbr>. He always believed things would get better. So when his uncle asked him to join an <abbr title="n. 探险">**expedition**</abbr> across the <abbr title="n. 大西洋">**Atlantic**</abbr>, Leo was excited. The trip sounded like a big adventure.

Before they left, the old ship needed a complete <abbr title="n. 彻底检修">**overhaul**</abbr>. Uncle Ben worked on the engine with amazing <abbr title="n. 精湛技巧">**virtuosity**</abbr>. He fixed every part quickly and perfectly. Leo watched and learned.

They packed lots of <abbr title="n. 器具">**utensils**</abbr> for cooking. Spoons, pots, and a small stove were all ready. As they sailed into the open sea, the weather turned bad. Giant waves hit the ship. The situation became very <abbr title="adj. 危险的">**precarious**</abbr>. One wrong move and they could fall into the water. Uncle Ben stayed calm, but everyone else was scared.

After days, they reached a small island. The storm had <abbr title="v. 连根拔起">**uprooted**</abbr> many big trees. Their roots stuck up into the air. It looked like a giant had played with them.

They walked carefully into the forest. Leo saw something move, but it was so <abbr title="adj. 不引人注目的">**unobtrusive**</abbr> that he almost missed it. A tiny animal with <abbr title="adj. 柔和的">**pastel**</abbr> pink fur sat under a bush. When they got closer, the animal stayed <abbr title="adj. 无法移动的">**immobile**</abbr>. It did not run away. That was strange.

“Look, it’s a <abbr title="n. 有袋动物">**marsupial**</abbr>!” Uncle Ben whispered. The creature had a pouch on its belly, just like a kangaroo. But it was much smaller. People had long made <abbr title="n. 推测">**speculations**</abbr> about such a rare **marsupial**, but no one had real proof before.

Leo saw that the little **marsupial** was hurt. Its leg was bleeding. They used some <abbr title="n. 纱布">**gauze**</abbr> from the first-aid kit to wrap the wound. As they cleaned it, they noticed a small <abbr title="n. 凝块">**clot**</abbr> of blood had already formed. That was a good sign.

While they helped it, Leo saw something unusual. The **marsupial** had two tails! That was a clear <abbr title="n. 突变">**mutation**</abbr>. Normal **marsupials** of this kind had only one tail. There was a big <abbr title="n. 差异">**discrepancy**</abbr> between what the old books described and what they saw. Maybe the **mutation** caused it.

Suddenly, the guide, Mr. Green, who had been saying he loved all animals, took out a small net. “I'll take it for the zoo,” he said with a greedy smile. Leo was shocked. Telling everyone to protect animals but then trying to capture one was pure <abbr title="n. 虚伪">**hypocrisy**</abbr>. Uncle Ben stopped him. “This is wrong, and you know it. That’s **hypocrisy**,” he said. The guide looked down and put the net away.

Later, they followed the **marsupial**, now able to walk, to a hidden cave. Inside, they found an ancient stone statue. It looked exactly like the **marsupial**—a perfect <abbr title="n. 复制品">**duplicate**</abbr> in rock. The statue even had two tails! The ancients must have seen this **mutation** long ago.

The adventure <abbr title="v. 达到顶点">**culminated**</abbr> in that moment. All their guesses, the hard trip, and the discovery came together right there. Leo felt his heart beat fast. Being an **optimist** had truly paid off.

They returned to the ship, and the **marsupial** waved its two little tails from the shore. Leo smiled. It was the best **expedition** ever.

## 06月11日

### 01

It was a warm Saturday morning. Lily went to visit her grandpa. She found him in the back garden. He was trimming a tall, green <abbr title="n. 树篱">**hedge**</abbr> with big scissors. The **hedge** looked like a sleeping dragon. “Why a dragon, Grandpa?” she asked. “Because dragons can fly, just like my old dreams,” he said with a smile. Then he put on a pair of <abbr title="n. 护目镜">**goggles**</abbr>. “These **goggles** keep the tiny leaves out of my eyes. Here, try a pair.” He handed Lily a smaller, blue pair of **goggles**. She put them on, and the world turned a soft blue.

After trimming, Grandpa led her up to the dusty attic. In the middle of the room sat a strange machine with a big seat, a round dial, and a metal pedal. “This is my <abbr title="adj. 模拟的">**analog**</abbr> flight simulator,” he said. “It’s not like a digital screen. That moving needle shows the height. You must <abbr title="v. 踩踏">**tread**</abbr> on the pedal to make the wings go up.” Lily sat down and began to **tread** the pedal. The needle jumped and her heart raced. It felt like a real adventure.

Right next to the simulator stood a small wooden instrument with yellow keys. “And this,” Grandpa whispered, “is a <abbr title="n. 古钢琴">**clavichord**</abbr>.” He pressed a key, and the **clavichord** made a sound as soft as a bird’s feather. “I used to play it when I was worried,” he said.

Then Grandpa pulled an old diary from a shelf. The pages were thin, but the handwriting was still very <abbr title="adj. 字迹清楚的">**legible**</abbr>. Lily read a page aloud: “Today I learned a new word from my <abbr title="n. 航空">**aviation**</abbr> book: ‘fuselage.’ So much <abbr title="n. 行话">**jargon**</abbr> to remember!” She looked up. “What is **aviation**?” “It means flying—the whole world of planes,” Grandpa said. “I was full of <abbr title="n. 狂热">**fanaticism**</abbr> for it. My **fanaticism** was so huge that I built models every night.”

“I was one of the best <abbr title="n. 工匠">**craftspeople**</abbr> in our town,” he explained. “We **craftspeople** made airplanes out of wood and cloth. My <abbr title="n. 真理">**gospel**</abbr> was simple: never stop learning. That was my true **gospel**.” Every morning, Grandpa did <abbr title="n. 有氧运动">**aerobics**</abbr> in the living room. The **aerobics** gave him a strong <abbr title="n. 体质">**constitution**</abbr>; even at seventy, he had a powerful **constitution** and never felt tired.

Then a sad shadow crossed his face. “But one dark night,” he went on, “a great storm came. It <abbr title="v. 严重毁坏">**eviscerated**</abbr> my little workshop. The terrible wind **eviscerated** the roof, and the rain ruined all my plans.” Lily held his hand. “I lost almost everything and faced <abbr title="n. 破产">**bankruptcy**</abbr>. I was very close to **bankruptcy**.”

“What did you do?” Lily asked in a quiet voice. “I did not give up. I sold a <abbr title="n. 债券">**bond**</abbr> to my best friends. With that **bond**, I bought new wood and tools. The <abbr title="n. 后果">**fallout**</abbr> from that disaster was painful, but it made me stronger.” He opened the diary again and pointed. “Look here, I used a <abbr title="n. 冒号">**colon**</abbr> to write a safety list: always wear **goggles**, check the wings, and believe in yourself. The **colon** helped me remember the most important rules.”

“And did you finally fly?” Lily asked. “Yes! I even built a little gadget to <abbr title="v. 使偏斜">**deflect**</abbr> birds away from the runway. It made a loud, funny noise to **deflect** them. And I found a special foam that could <abbr title="v. 中和">**neutralize**</abbr> dangerous oil spills. That foam could **neutralize** the fuel in seconds.” Lily’s eyes grew big. “You are a real hero, Grandpa!” He hugged her tight. “Remember, Lily, the **fallout** from hard times can help you become braver than ever.”

### 02

Last summer, Jack and his little sister Lucy went to visit their grandfather. Grandpa lived in a small village deep in the hills. Some city people called it a <abbr title="n. 乡下人">**hillbilly**</abbr> place, but Jack loved its green trees and quiet streams.

Grandpa was the oldest person in the village. He had a <abbr title="adj. 族长的">**patriarchal**</abbr> way of making decisions for the whole family. Everyone respected his words.

One morning, Grandpa said, “Let’s visit the home of our <abbr title="n. 祖先">**forebears**</abbr>.” He walked with Jack and Lucy across a wide <abbr title="n. 大片土地">**tract**</abbr> of golden grass. Butterflies danced around their feet. At the end of the tract stood a very old wooden house. Its windows were dusty, but it felt warm and safe inside.

“I want to make a <abbr title="n. 目录">**catalogue**</abbr>,” Grandpa told them. “We need a careful <abbr title="n. 验证">**verification**</abbr> of every old thing here. This will help the village museum.” Jack began to look through drawers. He found a small <abbr title="n. 墨盒">**cartridge**</abbr> —the kind used in an old ink printer. He showed it to Grandpa. “Ah, your grandmother used that cartridge to print letters to me when I was young,” Grandpa said softly.

The next day, the village prepared for its yearly <abbr title="n. 文艺复兴">**Renaissance**</abbr> fair. People were excited about the big costume <abbr title="n. 游行">**parade**</abbr>. Lucy had an idea to cook beef <abbr title="n. 炖菜">**stew**</abbr> for the food contest. She worked in the old kitchen. Suddenly, Jack smelled a strange <abbr title="adj. 气体的">**gaseous**</abbr> odor. He knew that smell—it was gas! He quickly ran to turn off the stove. Grandpa said, “You acted fast to <abbr title="v. 抑制">**curb**</abbr> a danger, Jack.”

At the fair, a man brought a trained <abbr title="n. 猎鹰">**falcon**</abbr>. The bird flew high and then returned to the man’s hand. Jack watched it closely. A strange, <abbr title="adj. 潜意识的">**subliminal**</abbr> feeling came over him. It was like a memory he could not quite touch. Maybe he had seen the same falcon in a dream.

Then dark clouds filled the sky, and heavy rain began to <abbr title="v. 淹没">**inundate**</abbr> the field. The water rose quickly. Everyone rushed to save tables and chairs. Poor Lucy fell and cut her leg badly. The fair doctor hurried over. He gave her a small shot for <abbr title="n. 麻醉">**anesthesia**</abbr> and stitched the cut. Lucy did not cry.

The rain was so strong that the parade had to stop. Grandpa held a family meeting. Some uncles wanted to give up the fair forever. But Grandpa made a <abbr title="n. 让步">**concession**</abbr>. “The young people can <abbr title="v. 改革">**reform**</abbr> the rules for next year,” he said. Jack and Lucy felt very proud.

That evening they sat inside, eating warm **stew**. Grandpa told stories of his life. “I learned woodworking from my own grandfather. Now, Jack, I want you to become my <abbr title="n. 门徒">**disciple**</abbr>.” Jack nodded with a big smile. Grandpa laughed and added, “Even at my age, my <abbr title="n. 新陈代谢">**metabolism**</abbr> is still fast. I can eat this whole bowl of stew and ask for more!”

Later, Jack looked at the old **cartridge** again. The **subliminal** feeling from the fair returned. He thought about the wise **forebears** and the strong **falcon**. He was beginning to feel part of something very old and very special. Lucy smiled. “Next year’s Renaissance will be even better. The rain tried to inundate our joy, but it failed.” The stew, the falcon, the parade—everything would stay in their hearts.

### 03

Lucy Lee sat at her desk, her heart beating fast. The <abbr title="n. 入学">**matriculation**</abbr> exam was in three days, and she still had to organize all her <abbr title="n. 证书">**credentials**</abbr>. Her mother said, “Lucy, make sure you have your old school reports and the letter from the headmaster. Those are important **credentials**.” Lucy nodded and opened a box. Just then, a loud buzz came from the window. A huge <abbr title="n. 一大群">**swarm**</abbr> of black bugs flew into the room! They came in like a dark cloud. “Oh no!” cried Mom. “It’s like a <abbr title="n. 瘟疫">**plague**</abbr>!” The bugs flew into Lucy’s hair, making a terrible <abbr title="n. 缠结">**tangle**</abbr>. They also got into her books and papers. It was a big mess.

Lucy ran out of the house and went down the lane to Mr. Potter’s <abbr title="n. 小屋">**cottage**</abbr>. Mr. Potter was famous for his skill with clay. His <abbr title="n. 陶器">**earthenware**</abbr> pots and vases were sold all over town. People admired his <abbr title="n. 能力">**competence**</abbr>. But today, he looked sad. “Lucy, I have a <abbr title="adj. 彻底的">**radical**</abbr> plan to stop the bugs without using poison. But some people think it’s a crazy idea. They say using poison is quick, but I think it’s <abbr title="adj. 野蛮的">**barbaric**</abbr> to kill all those little lives. That’s my <abbr title="adj. 意识形态的">**ideological**</abbr> belief. I believe in living with nature, not against it.” He showed Lucy his new machine. It used light and sound to catch bugs.

“A <abbr title="n. 消费者">**consumer**</abbr> group wants to test my machine,” Mr. Potter continued. “They need to <abbr title="v. 批准">**ratify**</abbr> it before I can sell it. But there is a big <abbr title="n. 缺陷">**defect**</abbr>. Look here.” He pointed to a metal handle. “This <abbr title="n. 曲柄">**crank**</abbr> is stuck. It won’t turn the wheel that powers the light.” Lucy was a <abbr title="adj. 务实的">**pragmatic**</abbr> thinker. She examined the crank and said, “I think we just need some oil and a strong push. Let’s fix it now.” So they went to work. But while they were busy, the door opened quietly. A mean boy named Jack tried to <abbr title="v. 实施（坏事）">**perpetrate**</abbr> a wicked joke. He took a bag full of bugs and emptied it onto Mr. Potter’s bed—right onto the <abbr title="n. 床垫">**mattress**</abbr>! The bugs crawled all over it, creating a <abbr title="adj. 大量的">**substantial**</abbr> dirty spot. When Lucy saw it, she gasped. “Who would do such a thing?”

Later, Lucy’s little brother Tim came to the cottage. He had his grammar book. “Lucy, guess what? I learned a new word: ‘they’ is a <abbr title="n. 代词">**pronoun**</abbr>!” Tim said proudly. Lucy hugged him. “That’s great, Tim. We can say, ‘They did it,’ if we don’t know who. But I think I know who tried to **perpetrate** that trick.” She looked at the muddy footprints leading from the **mattress** to the door. The **swarm** of bugs on the mattress was still moving. “We need to clean this **substantial** mess,” said Mr. Potter. Lucy’s **pragmatic** mind started working again. She used a vacuum cleaner to safely remove the bugs and took them to the woods. Meanwhile, they finished fixing the **crank**, and the **defect** was gone. The machine hummed to life.

The next day, the **consumer** group arrived. They tested the machine and were amazed. They quickly decided to **ratify** it. “Mr. Potter, your **competence** is outstanding,” they said. “Your **radical** idea is truly clever, and not **barbaric** at all. Your **ideological** belief has brought a safe solution.” The town was soon free of the bug **plague**. No more **tangle** of bugs in people’s hair. Lucy passed her **matriculation** with excellent marks, thanks to her hard work and good **credentials**. Mr. Potter gave her a lovely **earthenware** bowl as a present. And Jack? He had to say sorry and help clean the cottage. Even Tim learned all the **pronoun** words: he, she, it, they, we. Everyone learned that even a **substantial** problem can be solved with a **pragmatic** heart.

### 04

Tom was a city boy, but this summer he visited his grandfather’s <abbr title="adj. 乡村的">**rustic**</abbr> farm. The old wooden house had a certain <abbr title="n. 光泽">**gloss**</abbr> on its windows, cleaned by the recent rain. On his first day, Grandfather gave him a leather book. “Here is a <abbr title="n. 汇编">**compendium**</abbr> of the valley’s <abbr title="n. 植物群">**flora**</abbr>,” he said. “But be <abbr title="adj. 谨慎的">**wary**</abbr> – near the old well lives a <abbr title="adj. 凶猛的">**vicious**</abbr> dog, and many bushes are <abbr title="adj. 有刺的">**spined**</abbr>.”

Tom opened the book. He <abbr title="adv. 几乎">**practically**</abbr> gasped at the beautiful drawings. The book described a <abbr title="adj. 过多的">**plethoric**</abbr> variety of flowers, herbs, and trees. But it also had a note: “After heavy <abbr title="n. 降水">**precipitation**</abbr>, the path beyond the creek becomes dangerous.” Tom read about the <abbr title="adj. 费力的">**exacting**</abbr> work needed to identify each plant – you had to count petals, measure leaves, and draw with care. He felt a small <abbr title="n. 后悔">**regret**</abbr> that he didn’t know any botany.

Still, he wanted an adventure. His grandfather’s words tried to <abbr title="v. 限制">**circumscribe**</abbr> his plans, but Tom decided to go just a little way. “I can be <abbr title="adj. 能干的">**competent**</abbr> enough,” he thought. He didn’t tell anyone; he wanted his trip to be <abbr title="adj. 不引人注意的">**discreet**</abbr>. Grandfather had said he <abbr title="v. 推荐">**recommended**</abbr> staying near the garden, but Tom ignored that.

He walked past the apple trees. A cool wind blew, <abbr title="adj. 极冷的">**glacial**</abbr> against his cheeks. He soon saw a deep <abbr title="n. 裂缝">**fissure**</abbr> in the ground, like a crack made by an earthquake long ago. The **compendium** mentioned such **fissures**; they were safe if you didn’t step inside. But when Tom looked around, he felt a sudden <abbr title="n. 极度痛苦">**anguish**</abbr> building in his chest – he was already lost. Every tree looked the same, and the **flora** was too **plethoric** to use as a guide.

The sky turned gray, and **precipitation** started to fall, softly at first, then harder. Tom’s clothes were soaked. He slipped and landed near a **spined** bush; thorns cut his sleeve. His **anguish** grew into real fear. The **vicious** dog might be somewhere, he thought, and he would have no defense. He **regretted** his stubbornness deeply. This was a true <abbr title="n. 放弃">**renunciation**</abbr> of his pride – he wanted only to be home.

Time passed. Grandfather found him, carrying a lantern. “You look **practically** frozen,” he said, wrapping Tom in a coat. Tom’s **regret** poured out in words. “I should have **circumscribed** my exploring,” he said. Grandfather nodded. “Next time, be more **wary** and use the **compendium** as a guide. It will help you **circumscribe** your steps safely.”

Back at the house, Tom looked at the **flora** book again. The **gloss** on its pages seemed welcoming. He realized that being **competent** meant being patient and **discreet**. He decided to study the **plethoric** plants, one by one, even if it was **exacting** work. The **precipitation** had stopped, and the **glacial** wind was gone. The **vicious** dog was only a story, but Tom remained **wary**. He would never again ignore Grandfather’s advice. The **renunciation** of his old careless ways made him feel stronger.

## 06月13日

### 01

In a small **postwar** town, the houses were new but simple. The war had ended years ago, but money was still tight for many families.  
Timmy lived there with his old father. His family was **indigent**; they often had only bread and soup for supper. Timmy wanted to go to middle school, but his **enrolment** hit a problem. A missing paper from his old village could **impede** his start. Without it, the school might say no.  
Luckily, the school had a kind **convention**. Every autumn, they kept a week open to help poor students with late papers. So Timmy still had a chance.  

One day, something began to **intrigue** Timmy. Near the river, there was an old **landfill**. People threw trash there for years. Timmy liked to walk around it. He noticed a strange **layering** in the hill: one dark line of soil, then a line of old plastic bags, then more soil. And from the bottom, a small **trickle** of dirty water ran into the river. It was no wider than his finger.  
Then he found a heavy, dark rock in the mud. It did not look like other stones. “Could this be a **meteor**?” he thought, because he had seen pictures of space rocks in a library book.  

His science teacher, Mrs. Green, saw Timmy’s rock. “Your **calibre** for science is high,” she said. “You think like a young scientist.” Timmy felt proud. He went home and wrote a short **treatise** about the landfill and the rock. He drew the layering and the water trickle.  
Mrs. Green read his work. “I have a small **critique**,” she said. “Your ideas are good, but you should write more about the dirty water. Is it safe?”  

Soon, a big company came to the town. They wanted to use the landfill for more trash. Their **voracity** for cheap land was strong — they just wanted more and more, and they did not care about the river. The old mayor would **preach** at town meetings, “We must keep our town clean and pretty!” But he did not stop the company.  
Many people **gullibly** believed the company’s promises. “They will give us jobs,” they said, and they did not ask about the dirty water.  
But Timmy did not give up. That was one good **trait** of his: he always asked questions. He talked to people about the landfill’s danger. He told them the town had no **contingency** plan. If a big storm came, the hill could slide into the river. Nobody had a backup idea.  

One night, the weather changed **dramatically**. The sky went black, and rain fell like a waterfall. Thunder shook the windows. The old landfill could not hold the water. A pipe under it suddenly **ruptured**, and a big jet of water shot up through the trash. The hill of trash began to move. Thick, brown water spilled into the river.  
People ran to help. They remembered what Timmy had said. Because of his small treatise, they knew the trickle had already warned them. The village finally worked together to stop the trash slide. After that, the company left. The mayor stopped preaching and started doing.
