At last, Timmy’s missing paper arrived. His enrolment was complete, and he joined the middle school on a bright autumn day. Mrs. Green smiled at him. “You see? Your calibre and your never-stop trait helped the whole town.” Timmy looked at his meteor rock on his desk. A small wonder from a landfill had changed everything.

### 02

Lily lived in a big old <abbr title="n. 庄园">**manor**</abbr>. It had many rooms, but some were empty. Last month, bugs began to <abbr title="v. 侵扰">**infest**</abbr> the kitchen. Lily’s mom could not pay to fix it because her bank account had an <abbr title="n. 透支">**overdraft**</abbr>. Lily felt worried, but she had a school project to focus on.

At school, Mrs. Clark, the science teacher, had an <abbr title="n. 古怪">**eccentricity**</abbr>. She believed in an old idea called <abbr title="n. 活力论">**vitalism**</abbr>, the thought that living things have a special force. She was a <abbr title="n. 活力论者">**vitalist**</abbr> and loved to talk about it. But Lily liked real science, like <abbr title="n. 热力学">**thermodynamics**</abbr>. The class was doing a play about it. Lily and her friends formed an <abbr title="n. 合奏组">**ensemble**</abbr> to perform. They wanted to <abbr title="v. 模仿">**emulate**</abbr> a famous scientist’s speech. They used a <abbr title="n. 剧本">**transcript**</abbr> of a real lecture, but they changed some words to make it fun. Lily wrote the new lines.

The day before the show, they practiced on the old stage. The air was <abbr title="adj. 不新鲜的">**stale**</abbr>, smelling of dust. Lily’s <abbr title="n. 喉">**larynx**</abbr> hurt from shouting too much. Her voice became rough and <abbr title="adj. 粗糙沙哑">**coarse**</abbr>. She was scared she could not sing the opening song. Then she saw the costumes. They used cheap gray cloth that was also <abbr title="adj. 粗糙的">**coarse**</abbr>. The colors did not match — it looked <abbr title="adj. 不协调的">**incongruous**</abbr>, like a clown in a lab coat. Lily felt upset and went to the playground to <abbr title="v. 荡秋千">**swing**</abbr>. As she swung back and forth, she thought about her problems. She knew she needed a <abbr title="adj. 充满活力的">**vigorous**</abbr> speech to save the performance.

Then she remembered a <abbr title="n. 评审团">**jury**</abbr> of teachers would judge them. One judge was Mrs. Clark, the <abbr title="n. 活力论者">**vitalist**</abbr>. Lily’s new script had a joke about vitalism. It said, “Some people think life is magic, but thermodynamics is real.” It was a little <abbr title="adj. 不敬的">**irreverent**</abbr>, but she thought it would be funny. However, when Mrs. Clark heard it, she took it as an <abbr title="n. 冒犯">**affront**</abbr>. Her face turned red, but she said nothing.

The next day, the show began. The <abbr title="n. 合奏组">**ensemble**</abbr> walked on stage. The **stale** air now felt hot. Lily tried to speak, but her **larynx** was still sore. She took a deep breath and pushed with a **vigorous** voice — strong and loud. It worked! She talked about heat, energy, and how things like wax move from solid to an <abbr title="adj. 无定形的">**amorphous**</abbr> shape, like a soft blob. She even drew a picture of an **amorphous** blob on the board. The **jury** leaned forward. Mrs. Clark still looked hurt by the **irreverent** line, but the other judges smiled.

After the play, the **jury** discussed. Lily’s **ensemble** had done a great job, but one judge said the joke was too **coarse** and **irreverent**. Mrs. Clark argued, “This is a **legislative** matter — we need a rule about respect in plays.” That started a long talk, almost like a **legislative** meeting. Lily’s heart sank. But then a kind science teacher stood up. He said, “Science grows when we question old ideas. Lily did **emulate** the bold spirit of great scientists.” The **jury** finally gave them the first prize.

That evening, Lily went home to the **manor**. Her mom had good news: the bank fixed the **overdraft** by mistake, and a man came to stop the bug **infest**ation. The **manor** felt happy again. Lily sat on the **swing** in the garden, looking at the stars. She felt tired but glad. She whispered, “Tomorrow, I must rest my **larynx**.” Then she laughed, thinking how her **ensemble** turned an **incongruous** jumble of **coarse** cloth and **stale** air into a real show. Even a **vitalist** like Mrs. Clark had to agree that **thermodynamics** was full of surprises — and so was life.

### 03

It was a warm spring day. Our class was going on a field trip with our <abbr title="n. 教务长">**dean**</abbr>, Mr. Brown. He planned all our school trips. We met in a quiet <abbr title="adj. 住宅的">**residential**</abbr> area. Rows of houses with small gardens lined the street. The smell of grass filled the air. "Let's go, and move <abbr title="adv. 飞快地">**apace**</abbr>!" called Mr. Brown. So we walked very fast, almost running. My friend Lily nearly tripped, but we slowed down a little.

Soon we reached a big park. At the entrance stood a huge <abbr title="n. 旋转木马">**carousel**</abbr>, an old merry-go-round with painted horses. But today it was still and silent. Next to it, a wide <abbr title="adj. 休耕的">**fallow**</abbr> field spread out. No crops grew there this year. The farmer had let the land rest, so wild grasses covered the ground.

Mr. Brown led us to a tree. "This is a <abbr title="n. 酸橙">**lime**</abbr> tree," he said, picking a small green fruit. "It tastes very sour. Now, look at its leaf. This is part of <abbr title="n. 形态学">**morphology**</abbr> — the study of the shapes of living things." We looked closely at the leaf's pointed tip. Then we went to an oak tree and saw its different, wavy leaves. "**Morphology** shows that every plant has its own shape," Mr. Brown added.

Suddenly, something moved above us. An <abbr title="adj. 树栖的">**arboreal**</abbr> animal, a gray squirrel, dashed along a branch. It lived in trees and almost never came down. We also saw another **arboreal** creature, a tiny green lizard, clinging to the trunk. It was fun to watch them.

A flash of bright red caught my eye. On a fence sat a <abbr title="n. 北美红雀">**cardinal**</abbr> bird, with feathers like fire and a pointy crest. It sang a sweet song. Under a bush, we spotted a <abbr title="n. 豪猪">**porcupine**</abbr>. Its back was covered with long, sharp needles. It had a shy <abbr title="n. 性情">**disposition**</abbr> and slowly moved deeper to hide.

"Be careful," said Mr. Brown. "We must never <abbr title="v. 伤害">**victimise**</abbr> animals—never hurt them or be unkind. That is a matter of <abbr title="n. 道德">**ethics**</abbr>, the rules about right and wrong." We all nodded and kept a safe distance, just watching the **porcupine**'s needles shake as it moved.

At lunch, we sat on the grass. My rice had a little <abbr title="n. 酱油">**soy**</abbr> sauce, a salty brown sauce my mom always adds. Tom, the boy with a big smile, tried to <abbr title="v. 索取">**solicit**</abbr> cookies from everyone. He went from person to person asking, "Can I have one?" But nobody gave him any, so he laughed and ate his own sandwich.

After lunch, we heard a distant *thwack* sound. It came from a <abbr title="n. 板球">**cricket**</abbr> game on a far-away field. We could see players running with a bat and ball.

Then Mr. Brown took us to a quiet, dark pond. The water was very still. "I will tell you a local <abbr title="n. 传说">**lore**</abbr>," he said. "A lore is an old story. Long ago, a <abbr title="adj. 彗星的">**cometary**</abbr> stone—a rock from a comet—fell into this pond. After that, on some nights, the water became <abbr title="adj. 发光的">**luminous**</abbr>. It glowed with a soft blue-green light, like stars under the water. Some say the light comes back even now." We stared at the dark water, trying to imagine the **luminous** glow. I wished I could see it for real.

Near the shore, we found plants with long stems and tiny white flowers. Mr. Brown pointed. "These plants <abbr title="v. 繁殖">**propagate**</abbr> by sending seeds floating on the water. The wind and water help **propagate** them to new places." We watched little seeds drift slowly away.

It was time to go back. We walked calmly, not **apace** this time, because we were tired. The old **carousel** looked peaceful in the afternoon light. Far away, the **cricket** game was still going. Mr. Brown, our **dean**, smiled. "Nature is the best classroom," he said. And we all agreed.

## 06月14日

### 01

Lily lived in a small town. Her father was the <abbr title="n. 市长">**mayor**</abbr>. Every day, she got an <abbr title="n. 零花钱">**allowance**</abbr> of two dollars. She saved it to buy seeds.

One morning, Lily saw a black <abbr title="n. 乌鸦">**crow**</abbr> on the fence. It cawed loudly. She had a big idea for the science fair. She would need a <abbr title="n. 板条箱">**crate**</abbr> for soil. So she went to the store with a <abbr title="n. 手推车">**trolley**</abbr>.

She bought a wooden **crate** and two kinds of soil. One soil was dark and <abbr title="adj. 多孔的">**porous**</abbr>, with tiny holes that let water through. The other was heavy and not **porous**. She carried home the **crate** on the **trolley**.

At home, she had to <abbr title="v. 俯身">**stoop**</abbr> to fill the **crate** with soil. Her little brother Tom came. Tom was the <abbr title="n. 继承人">**heir**</abbr> to be **mayor** one day, but he was only six. Lily let him put seeds in the **porous** soil. The seed pack said the <abbr title="n. 可行性">**viability**</abbr> was high—most seeds would live.

Tom had <abbr title="n. 肺炎">**pneumonia**</abbr> last month. Cold air could <abbr title="v. 加剧">**exacerbate**</abbr> his cough, so Mom kept him warm. Now he felt better. Lily and Tom had a <abbr title="adj. 纯洁的">**chaste**</abbr> friendship; they never fought.

Lily covered one plant with <abbr title="adj. 不透明的">**opaque**</abbr> plastic. No light passed through the **opaque** cover. Another plant got clear glass. The sun made water drops <abbr title="v. 闪光">**glisten**</abbr> on its leaves.

She read about eyes: light hits the <abbr title="n. 视网膜">**retina**</abbr>, and the **retina** sends pictures to the brain. “That’s how we see!”

The next day, the **mayor** talked about a park where two farms <abbr title="v. 邻接">**abut**</abbr>—their fields touch. He asked about the **viability** of the plan. Lily knew **viability** meant “Can it work?”

In her garden, a <abbr title="n. 增殖">**proliferation**</abbr> of weeds grew fast. She pulled them out. “Too much **proliferation** is bad,” she thought.

Her friend Emma talked about Earth’s <abbr title="n. 构造学">**tectonics**</abbr>—big plates moving under the ground. Lily found **tectonics** cool but hard.

On fair day, she put the **crate** on the **trolley** and went to school. She had to **stoop** to check. The plant with the **opaque** cover was pale. The one in **porous** soil with light grew tall, and drops still **glisten**ed. She showed how the **retina** works.

Tom came and said, “I want to be a scientist, not just the **heir**!” Mom and the **mayor** were proud. She won first prize. A **crow** flew above again.

Lily could <abbr title="v. 设想">**envisage**</abbr> herself in a lab, studying plants or **tectonics**. All this <abbr title="v. 关于">**pertain**</abbr>s to nature. Her **allowance** was well spent. The doctor said the cold did not **exacerbate** Tom’s **pneumonia**, and their **chaste** bond stayed strong.

### 02

It was a sunny morning at Sunfield School. All the kids were excited because today they would take a <abbr title="n. 穿梭巴士">**shuttle**</abbr> to Greenpeak Park. Before going, Teacher May checked everyone’s <abbr title="n. 疫苗接种">**vaccination**</abbr> papers. “No **vaccination**, no trip,” she said with a smile.

On the **shuttle**, Tom looked at the park’s <abbr title="n. 简介手册">**prospectus**</abbr>. The **prospectus** showed a big old tree, a lake, and a bird called a <abbr title="n. 渡鸦">**raven**</abbr>. “I want to see the **raven**!” said Tom’s friend Lily.

When they arrived, the park had a lovely <abbr title="n. 氛围">**ambience**</abbr>. Soft music played, and flowers smelled sweet. The **ambience** made everyone feel happy. Near the entrance stood a wooden sign: “Greenpeak Park **Council** – est. 1980.” Mr. Hill, the park guide, said, “The <abbr title="n. 委员会">**council**</abbr> takes care of this park. I am not the boss, just a helper. The real boss is a <abbr title="n. 傀儡">**figurehead**</abbr>, an old wise owl who lives in the tree. But the owl does nothing; the **council** makes all the rules.”

Tom saw a beautiful <abbr title="n. 吊坠">**pendant**</abbr> lying on the ground. It was silver with a blue stone. He picked it up. “Look! A **pendant**!” Just then, a loud, <abbr title="adj. 模糊的">**indistinct**</abbr> noise came from the old tree. It sounded like a bird, but the words were **indistinct**. Lily said, “Maybe it’s the **raven**!” They ran to the tree. A big black **raven** sat on a branch, holding a small <abbr title="n. 绳子">**cord**</abbr> in its beak. The **cord** dangled down.

“That’s my **cord**!” cried Lily. “I use it for my kite!” The **raven** dropped the **cord** and flew away. Lily caught it. Tom showed the **pendant** to Mr. Hill. Mr. Hill looked closely. “This **pendant** is a <abbr title="n. 伪造品">**forge**</abbr>,” he said. “Someone tried to **forge** it to look like real silver. It’s not real.” Tom was disappointed.

At noon, the sun reached its highest point, the <abbr title="n. 正午">**meridian**</abbr>. Mr. Hill announced, “Now the fun will <abbr title="v. 达到高潮">**culminate**</abbr> in a big show!” The show was an <abbr title="adj. 空中的">**aerial**</abbr> act by a circus team. Acrobats swung on ropes high in the air. The **aerial** show was amazing. But before it started, there was a problem. A park worker said a <abbr title="n. 被告">**defendant**</abbr> had stolen some <abbr title="n. 糕点">**pastry**</abbr> from the kitchen. The **pastry** was for the visitors. A boy named Ben became the **defendant** because he was seen near the kitchen. Ben cried, “I didn’t take the **pastry**!”

Tom and Lily decided to have a <abbr title="n. 对话">**dialog**</abbr> with Ben. In their **dialog**, Ben said he only went to get water. Tom said, “Let’s solve this.” They found the real thief—a squirrel! The squirrel had taken a **pastry** and run up a tree. Ben was not a **defendant** anymore.

The <abbr title="adj. 原始的">**primal**</abbr> instinct of the squirrel was to hide food, like a **primal** need. Tom felt a **primal** excitement seeing it. After the show, everyone ate **pastry** under the trees. The park’s **lease** said the school could stay until sunset. Mr. Hill explained, “We have a <abbr title="n. 租约">**lease**</abbr> with the town. The **lease** lets schools use the park for free on Fridays.”

Suddenly, Lily felt something pull her hat. The **raven** was back, holding a new **cord**—this time with a real shiny **pendant**! The **raven** dropped it into Lily’s hand. “It’s a real **pendant**!” she said. The **raven** gave a happy caw. Everyone laughed. The day’s events, from the fake **pendant** to the **aerial** show, **culminated** in this surprising gift. “The **raven** is the true **figurehead** of the park!” Tom joked.

As the **shuttle** took them home, Tom thought about the **indistinct** sounds, the **council**’s kind work, and how he almost gave in to his <abbr title="n. 惰性">**inertia**</abbr> that morning. His **inertia** had told him to stay in bed, but he fought it. He was so glad he overcame that **inertia**.

### 03

The morning started with a <abbr title="n. 小测验">**quiz**</abbr>. Jack had to <abbr title="v. 沉思">**contemplate**</abbr> every question carefully. He **contemplated** so long that he almost ran out of time. After the **quiz**, he felt tired and hungry.

At lunch, Jack got a <abbr title="n. 饮料">**beverage**</abbr> in a plastic cup, but the food was <abbr title="adj. 难吃的">**unpalatable**</abbr>. The soup was cold and the bread was hard. He could <abbr title="v. 察觉到">**perceive**</abbr> that many students were unhappy. They pushed their plates away and whispered angrily.

Suddenly, Leo, a well-known talker in school, stood up. Many called him a <abbr title="n. 平民领袖">**populist**</abbr> because he always said he fought for ordinary students. “This food is **unpalatable**!” he shouted. With great <abbr title="n. 自发性">**spontaneity**</abbr>, a group of students gathered around him. They didn’t plan it; it just happened. They began calling themselves <abbr title="n. 抗议者">**protestants**</abbr> — people who protest for a better school life.

Tim, a boy who was <abbr title="adj. 半文盲的">**semiliterate**</abbr>, wanted to help. He could only read and write very simple words. He grabbed a piece of <abbr title="n. 画布">**canvas**</abbr> from the art room’s <abbr title="n. 库存">**stock**</abbr> and tried to make a sign. But Tim wrote “FIX FOD” instead of “FIX FOOD”. Everyone still loved his effort.

Soon, the principal came out. He looked angry. “This is a <abbr title="n. 违反">**breach**</abbr> of school rules!” he said. He gave an <abbr title="n. 禁令">**injunction**</abbr>: “No student is allowed to stand together in a group larger than three.” Then he called a <abbr title="n. 律师">**solicitor**</abbr> to talk about the **breach**. Teachers began to <abbr title="v. 巡逻">**patrol**</abbr> the hallways, making sure no one broke the **injunction**.

Emma, Jack’s best friend, was very <abbr title="adj. 天真的">**ingenuous**</abbr>. She believed the principal would listen if we explained nicely. Jack felt an <abbr title="adj. 不自觉的">**involuntary**</abbr> shake in his hands. His <abbr title="n. 腺体">**glands**</abbr> made his palms wet and his heart beat fast — a sign of feeling nervous. He saw some students <abbr title="v. 屈服">**succumb**</abbr> to fear and leave. They gave up and went back to class. But the **protestants** did not <abbr title="v. 投降">**surrender**</abbr>.

Jack, Emma, and Leo decided to write a polite letter instead of shouting. The **solicitor** read the letter and nodded. The principal realized that the **unpalatable** food was a real problem. He agreed to order better food and even new **beverage** machines. The **injunction** was lifted. The **protestants** cheered. Tim drew on his **canvas** a happy face. From that day, lunch became everyone’s favorite time.

And Jack learned that **spontaneity** can be powerful, but a calm talk could also fix things. He never **contemplated** leaving school again—he felt happy.

### 04

Her name was **Daisy**, just like the little white flower with a yellow center. She used to think the name was silly. But one day, she saw a real <abbr title="n. 雏菊">**daisy**</abbr> growing in the garden, and it made her smile.

One morning, **Daisy** went to water her plants. She carried a bottle that could hold exactly one <abbr title="n. 升">**litre**</abbr> of water. As she poured, she noticed a tiny green <abbr title="n. 斑点">**speck**</abbr> on a leaf. She put her face very close. It was a small bug with many legs. “A <abbr title="n. 寄生虫">**parasite**</abbr>!” she whispered. She knew it needed a <abbr title="n. 宿主">**host**</abbr> plant to live. The bug was taking food straight from the leaf. She became so <abbr title="v. 全神贯注">**engrossed**</abbr> in watching it that she did not hear her mother calling her for breakfast. Even the smell of fresh bread could not pull her away.

Her mother was a very <abbr title="adj. 宽容的">**permissive**</abbr> person. She walked outside and smiled. “You found a new friend?” she asked. She let **Daisy** keep the bug. She even gave her a shiny box to study it. The box was made of <abbr title="n. 黄铜">**brass**</abbr>, a bright gold-colored metal. Along its edge ran a narrow <abbr title="n. 条状物">**strip**</abbr> of silver. **Daisy** loved that **brass** box. From that day on, she had an <abbr title="n. 痴迷">**obsession**</abbr> with bugs. Every day she looked under rocks and leaves, hoping to find more.

Soon, **Daisy** joined an online club called the Bug Watchers <abbr title="n. 协会">**Guild**</abbr>. Members shared photos and talked about where bugs live. One older member said, “Some bugs can survive deep inside the <abbr title="n. 岩石圈">**lithosphere**</abbr>, the hard rocky shell of our Earth.” **Daisy** could not stop thinking about that. The **lithosphere** seemed so far away and so strong. How could a tiny bug live in hard rock?

Her father was a <abbr title="n. 工匠">**wright**</abbr>, a person who makes things from metal. He saw her interest and wanted to help. Using a sheet of **brass**, he built a small toy <abbr title="n. 气垫船">**hovercraft**</abbr>. It had a little fan that blew air downward, so it floated just above the ground. **Daisy** held the **hovercraft** and imagined riding it all the way down through the soil to explore the **lithosphere** herself.

One very hot afternoon, a <abbr title="n. 悲剧">**tragedy**</abbr> struck. **Daisy** forgot to move the **host** plant to the shade. The sun burned down. The plant dried up, and without its food, the little **parasite** died. **Daisy** found it in the evening and burst into tears. She held the empty **brass** box and made a strong <abbr title="n. 誓言">**vow**</abbr>. “I will never let this happen again,” she said. “I **vow** to be a better protector.”

That night, she felt a strange thing in her heart. It was a <abbr title="n. 悖论">**paradox**</abbr>. She loved the bugs more than anything, but her own actions had caused the **tragedy**. Love and harm lived together, and it made no sense.

The next morning, **Daisy** walked slowly around the <abbr title="n. 外围">**periphery**</abbr> of the garden, far from the house. Behind some old bushes, she found a small corner. It was cool and damp there, a true <abbr title="n. 避难所">**haven**</abbr> from the hot sun. Under a large leaf, she spotted a brand new **daisy** pushing up from the soil. Next to it, on a fresh green **host** plant, rested another tiny bug. The sight filled her with hope.

Suddenly, she understood something important. Sometimes, things must come into a <abbr title="n. 和谐一致">**congruence**</abbr>—a kind of perfect balance—to work well. The bug and the plant could live together if everything was just right. Too much sun could break that **congruence**, but a little shade and water could bring it back.

**Daisy** picked up her **litre** bottle, filled it with water, and gave the new **host** plant a careful drink. She remembered her **vow**. She would keep learning from the Bug Watchers **Guild**, and she would never forget the **paradox** she had felt. Maybe one day, she would even ride a real **hovercraft** and see the **lithosphere** with her own eyes. But for now, she would guard this tiny **haven** at the **periphery** of the garden.

# 第3周

## 06月15日

### 01

Tim had a bad habit: <abbr title="n. 迟到">**tardiness**</abbr>. He was often late for school. One morning, he woke up late again. He remembered the school art contest. The theme was “Magical Breakfast.” He had to make something special. He decided to make a bowl of <abbr title="n. 粥">**porridge**</abbr>. But he needed good ideas. He called his friend Sara and said, “I <abbr title="v. 委托">**delegate**</abbr> you to bring me some <abbr title="n. 麦秆">**straw**</abbr>.” Sara brought him many yellow straws.

Tim started to work. He mixed the **porridge** with food colors. Then he added the **straw**. Suddenly, the porridge looked very strange. It became a <abbr title="n. 模糊">**blur**</abbr> of colors, like a rainbow after rain. The colors moved and changed. It looked <abbr title="adj. 迷幻的">**psychedelic**</abbr>! When Tim touched it, he felt a <abbr title="n. 刺痛感">**tingle**</abbr> on his fingers, like little electric sparks. It was magic porridge!

He took the bowl to school. Many kids looked at his work. They made many <abbr title="n. 猜测">**speculations**</abbr>. Some said it was a soup; some said it was a painting. A girl named Lily came and said, “This looks so fun! But maybe you should add sugar to <abbr title="v. 引诱">**seduce**</abbr> the judges.” Tim smiled but shook his head. He didn’t want to cheat.

The judges arrived. One was a <abbr title="adj. 高雅的">**highbrow**</abbr> art teacher, Ms. White. She wore glasses and used big words. She looked at Tim’s porridge and frowned. “This is too <abbr title="adj. 肤浅的">**superficial**</abbr>,” she said. “It’s just colors and feelings. No deep meaning. The bowl looks <abbr title="adj. 劣质的">**shoddy**</abbr> — it’s not clean. And using straw is just <abbr title="n. 轻浮">**frivolity**</abbr>.” Tim’s heart sank.

But the other judge, Mr. Brown, said, “Wait. Look closer. The <abbr title="adj. 多变的">**variable**</abbr> colors show how life is always changing. The straw is not just for fun; it <abbr title="v. 巩固">**cements**</abbr> the idea that simple things can be strong. This porridge uses <abbr title="adj. 图像的">**iconographic**</abbr> pictures in our mind: straw and rainbows remind us of farms and sky.” He touched the bowl, and a soft <abbr title="n. 共鸣">**resonance**</abbr> hummed like music. The room felt a **tingle** again. Mr. Brown used his finger to <abbr title="v. 强调">**underscore**</abbr> the **straw** and said, “This work <abbr title="v. 召唤">**summons**</abbr> our childhood memories. It’s not **shoddy** at all.”

Tim felt better. He realized that his <abbr title="n. 手工">**craft**</abbr> was not just **superficial**. He didn’t need to **seduce** anyone. His **tardiness** almost made him miss the contest, but now he was proud. The **variable** opinions showed that art is for everyone. And the **resonance** of the magic porridge stayed in everyone’s heart.

### 02

Sam was a <abbr title="n. 郊区居民">**suburbanite**</abbr>. He lived in a quiet town with many trees and small houses. Last summer, he <abbr title="v. 获得">**acquired**</abbr> a love for hiking. His dad gave him an old map and a colorful <abbr title="n. 小册子">**brochure**</abbr> about a wildlife <abbr title="n. 避难所">**sanctuary**</abbr> deep in the hills. The **brochure** showed a bright <abbr title="adj. 蓝绿色的">**turquoise**</abbr> lake and an old stone <abbr title="n. 要塞">**garrison**</abbr>. Sam’s eyes grew wide. He wanted to go on a <abbr title="n. 长途跋涉">**trek**</abbr> there.

He asked his friend Leo, an <abbr title="n. 学徒">**apprentice**</abbr> guide at the local nature club. Leo nodded and said, “The **trek** is not easy. You must face the <abbr title="n. 艰苦">**rigor**</abbr> of the mountain path.” Sam also invited Mia, but she was often <abbr title="adj. 消极的">**passive**</abbr>. She just shrugged and came along without much joy.

They packed their bags. Sam’s dad <abbr title="v. 委托">**entrusted**</abbr> Leo with the first-aid kit. “Take care of this,” he said. Sam felt a deep <abbr title="n. 尊敬">**esteem**</abbr> for his dad’s trust. They set off early the next morning.

The path began gently, but soon the **rigor** kicked in. Steep rocks and thick bushes made the **trek** hard. Sam felt sweat like a cold <abbr title="n. 水珠">**bead**</abbr> on his forehead. Suddenly, a thick green vine <abbr title="v. 缠绕">**entwined**</abbr> his ankle. He fell and scraped his knee. Leo quickly took out some <abbr title="n. 胶带">**tape**</abbr> and wrapped it around the cut. “This special **tape** sticks well even on skin,” Leo explained. Later, when a strap on Mia’s bag broke, they found that the buckle from Sam’s extra bag was <abbr title="adj. 可互换的">**interchangeable**</abbr> with hers. They fixed it in seconds. “See? Parts can be **interchangeable**,” Leo smiled.

Mia complained all the way. “My legs hurt! It’s too hot!” Leo, the main guide and the <abbr title="n. 核心">**nuclei**</abbr> of our group, finally had enough. He did not shout, but he gave her a kind <abbr title="n. 指责">**censure**</abbr>. “Mia, a **passive** heart makes the journey longer. Try to see the beauty.” Mia looked down, feeling ashamed.

By noon, they reached the **sanctuary**. The **turquoise** lake sparkled under the sun. Water drops like tiny **bead**s dotted the lily pads. An old **garrison** stood on a hill. It was from long ago, built to protect the land. They explored the **garrison** walls and found a small room inside. Just then, dark clouds gathered with sudden <abbr title="n. 巨变">**upheaval**</abbr>. The weather **upheaval** brought hard rain and cold wind. They ran into the **garrison** for shelter.

Shivering, Sam started coughing. His head felt hot. Leo suspected <abbr title="n. 流感">**influenza**</abbr>. “We must keep him warm.” They built a small fire. Mia finally acted. She did not stay **passive**; she **entrusted** Sam with her own jacket. Sam **acquired** a fever, but the warmth and care made him feel safe. That night, the **nuclei** of their friendship grew stronger.

The next morning, the sun returned. The **upheaval** was over. They **trekked** back home. Sam’s dad praised them and showed **esteem** for Leo’s good leading. Mia **acquired** a new hobby: hiking. She even asked to be Leo’s **apprentice**. And Sam? He already planned the next **trek**. He kept the **brochure** and a piece of **turquoise** stone from the lake as a memory. The **rigor** of that trip made him stronger, and he will never forget how the vine **entwined** his leg or how the **bead** of sweat turned into a smile.

### 03

Tom’s Countryside Summer

Tom and Sue went to their grandpa’s farm. Grandpa was in the <abbr title="n. 海军">**navy**</abbr> long ago. He still wore a <abbr title="n. 海军">**navy**</abbr> blue cap. In the morning, he played his <abbr title="n. 小号">**trumpet**</abbr>. In the evening, he played an <abbr title="n. 手风琴">**accordion**</abbr>. Tom loved the happy sounds. Sue tried to blow the <abbr title="n. 小号">**trumpet**</abbr> and made a funny noise. Everyone laughed.

“I am the <abbr title="n. 首领">**chief**</abbr> gardener here,” Grandpa said. He was a <abbr title="n. 被提名者">**nominee**</abbr> for a big prize from the garden <abbr title="n. 委员会">**commission**</abbr>. He felt proud.

One day, Tom helped Grandpa <abbr title="v. 修剪">**trim**</abbr> the bushes. Grandpa said, “Plants need carbon <abbr title="n. 二氧化物">**dioxide**</abbr>. They take <abbr title="n. 二氧化物">**dioxide**</abbr> from the air.” Tom nodded and breathed out. Grandma gave Sue a soft <abbr title="n. 被子">**quilt**</abbr>. “Here is the <abbr title="n. 保修">**warranty**</abbr> card,” she said. Sue put the <abbr title="n. 保修">**warranty**</abbr> away in her drawer.

Sue always used her left hand. Grandpa called it <abbr title="n. 偏侧性">**laterality**</abbr>. “Your <abbr title="n. 偏侧性">**laterality**</abbr> is a gift,” he said. Sue danced in a happy <abbr title="n. 狂热">**frenzy**</abbr>. Tom joined the <abbr title="n. 狂热">**frenzy**</abbr>, and they both fell down laughing on the soft grass.

The next day, they explored beyond the <abbr title="n. 稻田">**paddy**</abbr> fields. The <abbr title="n. 稻田">**paddy**</abbr> was bright green and full of rice plants. Then the ground turned soft and <abbr title="adj. 沼泽的">**marshy**</abbr>. “Careful, it’s <abbr title="adj. 沼泽的">**marshy**</abbr> here,” Tom said. Sue’s boot sank into the mud. Tom pulled her out, and they both got a little dirty.

A small stream joined a larger river. “That’s a <abbr title="n. 支流">**tributary**</abbr>,” Grandpa said. By the <abbr title="n. 支流">**tributary**</abbr> sat a giant rock. “This <abbr title="n. 巨石">**boulder**</abbr> is very old,” Grandpa added. Tom climbed onto the <abbr title="n. 巨石">**boulder**</abbr> and pretended it was a ship. Sue sat below, drawing the <abbr title="n. 支流">**tributary**</abbr>.

A dog ran up, <abbr title="v. 咆哮">**snarling**</abbr> loudly. Grandpa smiled. “That’s Max. He’s just <abbr title="v. 咆哮">**snarling**</abbr> to say hello.” The dog wagged its tail and licked Sue’s hand.

Then Grandpa shared a secret. “A <abbr title="n. 领事">**consul**</abbr> once visited our farm. He wore a funny hat and drank tea. He told me this rock fell from the sky. It’s a <abbr title="n. 陨石">**meteorite**</abbr> from <abbr title="adj. 原始的">**primordial**</abbr> space.” Tom’s eyes opened wide. A <abbr title="adj. 原始的">**primordial**</abbr> <abbr title="n. 陨石">**meteorite**</abbr>, right here! He touched it again. “It is older than any dinosaur,” Grandpa said.

That night they lay under the <abbr title="n. 被子">**quilt**</abbr> Grandma made. Tom thought about the <abbr title="n. 陨石">**meteorite**</abbr> and the kind <abbr title="n. 领事">**consul**</abbr>. Grandpa played a soft song on the <abbr title="n. 手风琴">**accordion**</abbr>. Sue dreamed of <abbr title="adj. 原始的">**primordial**</abbr> worlds and bright stars.

The next morning, Tom helped <abbr title="v. 修剪">**trim**</abbr> the hedges again. Grandpa talked about the garden <abbr title="n. 委员会">**commission**</abbr>. “The <abbr title="n. 委员会">**commission**</abbr> is a group of experts. They will visit next week. If I win as <abbr title="n. 被提名者">**nominee**</abbr>, I’ll take you to meet the <abbr title="n. 领事">**consul**</abbr> in the city,” he promised. Tom remembered Max’s <abbr title="v. 咆哮">**snarling**</abbr> but knew he was a friendly dog.

On the last day, Sue drew the <abbr title="n. 巨石">**boulder**</abbr> and the <abbr title="n. 支流">**tributary**</abbr> with her left hand. She was proud of her <abbr title="n. 偏侧性">**laterality**</abbr>. Tom played a funny tune on the <abbr title="n. 小号">**trumpet**</abbr>. A <abbr title="n. 狂热">**frenzy**</abbr> of packing began. Sue could not find her socks. Tom looked for his compass <abbr title="n. 保修">**warranty**</abbr> card. Grandma folded the <abbr title="n. 被子">**quilt**</abbr> with care. “Goodbye, <abbr title="n. 首领">**chief**</abbr> gardener,” Tom called. Grandpa waved his old <abbr title="n. 海军">**navy**</abbr> cap.

As the car drove away, Tom looked at the <abbr title="n. 稻田">**paddy**</abbr> fields and the <abbr title="adj. 沼泽的">**marshy**</abbr> land. He would never forget the <abbr title="adj. 原始的">**primordial**</abbr> <abbr title="n. 陨石">**meteorite**</abbr>, Grandpa’s talk about <abbr title="n. 二氧化物">**dioxide**</abbr>, and the sweet songs of the <abbr title="n. 手风琴">**accordion**</abbr>.

### 04

It was a rainy evening. Tom sat at his desk with a long list of words. He tried to learn them by <abbr title="n. 死记硬背">**rote**</abbr> – reading them again and again. It felt very slow and boring. He put his head on the book and fell asleep.

Suddenly, he felt cold wind on his face. He was standing on a wide, flat land with no trees at all. Only white snow and ice went on and on. A voice said, “You are on the <abbr title="n. 冻原">**tundra**</abbr>.” Tom turned around and saw a big monkey with a long face and a red nose. “I am a <abbr title="n. 狒狒">**baboon**</abbr>,” the animal said. “I am also the <abbr title="n. 巡逻员">**patroller**</abbr> here. I walk around all day to keep this land safe.” The **baboon** wore a bright yellow hat and carried a little flashlight.

The **baboon** led Tom to a large white building with many windows. “This is a <abbr title="n. 疗养院">**sanatorium**</abbr>,” the **baboon** explained. “People come here to rest and get better when they are tired or ill.” Inside, the rooms were quiet and clean. They walked into a big hall where many people sat in a circle. They were having a <abbr title="n. 会议">**conference**</abbr> to solve a problem. A woman stood up and pointed at a man. “He took my beautiful clay <abbr title="n. 器物">**ware**</abbr>!” she cried. She was the <abbr title="n. 原告">**plaintiff**</abbr>. The **plaintiff** showed everyone a picture of the old clay pot.

The man said, “I made a <abbr title="n. 出价">**bid**</abbr> for that **ware** at a sale. I offered ten coins.” He even paid the first <abbr title="n. 分期付款">**instalment**</abbr> – two coins. But he never paid the rest. The judge’s face was serious. “My decision is <abbr title="adj. 不可撤销的">**irrevocable**</abbr>. It will not change.” The man had to give the pot back. To show the punishment in a soft way, the people <abbr title="v. 吊起">**hanged**</abbr> a big toy doll from the ceiling with a rope. Everyone laughed as the doll swung gently. As an <abbr title="n. 自然结果">**outgrowth**</abbr> of this decision, the **plaintiff** finally got her **ware** back.

Tom and the **baboon** left the building. They walked down a <abbr title="adj. 侧面的">**lateral**</abbr> path that went to the side of the main road. Near the **lateral** path, a pot of soup began to <abbr title="v. 炖">**simmer**</abbr> on a small fire. Little bubbles rose slowly, and the smell was warm and good. Tom’s leg suddenly felt very tight and painful. “Ouch! I have a <abbr title="n. 抽筋">**cramp**</abbr>!” he cried. The **baboon** quickly helped him sit down and rubbed his leg. The **cramp** slowly went away.

While sitting, Tom looked sideways. In his <abbr title="adj. 外围的">**peripheral**</abbr> vision, he noticed a dark line on the ground. He turned his head and saw a deep <abbr title="n. 裂缝">**fissure**</abbr> – a narrow crack in the earth. The **baboon** saw the **fissure** too and did a funny dance right next to it. This <abbr title="n. 引出">**elicitation**</abbr> made Tom laugh out loud. The **elicitation** was so surprising that Tom forgot his fear. “You made me laugh on purpose!” Tom said. The **baboon** nodded. “A good laugh can fix many things,” it said.

They looked into the **fissure** and found old bones and a broken clay pot. “This is <abbr title="n. 考古学">**archeology**</abbr>,” the **baboon** whispered. “We dig up and study very old things from long ago.” Tom felt like a real explorer.

Just then, Tom’s mom called him for dinner. He woke up and looked at his word list. He now understood that learning by **rote** was like digging for treasure. Each word was a little piece of **archeology** in his mind, waiting to be found.

## 06月16日

### 01

Old Herb was a <abbr title="n. 牧羊人">**herdsman**</abbr>. Every morning, he walked his sheep across the wide, windy hills. His best friend was a golden dog named Rusty. Rusty was a <abbr title="n. 寻回犬">**retriever**</abbr>, always happy to fetch sticks and bring them back. Herb had a long, curly <abbr title="n. 小胡子">**moustache**</abbr> that twitched when he smiled. Sometimes, when Rusty did something funny, Herb would <abbr title="v. 蹲下">**crouch**</abbr> down to pat him, his knees creaking a little.

One day, Rusty came running back with a strange object in his mouth. “What’s this <abbr title="n. 小发明">**gizmo**</abbr>?” Herb asked, taking it. It was a small metal box with a shiny <abbr title="n. 水晶">**crystal**</abbr> button on top. Inside, Herb could see a little <abbr title="n. 线轴">**spool**</abbr> with some thin wire wrapped around it. He pressed the **crystal** button, and a light flashed. Suddenly, a picture appeared in the air—a picture that looked as deep as a real place. It was a <abbr title="n. 立体照片">**stereophotograph**</abbr> of an old stone building. The building had tall walls made of <abbr title="n. 石造结构">**masonry**</abbr>, with dark windows.

Herb was curious. “Where did this **gizmo** come from? We must find its <abbr title="n. 来源">**provenance**</abbr>,” he said. He looked across the hills, but nothing was in his <abbr title="n. 视野范围">**ken**</abbr>. He decided to follow Rusty. The dog led him down a narrow path. They soon came to a low archway. Herb had to **crouch** again to go through it. Inside, they found an old campfire. Only a few <abbr title="n. 余烬">**ember**</abbr>s still glowed red in the ash. Next to the fire was a flat stone with a carved picture—a <abbr title="n. 石版画">**lithograph**</abbr> of a sheep. “This is very old,” Herb whispered.

Rusty began to <abbr title="v. 啃咬">**gnaw**</abbr> on a dry bone he found. Then Herb heard a soft groan. Behind a fallen piece of **masonry**, a man was lying down. His face was pale and he looked weak. “Water… please,” the man said. He was suffering from <abbr title="n. 缺乏">**deprivation**</abbr>—too long without water. Herb quickly found a small pool nearby. He gathered leaves and made a simple tool to drop water slowly into the man’s mouth. This gentle <abbr title="n. 注入">**infusion**</abbr> of water helped the man feel better.

The man pointed to his bag. “There’s a small bottle… <abbr title="n. 吗啡">**morphine**</abbr>… for the pain.” Herb opened the bag and found the bottle. He saw the label and understood it was medicine to ease pain. But he didn’t use it, because the man’s pain seemed less now. Herb said, “I <abbr title="v. 保证">**warrant**</abbr> you’ll be safe with us. But we should not stay here; the old walls might fall and <abbr title="v. 危及">**jeopardise**</abbr> our safety.”

At first, Herb did not want to take the man home because his sheep might wander. But after seeing the man’s smile, Herb made a <abbr title="n. 让步">**concession**</abbr>. “Alright, you can come with us,” he said. With Rusty’s help, they slowly walked back to the stone house. By the time they arrived, the last **ember** in the sky—the setting sun—was turning red. Rusty, tired but happy, curled up and began to **gnaw** his old bone again, his tail wagging. The next morning, Herb’s **moustache** twitched as he saw the man sitting up, feeling much better.

### 02

Tom and his dog Buddy walked along the edge of the farm. The field was **<abbr title="pp. 播种">**sown**</abbr>** with wheat a few weeks ago. In English class, Tom learned that “**<abbr title="n. 分词">**participle**</abbr>**” is a word like *sown*, the past participle of *sow*. He felt proud for remembering. Buddy wagged his tail and sniffed the fresh soil.

Near the road, they passed a big pile of **<abbr title="n. 干草">**hay**</abbr>**. It smelled sweet and dusty. Just then, a car horn blasted—a loud **<abbr title="n. 刺激">**stimulus**</abbr>** that made Buddy jump. He broke free and ran into the road. Tom screamed, but the car hit Buddy. Tom’s heart began to **<abbr title="v. 沸腾/愤怒">**seethe**</abbr>** with anger at the driver. He was so upset that his face turned red and hot.

Quickly, Tom lifted Buddy into an old wooden **<abbr title="n. 小推车">**wagon**</abbr>**. He felt **<abbr title="adj. 负有责任的">**accountable**</abbr>** because he was supposed to hold the leash. Now, he had to help his best friend. Tom and his sister Lily began to **<abbr title="v. 启程">**embark**</abbr>** on the journey to the animal hospital. They pulled the **wagon** over the bumpy path as fast as they could.

At the hospital, the vet said Buddy needed a **<abbr title="n. 输血">**transfusion**</abbr>**. This would **<abbr title="v. 使必要">**entail**</abbr>** finding a donor dog with matching blood. The vet explained the strict **<abbr title="n. 方案/制度">**regime**</abbr>** of care: Buddy must stay very still and take medicine on time. Tom listened with care, using **<abbr title="n. 谨慎">**discretion**</abbr>**—he asked when to change the bandage and how to clean the cut without hurting Buddy.

The vet gave Buddy a special medicine to **<abbr title="v. 催化/促进">**catalyze**</abbr>** the healing. “It works at the **<abbr title="adj. 分子的">**molecular**</abbr>** level to help cells repair,” she said, pointing to a tiny picture of cells. Tom didn’t understand everything, but he trusted the vet. The medicine would make Buddy’s body heal faster.

While waiting, Tom noticed a small **<abbr title="n. 裂缝">**chink**</abbr>** in the wall. He looked through the crack and saw a nurse preparing **<abbr title="n. 供应品">**provision**</abbr>**s—clean towels, dog food, and warm blankets. The vet told them, “The injury is serious, but not **<abbr title="adj. 致命的">**mortal**</abbr>**. He will live.” Tom let out a deep breath. He no longer felt the hot **seethe** of anger, only hope.

That night, Tom sat by the window and looked up at the **<abbr title="adj. 天体的/天空的">**celestial**</abbr>** lights—the moon and stars. He thought, if Buddy died, he would become a star. But the vet said it wasn’t **mortal**. Tom smiled and made a wish.

Three days later, Buddy came home. He lay in a **<abbr title="adj. 舒适温暖的">**snug**</abbr>** bed by the fireplace. Tom covered him with a soft blanket, and Buddy closed his eyes. Everything felt warm and safe.

Tom then wrote a **<abbr title="n. 请愿书">**petition**</abbr>** to put a speed bump on the road. Many neighbors signed it. The school also helped. Tom remembered the *sown* field and the **participle** lesson. That little grammar word now reminded him of life and second chances. And every time Tom saw the **wagon** or a pile of **hay**, he felt grateful and **accountable** for his dear Buddy.

### 03

Tom was staying at Grandpa’s house during summer vacation. One rainy afternoon, he had nothing to do. “Let’s explore the attic,” he thought. He climbed the creaky stairs. The floor was made of cold, white <abbr title="n. 瓷砖">**tile**</abbr>. He shivered. An old wooden <abbr title="n. 架子">**rack**</abbr> stood by the wall, with some dusty coats hanging on it. On the wall, there was a big painting. It was a beautiful <abbr title="n. 描绘">**portrayal**</abbr> of a strange village. Tom did not know what village it was.

He looked down and saw a flat, black stone. It was a <abbr title="n. 石板">**slate**</abbr>. Someone had written many signs on it. Between the signs, there were little dots and lines. Those were <abbr title="n. 标点">**punctuation**</abbr> marks. The first part of the writing looked like a story. It gave the <abbr title="n. 前因">**antecedent**</abbr> clues to find a hidden key. Tom wanted to understand. He <abbr title="adv. 故意地">**intentionally**</abbr> turned the slate over to check the back.

On the back, there was a picture of a bottle. Tom found a small bottle on a shelf. It was covered with green, slippery <abbr title="n. 黏液">**slime**</abbr>. He cleaned it and opened the cap. A sweet smell filled his nose. Then his head felt heavy and sleepy. He knew it was a <abbr title="n. 麻醉剂">**narcotic**</abbr>. He closed it fast.

Suddenly, his foot hurt. His new shoes rubbed his heel. A small, painful bubble grew there. It was a <abbr title="n. 水泡">**blister**</abbr>. He took off his shoe and sat down. His stomach grumbled. He felt very hungry. He started to <abbr title="v. 渴望">**crave**</abbr> something sweet and juicy. The fruit he wanted most was a <abbr title="n. 桃子">**peach**</abbr>—its yellow and pink skin, its soft, sweet taste.

He stood up and saw two thin swords on the wall. They were for <abbr title="n. 击剑">**fencing**</abbr>. In this sport, two people try to touch each other with the tip of a sword. Then, the old lamp in the corner flickered. It gave off <abbr title="adj. 迷幻的">**psychedelic**</abbr> light. Pink, green, and blue circles swirled on the wall. The colors danced and made him feel dizzy.

Tom looked at the slate again. He noticed a new <abbr title="n. 范例">**paradigm**</abbr>. To read the message, you had to read each word backward. So “emoh” was “home”. He also found a notebook with hard <abbr title="n. 术语">**terminology**</abbr>, like “cipher” and “key”. He could not understand those words.

Suddenly, an <abbr title="n. 猫头鹰">**owl**</abbr> hooted outside the window. Its big eyes stared at him. Then, a terrible smell came from a corner. It was <abbr title="adj. 讨厌的">**loathsome**</abbr>, like old, spoiled milk. Tom covered his nose and ran downstairs.

Grandpa was in the kitchen. “You found my old things!” he said. Tom told him everything. Then Tom pointed at the painting. “What is that village?” he asked. “That’s a <abbr title="n. 印第安村庄">**pueblo**</abbr>,” Grandpa said. “Native Americans built it long ago. The houses are made of clay and stick together like a big family.”

Grandpa smiled and gave Tom a big bowl of peaches. “Here you go. You can take more if you want. I am very <abbr title="adj. 慷慨的">**liberal**</abbr> with fruit from my garden.” Tom ate a peach and laughed. His blister still hurt a little, but the sweet peach made him happy. He decided to come back to the attic tomorrow. He wanted to learn more about the **pueblo** in the painting.

### 04

Lily was a girl from an <abbr title="adj. 富裕的">**affluent**</abbr> family. Her house had a big garden and a swimming pool. She always wore clean, pretty dresses. When she saw children from poor parts of the city, she felt <abbr title="n. 鄙视">**disdain**</abbr>. She did not want to play with them. Her smile at them was often <abbr title="adj. 做作的">**contrived**</abbr>. It was a fake, polite smile.

At school, Lily joined a <abbr title="n. 联盟">**federation**</abbr> that helped people in need. The **federation** decided to take students to a <abbr title="n. 贫民窟">**slum**</abbr>. A big flood had hit that area, and many families had to leave their homes. This sudden <abbr title="n. 迁移">**displacement**</abbr> pushed them into a dirty, crowded place. Lily’s teacher said, “It is <abbr title="adj. 首要的">**paramount**</abbr> that you go and see how others live. Kindness is **paramount**.” Lily did not agree, but she had to go.

The bus stopped, and Lily stepped out. The **slum** looked <abbr title="adj. 荒凉的">**bleak**</abbr>. Gray tents stood in the mud. The air was cold. No flowers grew there. She saw some <abbr title="n. 家畜">**livestock**</abbr> —thin goats and a few chickens. A goat walked past her, and Lily jumped back. She thought, “This place is so **bleak**.”

Then she heard a soft, sweet sound. It was a <abbr title="n. 小提琴">**fiddle**</abbr>. A boy not much taller than her was playing the **fiddle** under a tent. The music had a special <abbr title="n. 魅力">**charm**</abbr>. It made her forget the **bleak** camp. She walked to the tent. The boy’s name was Leo. His father was <abbr title="v. 拧">**wring**</abbr>ing water from a cloth. He put the cool cloth on a woman’s head. Leo said, “My mother has <abbr title="n. 糖尿病">**diabetes**</abbr>. She feels very tired.” Lily asked, “What helps her?” Leo answered, “She needs a <abbr title="n. 分子">**molecule**</abbr> called insulin. But here, we cannot get that **molecule** easily.”

Leo told Lily that his family used to be <abbr title="adj. 游牧的">**nomadic**</abbr>. “We moved with our animals from place to place,” he said. “But the flood took our land. Now we are stuck in this **slum**.” Lily listened. She felt something change inside her. She asked Leo to play the **fiddle** again. As he played, Lily felt a real smile on her face for the first time in weeks.

Suddenly, a man pushed through the crowd. He had a <abbr title="n. 匕首">**dagger**</abbr> in his hand. He was a <abbr title="n. 威胁">**menace**</abbr>. “Listen to me!” he shouted. “This woman has a <abbr title="adj. 传染的">**contagious**</abbr> sickness! If you stay near her, you will all get sick!” People cried in fear. The man was <abbr title="adj. 恶意的">**malignant**</abbr>. He wanted to scare them away so he could take their tents.

Leo stepped forward. “That is not true,” he said. “My mother’s diabetes is not **contagious**. No one can catch it!” Lily added, “He is right. A **molecule** problem in her body causes diabetes. It is not like a cold.” The crowd began to doubt the man. He waved his **dagger**, but a few strong men from the camp grabbed him. The **menace** dropped his **dagger** and ran away.

After that day, Lily and Leo became <abbr title="adj. 亲密的">**intimate**</abbr> friends. She no longer had **disdain** for the **slum** children. She saw that Leo’s **fiddle** music was true and beautiful, while her old smiles were **contrived**. She talked to her school **federation** about the **displacement** and the hard life there. The **federation** promised to send medicine, including the insulin **molecule**, and to help rebuild homes.

Lily understood that helping others was **paramount**. She kept thinking about the **charm** of the **fiddle** and the **nomadic** boy who taught her so much. Love and kindness, she learned, were far more **paramount** than money or a big house.

## 06月17日

### 01

Tom picked up his <abbr title="n. 小提琴">**fiddle**</abbr> and walked into the music room. Today was a big day: the school orchestra would <abbr title="v. 排练">**rehearse**</abbr> for the spring concert. Mr. Hill, the conductor, stood in front and gave everyone a <abbr title="n. 命令">**mandate**</abbr>. “You must check the new music <abbr title="n. 目录">**catalogue**</abbr> before we start. All songs are listed there.” He called his <abbr title="n. 下属">**underling**</abbr>, a boy named Billy, to bring the **catalogue** from the office. Billy was always the **underling** who helped with small tasks.

Tom opened his copy of the **catalogue** and turned the pages. He loved playing his **fiddle**, but his fingers felt strange. After hours of practice, his left hand had a bad <abbr title="n. 肿胀">**swelling**</abbr>. The **swelling** made his skin tight and hot. A dull pain began to <abbr title="v. 抽痛">**throb**</abbr> in his thumb. He shook his hand, but the **throb** did not stop.

While they **rehearse**d, someone tried to <abbr title="v. 暗中破坏">**undermine**</abbr> the practice. Tom’s music sheet disappeared from his stand. Without it, he could not play. Who would **undermine** their hard work? Later that day, Billy was found in the nurse’s room with a high fever. In his <abbr title="n. 神志不清">**delirium**</abbr>, he talked in a strange way. “I took the sheet… I wanted to be first fiddle,” he said. His words showed he was a <abbr title="n. 叛徒">**traitor**</abbr>. The <abbr title="n. 行为">**deed**</abbr> of stealing was wrong, but Billy was sick and not fully awake. The next morning, Billy’s fever went down, and his **delirium** was gone. He remembered his **deed** and cried, “I am sorry.” Tom forgave him, saying the **deed** was done but they could start again.

Mr. Hill made a <abbr title="adj. 事实的">**factual**</abbr> report about what happened. He said he would not punish Billy harshly. Instead, he decided to <abbr title="v. 隔离">**sequester**</abbr> Billy in a quiet room for a few days to rest and think. After Billy was **sequester**ed for three days, he returned to the orchestra with a new smile. “We all make mistakes,” Mr. Hill said. “A <abbr title="n. 开除">**dismissal**</abbr> from the group is too much.” So there was no **dismissal** of Billy. The **mandate** now was to work as a team.

After the **rehearse**al, Tom’s finger **throb**bed again. He went to see the nurse. On the way, he fell on the playground and got a <abbr title="n. 擦伤">**graze**</abbr> on his knee. The **graze** bled a little. The nurse cleaned the **graze** and put a bandage on it. She also checked his **swelling** finger. “You need to rest your **fiddle** practice,” she said.

In science class that week, they learned about <abbr title="n. 染色体">**chromosome**</abbr>s. The **chromosome**s carry information from parents to children. The teacher said, “Write a **factual** paragraph about **chromosome**s.” Tom liked **factual** writing because he just wrote what he knew.

After school, Tom talked with Mr. Hill. The teacher’s <abbr title="n. 业余爱好">**avocation**</abbr> was gardening. He had a piece of <abbr title="adj. 可耕的">**arable**</abbr> land outside town where he grew vegetables. Gardening was more than an **avocation**; it was his joy. “My garden is my <abbr title="n. 休养地">**resort**</abbr>,” he said. “When I feel tired, I go to that **arable** land and dig in the dirt. It is the best **resort** from a busy week.” He laughed and told Tom not to worry about the <abbr title="n. 浪费">**wastage**</abbr> of time on bad things. “The **wastage** of paper from the lost sheet is small. We can print a new **catalogue**.”

Tom felt better. He knew that even a **traitor** can learn and change. He looked at his **fiddle** and smiled, ready to **rehearse** again.

### 02

It was a Saturday morning. Mom wanted to cook spaghetti. She looked in the kitchen, but there was no <abbr title="n. 大蒜">**garlic**</abbr>. She called Tom. “Tom, please go to the <abbr title="n. 地窖">**cellar**</abbr> and bring some **garlic**.”

Tom walked to the cellar door. He felt a bit scared. The cellar was dark and <abbr title="adj. 冷的">**chilly**</abbr>. He took a deep breath to get his <abbr title="n. 勇气">**nerve**</abbr>. Then he opened the door. Cold air hit his face. He went down the steps.

In the cellar, Tom saw old boxes. On one shelf, he found a <abbr title="n. 木刻画">**woodcut**</abbr>. It was a piece of wood with a picture carved into it. The **woodcut** showed a <abbr title="n. 圣人">**saint**</abbr>. The **saint** had a kind face. Tom touched it. He also felt a <abbr title="n. 一丝">**tinge**</abbr> of dust in the air—just a little bit. Then he found the **garlic** and took some. He went back upstairs. Mom smiled.

In the living room, Dad was fixing a bookshelf. The bookshelf was <abbr title="adj. 歪斜的">**skew**</abbr>. It leaned to the left. Dad said, “This shelf is not <abbr title="adj. 垂直的">**plumb**</abbr>. I must make it straight.” Dad had a <abbr title="n. 夹板">**splint**</abbr> on his right leg. He broke his leg last week. The **splint** was white and stiff.

Dad looked out the window. The sky had a **tinge** of gray. He said, “We planned to go to the <abbr title="n. 太平洋">**Pacific**</abbr> beach today. But the weather is getting **chilly** and cloudy. And there is a problem at my factory. The workers plan a <abbr title="n. 罢工">**walkout**</abbr>. So I need to join a <abbr title="n. 谈判">**negotiation**</abbr>.” Tom asked, “What is a **walkout**?” Dad said, “It is when workers stop working to ask for something. And **negotiation** is like a talk to find an answer.” Mom came in. Her face showed a **tinge** of sadness. She said, “Oh, so we must <abbr title="v. 推迟">**postpone**</abbr> our beach trip.” **Postpone** meant put off for later. Dad nodded.

Tom thought the words were a bit <abbr title="adj. 中级的">**intermediate**</abbr>—not too easy, not too hard. He understood because Dad explained well.

The family lived in a <abbr title="adj. 半干旱的">**semiarid**</abbr> area. It did not rain much. This climate was <abbr title="adj. 相似的">**akin**</abbr> to a desert—very dry. But today, it felt **chilly** and damp. The dog, Bingo, came wagging his tail. He tried to <abbr title="v. 抢夺">**swipe**</abbr> a piece of bread from the table. He used his paw to snatch it. Tom stopped him. But Bingo gave him <abbr title="adj. 大量的">**profuse**</abbr> licks—so many licks that Tom laughed. **Profuse** meant a lot, like a shower of kisses.

Tom’s sister, Lily, played the piano. She played an **intermediate** piece. It was not a beginner song, but also not the hardest. Tom went back to the **cellar** to put away the **woodcut**. He held it carefully. He could <abbr title="adv. 可触摸地">**tangibly**</abbr> feel the old carving lines under his fingers. It felt real and solid.

Later, Dad came back from the **negotiation**. The **walkout** was over. They reached an agreement. The beach trip was no longer **postpone**d. The next day, the sun came out. Even in a **semiarid** land, a sunny day was common. The family packed food. Mom put **garlic** bread in the basket. Dad’s leg felt better, but he still had the **splint**. Tom carried the **woodcut** to show at the beach, in case they saw something **akin** to it near the **Pacific**. The **Pacific** Ocean was big and blue. Tom felt his **nerve** was strong now. He was not scared of the **cellar** anymore. With a little **nerve**, you can face **chilly** cellars, **skew** shelves, and even **intermediate** problems.

### 03

Tom and his family planned a special trip. Dad spread out a colorful map. “Look at this <abbr title='adj. 地形学的'>**topographical**</abbr> map,” he said, pointing to hills and rivers. “This will be our <abbr title='adj. 预期的'>**prospective**</abbr> adventure next weekend.” Mom looked at a candy wrapper on the floor with <abbr title='n. 轻蔑'>**contempt**</abbr>. She hated litter. She then packed some medicine for <abbr title='n. 时差'>**jetlag**</abbr>, because the long train ride might make them feel strange, like crossing time zones. Tom noticed a book on Dad’s desk: “Introduction to <abbr title='n. 超心理学'>**parapsychology**</abbr>.” Dad explained it was a science about mysteries, like mind reading and ghosts. Tom thought it was weird but interesting.

On Saturday morning, they hurried to the station. A huge, black <abbr title='n. 火车头'>**locomotive**</abbr> waited, puffing steam. Tom loved watching the smooth <abbr title='n. 移动'>**locomotion**</abbr> of the wheels as the train started. The carriage was hot and crowded. His little sister became <abbr title='adj. 易怒的'>**irritable**</abbr>; she whined and kicked the seat. Mom gave her a candy to calm her down.

After three hours, they arrived at the old castle. The first thing they saw was a wide <abbr title='n. 护城河'>**moat**</abbr> filled with green water. An old guide met them. He spoke <abbr title='adv. 恭敬地'>**reverently**</abbr>, in a soft voice, as if the stones were alive. He pointed to a big model of a sailing ship. “This is the famous <abbr title='n. 船难'>**shipwreck**</abbr> story of Captain Grey. In 1720, his ship sank right off the coast. He managed to save his treasure chest and hid it in a secret <abbr title='n. 金库'>**vault**</abbr> under the castle.” Tom’s eyes grew wide. The guide led them near a heavy iron door. A large red sign said DANGER – DO NOT ENTER. The guide explained, “This sign tries to <abbr title='v. 阻止'>**deter**</abbr> people from going in. The old **vault** is not safe.”

Then the guide told more stories. “Years ago, a young boy broke into the **vault** to steal the gold. He was caught by a guard. The court gave him a <abbr title='n. 定罪'>**conviction**</abbr> for theft. But because he was only fourteen, the judge gave him <abbr title='n. 缓刑'>**probation**</abbr> — he must not break any law for two years, and he had to work in the castle gardens.” The family listened closely. The guide also pointed to a lovely <abbr title='n. 小树林'>**grove**</abbr> of apple trees nearby. “Last summer, a visitor cut down one of these trees for firewood. That was a <abbr title='n. 侵权行为'>**tort**</abbr> — a wrong act against someone’s property. He had to pay a lot of money to the castle.” Tom frowned; he would never hurt a tree.

After the stories, the family walked to the **grove** for a picnic. Tall, strong stone columns rose from the grass to <abbr title='v. 支撑'>**underpin**</abbr> the castle wall. “These columns support the whole building,” Dad said. Tom touched the cold stone and felt safe. Then they enjoyed local <abbr title='n. 菜肴'>**cuisine**</abbr>: hot meat pies, apple tarts, and fresh milk. The food was simple but delicious.

“What a wonderful <abbr title='n. 短途旅行'>**excursion**</abbr>,” Mom said. Dad nodded. “Our next **prospective** trip could be to the mountains.” Mom saw a tourist throw a napkin into the **moat**. Her face showed **contempt** again. She picked it up and put it in a bin.

On the train back, Tom borrowed Dad’s **parapsychology** book. He read a chapter about telekinesis — moving objects with your mind. “That is a kind of **locomotion** without touching,” Dad chuckled. Tom tried to move his water bottle with his eyes, but nothing happened. The long day made them all feel like they had **jetlag**. As the **locomotive** whistle echoed, Tom leaned back, full of memories.

### 04

Tom loved <abbr title="n. 橄榄球">**rugby**</abbr>. Every day after school, he ran to the field. The **rugby** club was an <abbr title="n. 附属机构">**affiliate**</abbr> of the school. But one Tuesday, when Tom got home, his mother was lying on the sofa. She had a <abbr title="n. 偏头痛">**migraine**</abbr>. The bright lights hurt her head. But her <abbr title="adj. 母亲的">**maternal**</abbr> love was strong. She gave Tom a sandwich and said, “Go to practice. I’ll be okay.” Tom hugged her.

The next day, the coach called a meeting. “I have bad news,” he said. “Our trip to <abbr title="n. 大洋洲">**Oceania**</abbr> is in danger of <abbr title="n. 终止">**termination**</abbr>. We don’t have enough money. Not all members paid their <abbr title="n. 会费">**dues**</abbr>. We need a lot more.”

Tom felt sick. He and his friend Leo walked behind the tall, green <abbr title="n. 树篱">**hedge**</abbr> near the playground. They began to <abbr title="v. 密谋">**conspire**</abbr>. “We must stop the termination,” Tom whispered. “Let’s make a plan.”

Leo had an idea. “We can form a fundraising group. But it should be <abbr title="adj. 两院制的">**bicameral**</abbr>, like a real government! One group makes a news sheet, and the other group tells people on the street. Two groups, but one goal.” Tom loved it.

They named their news sheet the “Rugby <abbr title="n. 公报">**Gazette**</abbr>.” The first group wrote stories about **Oceania** and drew pictures. The second group decided to <abbr title="v. 纠察">**picket**</abbr> outside the school gate, holding big signs that said “Save Our Trip!”

But they still needed money. Tom’s mother could not <abbr title="v. 偿还">**reimburse**</abbr> him for the trip. She still had a **migraine** and couldn’t work extra hours. Yet her **maternal** support was clear: she gave him old bottles to sell.

Then the team’s new **bicameral** system worked well. The **Gazette** group wrote about a local shop owner who might help. Tom and Leo went to see him. “We need an <abbr title="n. 拨款">**appropriation**</abbr> from you,” Tom said. “Just a small amount to fund the trip.”

The shop owner smiled. “I will give you money, but you must sign an <abbr title="n. 契约">**indenture**</abbr>. It says you will work for me for three Saturdays, cleaning the back room. Agreed?” Tom and Leo signed it at once. “<abbr title="adv. 从此以后">**Henceforth**</abbr>, you work hard and earn the **appropriation**,” the man said.

That evening, Tom and his friends worked together. The **picket** group got five more members. Even the school’s **affiliate** clubs helped. Tom learned a lot about <abbr title="n. 纪律">**discipline**</abbr>. Every day they followed the plan without fail.

The next Saturday, Tom woke up early. The sky was still dark, and a beautiful <abbr title="adj. 新月形的">**crescent**</abbr> moon was in the sky. He walked to the shop, remembering the **indenture** he had signed. He swept and cleaned with **discipline**. His **maternal** mother, though her **migraine** was a little better, brought him a hot drink.

Finally, the **dues** were all collected, and the **appropriation** from the shop was earned. The coach announced: “The **termination** is off! We are going to **Oceania**!” Everyone cheered.

On the day of departure, the team planted an <abbr title="n. 桤木">**alder**</abbr> tree near the **hedge** where Tom and Leo had **conspired**. The little tree would grow tall, they said.

The trip to **Oceania** was amazing. They saw blue water and strange fish. The guide warned them: “Stay close. There are <abbr title="n. 捕食者">**predator**</abbr> fish near the rocks. They are not friendly.” Tom thought about the **predator** and remembered how they had worked together to avoid the **termination**. Just like that, working together saved them.

Back home, Tom’s mother felt much better. Her **migraine** was gone. She showed her **maternal** pride with a big hug. Tom looked at the **alder** tree through the window. The **crescent** moon was in the sky again. He felt happy. He had learned that with a good plan, **discipline**, and a little **conspiring** in secret, you can do great things.

### 01

One hot summer day, Lily and Tom played in Grandpa’s backyard. They dug a hole. Tom’s shovel hit a box. The box had a wax <abbr title="n. 封蜡">**seal**</abbr> on it. Lily and Tom took out the papers and began to <abbr title="v. 仔细阅读">**pore**</abbr> over them. They **pored** over every word and picture. One paper showed a big <abbr title="n. 四边形">**quadrilateral**</abbr>. It was a map of a field with four sides. A dot marked the <abbr title="n. 顶点">**zenith**</abbr> of a hill. “We must find that spot,” Tom said.

Lily sighed. “This feels like <abbr title="n. 官僚作风">**bureaucracy**</abbr>!” She remembered the town <abbr title="n. 条例">**ordinance**</abbr>: “No trading without a form.” They had to go to an office. The clerk gave them a long paper. “This is **bureaucracy**,” Tom said. The **bureaucracy** made them wait and wait. Finally, they got permission. They helped Mr. Brown carry apples to the **broker**. At the market, Tom saw a sign: “Water <abbr title="n. 节省">**retrenchment**</abbr> – save water!” He learned that **retrenchment** means saving.

After the trade, Mr. Brown said, “You are <abbr title="adj. 谨慎的">**prudent**</abbr> kids. You did everything carefully.” Then they walked up the hill. On the way, Lily sniffed a flower. But she was <abbr title="adj. 过敏的">**hypersensitive**</abbr> to pollen; she sneezed many times. The **hypersensitive** girl had to stay away from the flowers.

At the **zenith** of the hill, they found an old dying tree. Lily touched its dry bark. “Everything faces <abbr title="n. 必死性">**mortality**</abbr> one day,” she said softly. They felt a little sad. Then Tom found a stone with words carved on it. The stone was dirty, but Lily brushed away the mud. Suddenly, she got an <abbr title="n. 启发">**illumination**</abbr>. “Look! Grandpa wrote this: ‘I <abbr title="v. 播种">**sowed**</abbr> these seeds long ago. Do not <abbr title="v. 抛弃">**forsake**</abbr> them.’ So we must not **forsake** nature.”

The puzzle gave their brains a nice <abbr title="n. 刺激">**stimulation**</abbr>. It was like a fun game. The warm feeling of **stimulation** stayed with them. They decided to be **prudent**: they carefully picked up the seed bag and took it home. The **illumination** from the old stone helped them understand. They planted the seeds and promised never to **forsake** the garden. The broken **seal** no longer mattered—the real treasure was the lesson about life and care.

### 02

The Secret Explorer Clique

Lily, Max, and Tom were a secret explorer <abbr title="n. 小圈子">**clique**</abbr>. They loved finding hidden places near the sea. One sunny morning, Lily had a <abbr title="n. 直觉">**gut**</abbr> feeling that the weather might change. So the three friends took a small boat and rowed through a narrow <abbr title="n. 运河">**canal**</abbr>. The waterway cut between two tall grassy walls. They decided to <abbr title="v. 存放">**stow**</abbr> their picnic basket and a soft blanket under the boat seat. “Everything must stay dry,” said Tom.

Soon they reached Starfish <abbr title="n. 半岛">**peninsula**</abbr>, a long finger of land that reached into the blue sea. The beach was <abbr title="adj. 原始的">**pristine**</abbr>—clean white sand with no footprints anywhere. The water was so clear they could see tiny fish.

The tide was starting to <abbr title="v. 退潮">**ebb**</abbr>. The sea pulled back, leaving wet sand and small shells. They found little <abbr title="n. 蛤蜊">**clam**</abbr> shells half-buried in the sand. The round gray shells felt cool in their hands.

Lily dug in the sand and found an old metal box. The metal began to <abbr title="v. 失去光泽">**tarnish**</abbr>. It was covered with dark spots. “This used to be shiny silver,” she said sadly. Tom picked up the box and said, “I am an <abbr title="n. 拥护者">**advocate**</abbr> for the sea. We should leave things where they are.” He always spoke up for animals and nature.

Max got excited and jumped from a big rock, but he hit his <abbr title="n. 胫部">**shin**</abbr> on a sharp stone. “Ow! My lower leg hurts!” he cried. After a few minutes, his leg felt <abbr title="adj. 僵硬的">**stiff**</abbr>. It was hard for him to bend his knee, so he sat down on a soft rock.

Nearby, they found a small <abbr title="n. 天鹅绒">**velvet**</abbr> bag. It felt as soft as a kitten's fur. Someone had taken time to <abbr title="v. 刺绣">**embroider**</abbr> a golden star on it. Inside the bag was a piece of paper with a short <abbr title="n. 诗节">**verse**</abbr>, a little poem:

“The waves that touch the sandy shore,
Will sing and dance forevermore,
This great blue home, so wide and deep,
The <abbr title="adj. 永恒的">**eternal**</abbr> sea its secrets keep.”

“It means the sea will always be here,” whispered Lily.

Suddenly, a <abbr title="n. 旋风">**whirlwind**</abbr> rushed across the beach. A fast spinning wind lifted sand and leaves high into the air. Panic began to <abbr title="v. 接着发生">**ensue**</abbr>. Their hats flew away and sand stung their eyes. “Run to the cave!” shouted Tom. They ran with stiff legs and beating hearts.

The wind died after a few minutes. The beach was quiet again. They saw a seal resting on a rock. Tom said softly, “Long ago, people tried to <abbr title="v. 驯养">**domesticate**</abbr> these seals, to make them pets. But they belong to the wild sea.”

When the sun came out, the wet sand sparkled. It looked like a <abbr title="n. 万亿">**trillion**</abbr> tiny lights, more than anyone could count. The three friends sat and watched, happy they came.

The tide came back, so they got into the boat and rowed home. Lily looked at the soft bag and smiled. The sea had given them a perfect day.

### 03

Jack woke up with heavy <abbr title="n. 惰性">**inertia**</abbr>. He did not want to move. But his <abbr title="n. 猎犬">**hound**</abbr>, Max, licked him until he got up. He put on his <abbr title="n. 拖鞋">**slipper**</abbr> and went down.

On the path, he met Lily. She also looked for the **stagecoach**. “Let me <abbr title="v. 使认识">**acquaint**</abbr> you with its story,” she said. They became friends fast and walked with Max the **hound**.

Jack reached in and pulled out a shiny <abbr title="n. 牡蛎">**oyster**</abbr> shell and a white stone. “This stone is a <abbr title="n. 硅酸盐">**silicate**</abbr>,” Lily said. “My science book says it is a common mineral.” The **oyster** shell felt smooth. There was a dark spot on it. “It looks like <abbr title="n. 病理学">**pathology**</abbr> on the shell,” she said.

A big boy jumped out. “Give it to me!” He pushed Jack. It was <abbr title="n. 骚扰">**harassment**</abbr>. Jack tried to <abbr title="v. 抵挡">**fend**</abbr> him off. Max barked, and a quick <abbr title="n. 扭打">**scuffle**</abbr> started. Jack lost a **slipper**. The boy was <abbr title="adj. 专制的">**autocratic**</abbr> and mean.

Lily ran to <abbr title="v. 集结">**rally**</abbr> other kids. “Help!” she shouted. Five children came. They all <abbr title="v. 认为">**deem**</abbr> the boy a bully, and he fled.

Jack put his **slipper** on. His morning **inertia** was gone. He looked at the **oyster**. “Maybe the spot is not real **pathology**,” he said. They used the **vinegar** to clean off **contaminant** from the **stagecoach**.

Walking home, Jack took the **tinplate** box and decided to **transcribe** his adventure note to hide it again. What a **weird** and wonderful day! And he was glad Lily came to **acquaint** him with the forest’s secrets. Even Max the **hound** wagged his tail.

### 04

Our school had an old, empty corner behind the playground. It was full of <abbr title="n. 垃圾">**litter**</abbr>—old paper, plastic bags, and broken toys. One Monday, our teacher Ms. Lee smiled and said, “We got a <abbr title="n. 补贴">**subsidy**</abbr> from the city! Money to make this corner beautiful.” We all cheered. But then came a big <abbr title="n. 争议">**controversy**</abbr>. Half the class wanted a small soccer field. The other half wanted a flower garden. I <abbr title="v. 渴望">**yearned**</abbr> for a garden. Every night, I drew pictures of red tulips and yellow daisies. I wanted a place where butterflies could visit.

We <abbr title="v. 聚在一起">**huddled**</abbr> at lunch and talked. We voted. The garden won by two votes! The next day, we started the <abbr title="n. 清除">**elimination**</abbr> of all the rubbish. We wore gloves and filled three big bags. After that, we <abbr title="v. 擦干净">**wiped**</abbr> an old bench. We used wet cloths and scrubbed hard. The bench looked new. We also fixed a broken wall with <abbr title="n. 灰泥">**plaster**</abbr>. We mixed white powder with water, and it turned into a soft paste. We pushed the **plaster** into the holes and made it smooth. It dried strong.

The soil was sad and gray. Ms. Lee explained, “This soil has a kind of <abbr title="n. 营养不良">**dystrophy**</abbr>. It needs food.” We dug out the old dirt and added fresh, black soil. We chose <abbr title="adj. 陆生的">**terrestrial**</abbr> plants—ones that grow in land, not in water. Then we tried something exciting: <abbr title="n. 杂交">**crossbreeding**</abbr>. We took a tiny paintbrush and moved yellow pollen from a lily to a red rose, hoping for a new color. It was like being a plant wizard.

I am the shortest kid in my grade. My <abbr title="n. 身高">**stature**</abbr> is only 120 centimeters. But when we found a huge stone in the garden, I thought of a clever <abbr title="n. 巧妙移动">**manoeuvre**</abbr>. I found a long wooden pole and a small rock. I pushed the pole under the big stone and used the small rock as a fulcrum. We all pushed down, and the stone rolled away! Everyone clapped. My friend said, “Your small **stature** holds a giant brain.”

On the weekend, a <abbr title="n. 大队人马">**battalion**</abbr> of parents arrived, like a happy army. They carried <abbr title="adj. 各种各样的">**miscellaneous**</abbr> things: shovels, a wheelbarrow, seeds, and even a birdhouse. We worked fast. But I had a quiet <abbr title="n. 顾虑">**scruple**</abbr>. I looked at the small wild grasses we dug up. They were once homes for ants and ladybugs. So I put them carefully into a pot and kept them safe. That small act ended my **scruple**.

Then began the waiting. I ran to the garden every morning. I stood in <abbr title="n. 悬念">**suspense**</abbr>, staring at the brown earth. Day after day, nothing changed. The **suspense** grew stronger inside me. Would any seed wake up? One cloudy morning, I saw a thin green stem! I shouted so loud that the whole school heard.

Soon, the garden exploded with colors—red, pink, yellow, and a new peach color from our **crossbreeding**. It had a wild, happy <abbr title="n. 美感">**aesthetic**</abbr>. The flowers didn’t stand in straight lines; they danced together. That natural **aesthetic** made everyone smile. A low white fence <abbr title="v. 环绕">**hemmed**</abbr> the garden, like a hug around the flowers. Inside, it felt like a tiny fairy world.

That project left an <abbr title="adj. 持久的">**abiding**</abbr> mark on my heart. I still remember the **subsidy** that gave us a chance, and the **controversy** we solved by voting. I remember that my small **stature** didn’t stop me. The **abiding** truth is simple: when you love something and work hard, you can grow beauty from nothing.

### 01

While Dad was <abbr title="v. 割草">**mowing**</abbr> the lawn, Sam went to a garage sale. He found an old radio. It was dusty but still <abbr title="adj. 完整的">**intact**</abbr>. Sam said, "I can <abbr title="v. 抢救">**salvage**</abbr> this old radio and make it work again."

When Sam turned on the radio, he heard only <abbr title="n. 静电噪音">**static**</abbr>. Pzzzt! "No music, just noise," he said. He looked closer with a small <abbr title="n. 透镜">**lens**</abbr>. Under the **lens**, he found a loose wire.

His sister Lily was writing a <abbr title="n. 叙述">**narrative**</abbr> about a big <abbr title="n. 山体滑坡">**landslide**</abbr>. She read it aloud: "The rocks came down the mountain fast." Sam listened for a short time. It was a little <abbr title="n. 离题">**digression**</abbr> from his work.
