Then Sam bought a small <abbr title="n. 小装置">**gizmo**</abbr> online. The <abbr title="n. 商业">**commerce**</abbr> website had many cheap parts. He also saw a <abbr title="n. 商业广告">**commercial**</abbr> for a new radio. But his fixed radio was more special.

When Sam turned it on again, clear music came out! No more **static**. He took his radio to the school science fair. Many people liked his project. The teacher said, "I <abbr title="v. 表扬">**commend**</abbr> you for your hard work. You saved a thing and made it useful."

Sam felt proud. The old radio was **intact** again. It played songs all day. He learned that fixing things is a good **practicable** skill. From that day, Sam loved to **fiddle** with old machines. ### 02

Tom and his best friend Lily were very excited. Today, their class would visit the City Museum. The <abbr title="n. 壮丽">**magnificence**</abbr> of the huge building always amazed them. From the very <abbr title="n. 开始">**outset**</abbr> of the tour, the teacher said, "Stay close and do not touch anything."

There was a wooden <abbr title="n. 四轮马车">**waggon**</abbr> that people used long ago. Next, they visited a room full of glass cases. Inside each case was a different <abbr title="n. 标本">**specimen**</abbr>: a butterfly, a rock, and even a dried leaf.

In a big hall, they learned about old clothes. The guide showed them a piece of <abbr title="n. 织物">**fabric**</abbr> from a royal dress. The pattern was so beautiful that Lily wanted to touch it. He pointed to a word on the card and said, "Look, they forgot the <abbr title="n. 连字符">**hyphen**</abbr> in the artist's name!" Lily felt a little <abbr title="adj. 气愤的">**indignant**</abbr>. "Why must you always **niggle** about little things?" she asked. "Just enjoy the art." Tom wanted to argue, but he decided to <abbr title="v. 回避">**sidestep**</abbr> the fight. "You are right," he said. "Let's move on."

The next room told the story of a rich family who once lived in a big <abbr title="n. 庄园">**estate**</abbr>. The last stop was the old medicine room. The guide there was very <abbr title="adj. 狂热的">**frenetic**</abbr>; she talked fast and moved around quickly. "This bottle once held <abbr title="n. 海洛因">**heroin**</abbr>," she said, holding up an old glass bottle. "Long ago, people used **heroin** as a cough medicine, but now we know it is dangerous." To make things right, Tom helped her <abbr title="v. 放回原处">**reinstate**</abbr> the bottle exactly in its place. The guide thanked him and the room became quiet again.

On the bus ride home, Lily said, "That was a **staggering** day. I never knew a **hyphen** could be so important to you!" Tom laughed. "To me, even a **hyphen** matters. But I will try not to **niggle** too much." They both smiled, and Tom knew he had a great friend who could **sidestep** his silly moods.

### 03

Tom and his sister Lily went to the community garden to help. Today was Garden Day. They needed to <abbr title="v. 侦察">**scout**</abbr> for broken fences and messy paths. Lily pointed to an old **dwelling** at the corner. It was a small tool shed. The door was open, so they looked inside. The shed was dusty. On a shelf, there were some old <abbr title="adj. 白炽的">**incandescent**</abbr> light bulbs. Tom picked one up. “These bulbs are **incandescent**,” he said. “They make light by getting very hot.”

He smiled, but his body was weak. “I have a <abbr title="n. 肿瘤">**tumor**</abbr> in my head,” he said. “The doctor told me it is a <abbr title="n. 少数">**minority**</abbr> type. Tom and Lily felt sad. They wanted to help. “We can raise <abbr title="n. 收入">**revenue**</abbr> for your medicine,” said Lily. They planned a lemonade and cookie stand right in the garden. The next morning, Lily baked cookies. She made them in nice <abbr title="adj. 花的">**floral**</abbr> shapes—like roses and daisies. She also used a sweet <abbr title="n. 调味品">**condiment**</abbr>—pink icing and colorful sprinkles. The stand looked bright and fun.

To get <abbr title="n. 势头">**momentum**</abbr>, Lily stood on a box and gave a funny <abbr title="n. 独白">**monologue**</abbr>. “Get your magic cookies here! They make flowers bloom in your mouth!” she shouted. People stopped and laughed. Many began to buy.

But not everyone was kind. An old neighbor had <abbr title="adj. 根深蒂固的">**entrenched**</abbr> ideas. “Mr. Green is sick. He should just stay at home,” the man said in a <abbr title="adj. 厚颜无耻的">**brazen**</abbr> voice. Lily felt <abbr title="n. 焦虑">**agitation**</abbr> growing inside her. Her hands shook, and she almost knocked over the **condiment** bottles. Tom’s mom came over. “We do not need words. We need <abbr title="adj. 实质的">**substantive**</abbr> help,” she said firmly.

Mr. Green slowly stood up. Even with his **lethargy**, he walked to the stand. “I love this garden, and I love this town,” he said. His brave words started to <abbr title="v. 推翻">**overturn**</abbr> the man’s hard heart. The neighbors began to clap. Just then, a spark hit the old **incandescent** bulb on the stand. There was a small <abbr title="n. 燃烧">**combustion**</abbr>! Tom quickly blew it out. He smiled and said, “Look, the sun’s <abbr title="n. 光球层">**photosphere**</abbr> is **incandescent** too, but it shines by nuclear energy, not **combustion** like this fire.” The children were amazed.

A young mother came with her little <abbr title="n. 婴儿">**infant**</abbr>. The baby giggled and reached for a cookie. The scene became full of warmth. By evening, the stand had brought in more **revenue** than they hoped. The **entrenched** beliefs were slowly **overturn**ed. Mr. Green hugged them. “You saw me at my weakest, but you did not give up,” he said. Though his **tumor** was a **minority** case, he felt less alone. Tom knew that **substantive** actions, even small ones, could make a big change.

### 04

Lily loved to watch the sky. Her Uncle Ben was an <abbr title="n. 飞行员">**aviator**</abbr>, and she dreamed of flying one day. One morning, something bad happened. The town's big clothing shop was <abbr title="v. 抢劫">**robbed**</abbr>. A thief took a costly <abbr title="n. 衣服">**garment**</abbr> – a silk dress. The shop owner found a small piece of cloth. It looked <abbr title="v. 磨损">**frayed**</abbr> at the edge.

Sheriff Stone came. He asked questions. “We must <abbr title="v. 系统整理">**codify**</abbr> all the clues,” he said. Lily didn’t know the word. Sheriff Stone wrote everything on a board in neat lines. Oh, to **codify** means to put things in order! Lily and her friend Tom wanted to help.

They followed a trail of threads to the old farm. Mr. Green practiced animal <abbr title="n. 畜牧业">**husbandry**</abbr> there. There were cows, sheep, and chickens. Behind the barn, they saw a big hole in the ground. It was a <abbr title="n. 火山口">**crater**</abbr> from a volcano long ago. Around the **crater**, beautiful <abbr title="adj. 多年生的">**perennial**</abbr> flowers grew every year – pink and yellow, never fading. The children looked down. Inside the **crater**, they saw a pile of **garments**!

Tom started to climb down, but the ground was loose. Lily shouted “Be careful!” Tom slipped a little. His shirt began to **fray** against a sharp rock. He climbed back up, <abbr title="v. 喘气">**panting**</abbr> heavily. “That was too close,” he **panted**. Lily felt <abbr title="n. 紧张">**tension**</abbr> in her chest. Her heart beat fast because she was scared.

Suddenly, a man came out from behind a tree. It was Mr. Doodle, the art teacher. He looked sad and <abbr title="adj. 贫困的">**needy**</abbr>. His old coat had holes. “Why did you **rob** the shop?” Lily asked. Mr. Doodle’s face fell. “I used to create <abbr title="adj. 舞蹈的">**choreographic**</abbr> shows for the town dance,” he said. He pointed to his notebook. It was full of **choreographic** symbols. “But they canceled my show. I felt <abbr title="v. 使幻灭">**disillusioned**</abbr>. My dream was <abbr title="v. 彻底摧毁">**obliterated**</abbr> – completely destroyed. I have no money. I became **needy**.”

Tom said, “But stealing is wrong. You must <abbr title="v. 丧失">**forfeit**</abbr> your freedom now.” Mr. Doodle nodded. “I know. I will **forfeit** everything I stole.”

Sheriff Stone arrived. “This is under my <abbr title="n. 管辖权">**jurisdiction**</abbr>,” he said. “I must take you in.” His **jurisdiction** covered the whole town. He was kind but firm. He took Mr. Doodle away. He also promised to find <abbr title="adj. 足够的">**adequate**</abbr> help for him – a job at the animal **husbandry** farm. “That will be **adequate** – enough to live simply,” said the sheriff.

That evening, Lily and Tom went to <abbr title="v. 进餐">**dine**</abbr> with Uncle Ben. They **dined** on fresh bread and soup. Uncle Ben poured juice. It was made from <abbr title="v. 浓缩">**condensed**</abbr> fruit syrup. “You add water to this **condensed** syrup, and it becomes sweet juice,” he explained. The children told him the whole story. “You two are little <abbr title="n. 策划者">**masterminds**</abbr>!” Uncle Ben said, winking. “You **masterminded** a plan to find the real **mastermind**.” Lily smiled. “No, we just helped **codify** clues.”

Later, Lily wrote in her diary: “Today I learned that even when people feel **disillusioned**, they can still choose to be good. We must not **forfeit** our kindness.” She also drew a picture of the **crater** with **perennial** flowers. And she drew herself as an **aviator**, flying above. She watched over everyone with brave eyes.

## 06月20日

### 01

Lily lived in a seaside village. Every Saturday, she helped her grandma sell vegetables at the market. Grandma’s old <abbr title="n. 手推车">**trolley**</abbr> was full of fresh <abbr title="n. 卷心菜">**cabbage**</abbr>, carrots, and onions. The **trolley** wheels squeaked, but it was still <abbr title="adj. 可用的">**operational**</abbr>—meaning it worked well enough to carry heavy things.

One morning, as Lily pushed the **trolley** toward the market, a sudden loud sound made her jump. It was the fog <abbr title="n. 喇叭">**horn**</abbr> from the lighthouse. The deep, low note rolled across the harbor. Lily’s grandma always said the **horn** was a warning for boats, telling them to stay safe from rocks. “Obeying the **horn** is a rule of the sea,” Grandma explained. “It’s like <abbr title="n. 服从">**obedience**</abbr> to a parent.”

Lily nodded. She understood **obedience** because her dog, Bingo, had to learn it too. If Bingo did not show **obedience**, he would run into the street. So, **obedience** kept everyone safe.

At the dock, Lily noticed a man she had never seen before. He stood by a fishing boat, wearing a <abbr title="adj. 得意的">**smug**</abbr> smile—a smile that said “I am better than you.” The man’s **smug** look made Lily feel uneasy. He kept glancing at the boat’s cabin, then at the market crowd. His <abbr title="adj. 习惯性的">**habitual**</abbr> way of biting his nails showed he was nervous, even with that **smug** face.

Later, Lily heard two fishermen talking quietly. They did not say the man’s name. Instead, they used words that <abbr title="v. 暗示">**allude**</abbr> to him, like “that new guy” and “the one with the fancy watch.” They were careful not to **allude** directly, but Lily could guess who they meant.

Suddenly, a real <abbr title="n. 大黄蜂">**hornet**</abbr> buzzed near Lily’s ear. She swung her hand and hit her own <abbr title="n. 下巴">**jaw**</abbr> by accident. “Ouch!” she cried. Her **jaw** ached, but she did not cry much. Rubbing her sore **jaw**, she saw the **smug** man slip into the boat cabin.

Without a moment’s <abbr title="n. 犹豫">**hesitation**</abbr>, Lily decided to peek. She knew that **hesitation** could give a wrongdoer time to escape. So, she crept closer. Through a small window, she saw the man take a bag from under a seat. Then he hid it inside a box of **cabbage** on the dock! That was a clear <abbr title="n. 表现">**manifestation**</abbr> of stealing—his quick, sneaky action was a **manifestation** of guilt.

Lily rushed to tell her grandma’s friend, Officer Lee. Officer Lee had a <abbr title="adj. 记性好的">**retentive**</abbr> memory; he could remember every small detail of a person’s face. Because of his **retentive** mind, he quickly recognized the man from a wanted poster. “That’s the smuggler we’ve been watching,” he said. The man had tried to <abbr title="v. 交织">**interweave**</abbr> his own ropes with the boat’s lines, trying to **interweave** his actions so no one would notice. But the **interweave** of ropes looked messy and out of place.

Officer Lee and two police officers walked onto the dock. “We need to <abbr title="v. 暂停">**suspend**</abbr> your activities here,” they told the man. To **suspend** meant to stop him right away. They took the stolen bag, which was full of rare coins. The man’s <abbr title="n. 祖先">**ancestor**</abbr> had been a famous sailor, but this descendant had chosen to <abbr title="v. 偷">**steal**</abbr> instead of sail. It was sad that his **ancestor**’s honor meant nothing to him.

The police also checked his boat’s <abbr title="adj. 近海的">**offshore**</abbr> route. He had planned to go **offshore** at midnight, far from the coast. But now he would be <abbr title="v. 驱逐">**deport**ed</abbr>. To **deport** him meant sending him back to his own country because he broke the law. The **deport** order was signed quickly. Before the **deport**, the man’s **smug** face finally disappeared. He looked down at the ground.

Later, Grandma gave Lily a warm cabbage soup. “You acted with no **hesitation**,” she said. “That was brave.” Lily felt proud but not **smug**. She knew that **obedience** to the law and a keen eye helped protect their village. As the fog **horn** gave a final <abbr title="n. 一阵喷气">**puff**</abbr> of sound—a short, soft **puff** like a sigh—the **offshore** wind blew, and the sea was calm again. The little **hornet** flew away, and Lily’s **jaw** no longer hurt. She smiled, knowing she had helped make things right.

### 02

Lily and Grandpa hiked into the green forest for a camping trip. Sunlight came through the leaves. They carried a big <abbr title="n. 帐篷">**tent**</abbr> and a <abbr title="n. 保温瓶">**thermos**</abbr> full of ice water. “Why ice water, Grandpa?” Lily asked. “You’ll see,” he said with a wink. After walking for a while, they found a flat, soft place. They dropped their bags and took out the **tent**. The **tent** was bright blue. They pushed poles through loops and soon the **tent** stood up. Lily felt proud.

As they finished, Lily held her stomach. “My <abbr title="n. 肠道">**gut**</abbr> hurts,” she said. Grandpa looked worried. He opened his bag and gave her an <abbr title="adj. 抗菌的">**antibacterial**</abbr> wipe. “Use this. Sometimes your **gut** gets upset from dirty hands while setting up the **tent**.”

“Grandpa, tell me about your work,” Lily said. Grandpa sat down on a log. “I used to work in a big <abbr title="n. 实验室">**laboratory**</abbr>. I loved to <abbr title="v. 解释">**expound**</abbr> about my experiments. I once invented an **antibacterial** medicine. It was strong and killed bad germs. But a <abbr title="n. 冲突">**conflict**</abbr> with a big company happened. They did not like a small startup. Soon, our business became <abbr title="adj. 破产的">**bankrupt**</abbr>.”

Lily asked, “Why didn’t they like you?” Grandpa sighed. “Their thinking was <abbr title="adj. 封建的">**feudal**</abbr>—they wanted old ways, not new. But the patent for my medicine was <abbr title="adj. 可转移的">**transferable**</abbr>. I sold it to another company. Still, I lost a lot of money and my **laboratory**.” Lily hugged him.

Later, Lily’s mouth felt dry. She had a <abbr title="n. 渴望">**craving**</abbr> for a sweet drink. “Can I have some juice?” she asked. Grandpa shook his head. “We must <abbr title="v. 克制">**refrain**</abbr> from too much sugar. Look.” He took out a small bottle from the **thermos**. “This is <abbr title="n. 胰岛素">**insulin**</abbr>. My body needs it because I have diabetes. The **insulin** must stay at 4 degrees <abbr title="n. 摄氏度">**centigrade**</abbr>. That’s why I brought the **thermos** with ice.” Lily looked at the clear liquid. “Life has <abbr title="n. 有限">**finitude**</abbr>, Lily,” Grandpa said softly. “We must take care of our health.” Lily <abbr title="v. 吐露">**confide**</abbr>d, “I miss Mom’s candy. She always brought chocolate.” Grandpa put an arm around her. “Love is **transferable**. You can love me and Mom at the same time.”

After resting, they walked to a water pump. There was a long <abbr title="n. 队列">**queue**</abbr> of campers. The sun was hot. Lily stood on her tiptoes. A woman with bright red nails was ahead. She turned and smiled. “Hello! I’m a <abbr title="n. 美甲师">**manicurist**</abbr>. I paint nails in the city. But today my **gut** feeling said, ‘Go to the woods!’ So here I am.” Lily laughed. “Your nails are beautiful!”

Suddenly, a tall man pushed in front of everyone. A **conflict** began. The **manicurist** said, “Sir, there is a **queue**. Please wait.” The man said, “I’m in a hurry!” Grandpa stepped forward. His voice was calm but strong. “If you do not follow the **queue**, I will tell the park ranger. The fine is a big <abbr title="n. 威慑物">**deterrent**</abbr>. Nobody wants to pay a fine.” The man looked down and went to the back. The **manicurist** smiled. “Kindness and rules <abbr title="v. 获胜">**prevail**</abbr>!”

That evening, they sat inside the **tent**. The sky was full of stars. Grandpa **expound**ed on space. “Do you see that star? Its light is **transferable** from millions of years ago. Even stars have **finitude**—they die one day. But their light keeps traveling.” Lily’s **gut** felt much better. She asked, “Will the sun die too?” Grandpa nodded. “One day. But we have millions of years.” Lily felt small but happy. She learned that love and knowledge are **transferable**. “I will **refrain** from too much candy,” she promised. “Good,” said Grandpa. “Your health will **prevail**.”

The next morning, the **manicurist** came to say goodbye. She gave Lily a tiny bottle of nail polish. “This color is **transferable** to your nails. And remember, a calm voice is the best **deterrent**.” Lily hugged her. They packed the **tent** and **thermos**. The camping trip reached its **finitude**, but the memories were warm.

### 03

Last summer, four friends—Tom, Lucy, Jack, and Emma—stayed with Grandpa in a far‑off, <abbr title="adj. 多丘陵的">**hilly**</abbr> village. One afternoon, they found an old map in the attic. It had strange symbols and a number: 78 degrees <abbr title="n. 经度">**longitude**</abbr>. Grandpa said, “There is an <abbr title="adj. 未经证实的">**unsubstantiated**</abbr> story about hidden treasure in these hills. Many have tried, but no one found it.” The kids' eyes shone with excitement. They decided to search for it.

Tom, whose <abbr title="n. 专长">**specialty**</abbr> was reading maps, pointed out they had to go far <abbr title="adv. 在远处">**afield**</abbr>, past the old <abbr title="n. 大教堂">**cathedral**</abbr> on the hill. Lucy, who loved science, talked about the sun's <abbr title="n. 太阳色球层">**chromosphere**</abbr> while they walked, but the others only half‑listened. They packed food: sandwiches, fruit, and some <abbr title="adj. 油腻的">**greasy**</abbr> potato chips.

As they entered the forest, a strict man stopped them. “I’m the forest <abbr title="n. 专员">**commissioner**</abbr>,” he said. “You need to <abbr title="v. 有资格">**qualify**</abbr> for the hiking trail by wearing proper boots, not sandals.” Jack was upset and whispered, “I’ll <abbr title="v. 起诉">**sue**</abbr> him for being so mean!” But Lucy laughed and said, “Let’s not; let’s just go back and change shoes.” They hurried home and started <abbr title="adv. 重新">**anew**</abbr> an hour later, this time with boots.

Along the trail, Emma showed a shiny compass. Jack felt <abbr title="adj. 嫉妒的">**envious**</abbr> and said, “Why does she get all the cool stuff?” They argued until Tom helped them <abbr title="v. 和解">**reconcile**</abbr>. “We’re a team!” he said. “This compass is <abbr title="adj. 有帮助的">**instrumental**</abbr> for finding the way.” Jack felt sorry and hugged Emma.

Later, they rested. Jack took out the chips. He ate with <abbr title="n. 贪食">**voracity**</abbr>, stuffing his mouth so fast that he nearly choked. Lucy warned, “Slow down!” Suddenly, they heard a loud BANG. Grandpa’s old truck, which they had borrowed, had hit a rock and its front <abbr title="n. 保险杠">**bumper**</abbr> fell off. They were stuck but decided to walk the last mile.

Near a stream, they saw a bird with a bright red <abbr title="n. 胸部">**breast**</abbr>. It seemed to lead them to a hidden cave. Inside, they found a dusty chest. They opened it, expecting gold. Instead, it was full of old musical instruments—flutes, violins, a drum. At first they were disappointed, but then Lucy said, “Maybe this treasure can <abbr title="v. 激励">**inspire**</abbr> us to start a band!” They realized the real treasure was not money but creativity and friendship. The <abbr title="n. 道德寓意">**morals**</abbr> of the adventure was to value teamwork and never give up.

They carried the instruments back, passing the **cathedral** as the sun set. Tom looked through a telescope later and saw the **chromosphere** glowing red, and felt that the day had been magical. They all agreed to learn music and play together **anew** next summer.

### 04

On Saturday morning, Tom woke up to a wonderful smell—his mom was going to <abbr title="v. 烤">**roast**</abbr> a chicken. But then he heard a loud buzz and a pop. The oven had a <abbr title="n. 故障">**malfunction**</abbr>. “Oh no!” Mom said. The chicken was only half-cooked. Dad quickly looked through a <abbr title="n. 目录">**catalog**</abbr> for a repair service. “We can call this number,” he said.

Mom took out the hand mixer, or <abbr title="n. 搅拌器">**whisk**</abbr>, to whip the cream. She turned it on and the cream became fluffy. Then Tom’s little sister Lucy came in. She wore a pretty dress and an old-fashioned <abbr title="n. 紧身胸衣">**corset**</abbr> from Grandma. “It feels a bit tight,” Lucy said, but Mom smiled and said it was a family treasure. They all planned to go to the Harvest Festival in town.

At the festival, the first thing Tom saw was a big hot air balloon. The <abbr title="n. 热气球驾驶员">**balloonist**</abbr> waved from the basket. He turned on the fire, and the balloon began to <abbr title="v. 膨胀">**dilate**</abbr>—it got bigger and bigger. Tom watched the colorful balloon grow in the sky. A <abbr title="adv. 在当地">**locally**</abbr> famous band then played a short <abbr title="n. 前奏">**prelude**</abbr> before the main show. The music was happy and made people dance.

Tom felt hot, so he bought a small <abbr title="n. 喷雾">**spray**</abbr> bottle of water. He cooled his face with the fine mist. Near the food area, Dad pointed to a sign. “A <abbr title="n. 空位">**vacancy**</abbr>—a free table!” They went inside the busy restaurant. The room was full of <abbr title="n. 顾客">**patrons**</abbr> eating and laughing. A kind waitress found them a table by the window. Dad ordered a **roast** beef sandwich, and Tom got cold lemonade. The glass had tiny drops of water on the outside—that was <abbr title="n. 冷凝">**condensation**</abbr>. Mom explained that the cold drink made the warm air turn into water drops.

After lunch, they watched a horse race. A **jockey** in bright yellow and blue rode fast. One horse jumped over a small <abbr title="n. 小树枝">**twig**</abbr> that had fallen from a tree. “Look, a tiny branch!” Tom laughed. Then the town held a ceremony to <abbr title="v. 纪念">**commemorate**</abbr> the founder of the town. People clapped, and some old people cried a little. A man brought a big <abbr title="n. 大捆">**bale**</abbr> of hay for decoration. It was tied tightly with string and smelled like the farm.

Tom’s mom and dad held hands. They were <abbr title="n. 配偶">**spouses**</abbr> for ten years, and Dad gave Mom a kiss on the cheek. Suddenly, a cold wind blew. “It feels like a **glacial** wind—so icy!” Tom said, shivering. The **balloonist** was trying to land, but the wind was too strong. The balloon lifted and swirled wildly. “The wind is **whisking** the balloon away!” cried Tom. His **spray** bottle fell and rolled under a bench. The **jockey** ran to help catch the balloon rope, and many **patrons** cheered.

In all the excitement, Tom noticed that a warm, happy feeling was a <abbr title="adj. 伴随的">**concomitant**</abbr> of the festival—it came together with the fun, the music, and the people. Finally, the balloon landed safely. The **roast** beef sandwich was still so tasty in his memory. As they drove home, the **malfunction** oven was already fixed, and the house felt warm, even though the weather outside was still **glacial**. Tom thought about the **bale** of hay, the old **corset**, and the **catalog** from the morning. The **condensation** on the car window showed the frosty night. The happy **prelude** music stayed in his ears. The **balloonist** waved goodbye as they left. Their bag held **locally** grown apples, sweet and crisp. And the brave **jockey** became Tom’s new hero. The two **spouses** in the front seats laughed, and Tom knew that the **concomitant** joy of family would always stay with him.

### 05

Mia’s class went to the History and Science Museum. The teacher said, “Pay <abbr title="n. 注意">**heed**</abbr> to everything, because later there is a surprise quiz.” Mia was excited. She loved <abbr title="n. 考古学">**archeology**</abbr>—the study of old things dug from the ground.

Walking into the first room, she saw a huge painting on the wall, a <abbr title="n. 壁画">**mural**</abbr> that showed ancient people. In the **mural**, a woman was holding a round <abbr title="n. 馅饼">**pie**</abbr>. Near the **mural**, there was a strange old tool. A sign read: “A <abbr title="adj. 省力的">**laborsaving**</abbr> tool—it made hard work easier with less effort.” Mia thought, “So this tool is **laborsaving**, like a wheel. It saves energy.”

Then they went to the <abbr title="n. 解剖学">**anatomy**</abbr> room. Models of human bodies stood there. Mia remembered from biology class: **anatomy** is the study of the body’s parts. She looked at a heart model.

Suddenly, a man in plain clothes walked up. He was not wearing a museum uniform; he was <abbr title="adj. 秘密的">**undercover**</abbr>, trying to look like a regular visitor. He whispered to Mia, “I am a <abbr title="n. 管理员">**steward**</abbr> here. I take care of the museum. I need help.” Mia was surprised. The **steward** seemed worried, many problems <abbr title="v. 困扰">**beset**</abbr> him. He coughed and seemed to <abbr title="v. 噎住">**choke**</abbr> on his words. He took a sip of water. “Sorry, I’m nervous,” he said.

He spoke with great <abbr title="n. 简短">**brevity**</abbr>, using very few words. “Follow. I will <abbr title="v. 引路">**usher**</abbr> you.” An usher is someone who shows the way. He led her through a crowd. The hallway had so much <abbr title="n. 拥堵">**congestion**</abbr>—people everywhere, hard to move. “Too much **congestion**,” he said. “This way.”

They came to a small model of a river. You could see a <abbr title="n. 浅滩">**ford**</abbr>, a shallow part where you could walk across. “Step on the stones to cross the **ford**,” he instructed. Mia did, carefully. On the other side was a model of an old ship. They walked onto its wooden <abbr title="n. 甲板">**deck**</abbr>. The floor of a ship is called a deck. On the **deck**, there was a locked box.

“My father was a <abbr title="n. 牧师">**clergyman**</abbr>—a church leader. He loved history. He left me a key to this box, but I lost it. The box holds an artifact that I must <abbr title="v. 解救">**bail**</abbr> out, to get free from here. Can you find the key’s <abbr title="n. 关联">**relevance**</abbr> to the **anatomy** room?” Mia thought. Relevance means connection. She remembered the heart model. The heart shape was carved on the box! She pointed it out. The steward smiled. “You found the **relevance**!”

Inside the box was a small golden key. With it, they opened a door to a storage room. The **steward** got the artifact—an ancient cup. “Thank you for helping me **bail** it out. I was so **beset** by this problem.” Mia felt proud.

As a reward, the steward gave her a real apple **pie** from the museum café. “My mother is <abbr title="adj. 熟悉的">**conversant**</abbr> with baking; she made it. Conversant means she knows a lot about it.” The **pie** was warm and sweet.

Mia was so happy. She gave the steward a warm <abbr title="n. 拥抱">**embrace**</abbr>—a tight hug. He laughed. “Now you know many new words, and you used them wisely. Pay **heed** to them in your quiz.” And Mia indeed aced the quiz.

## 06月21日

### 01

Last Saturday, my parents and I went to the Harvest Fair in the town square. The fair was organized by the local <abbr title="n. 社区组织">**Commonwealth**</abbr> Club. Many families came to enjoy the bright autumn day. Balloons in red and yellow flew above the tents.

Near the gate, we saw a big <abbr title="n. 堆">**heap**</abbr> of pumpkins. They were piled up like a small orange mountain. Some were as big as my head! We took a funny photo by the **heap**.

Dad pointed to a tent with a wooden sign. “That is a small <abbr title="n. 酿酒厂">**brewery**</abbr>,” he said. We went inside. The air smelled sour and fruity because of <abbr title="n. 发酵">**ferment**</abbr>. A friendly man showed us a big pot of apple juice. “Watch the **ferment**,” he said. Tiny bubbles rose from the juice. The **ferment** made it turn into cider slowly.

Mom found a table with many cloths. She picked up a soft <abbr title="n. 亚麻布">**linen**</abbr> one. “Feel this,” she said. The **linen** was cool and smooth. She bought the **linen** tablecloth for our kitchen at home.

Then, Dad saw a pie-eating contest. On a <abbr title="n. 心血来潮">**whim**</abbr>, he signed up. I had to give my <abbr title="adj. 父母的">**parental**</abbr> okay because I was a helper under 12. It felt strange to give **parental** permission to my own dad!

The <abbr title="n. 裁判">**referee**</abbr>, a tall man with a loud whistle, said, “Go!” Dad ate too fast and got blueberry pie all over his face and shirt. His messy look <abbr title="v. 使丢脸">**mortified**</abbr> me. I felt my face turn hot and red. Other kids laughed, and I wished the ground could swallow me up.

But then a little boy fell down near our table. He cried loudly. Dad stopped eating right away and helped him up. He even gave the boy his last piece of pie to calm him. Everyone around us clapped and smiled. That was truly <abbr title="adj. 令人钦佩的">**admirable**</abbr>. His kindness helped <abbr title="v. 证明正当">**justify**</abbr> his silly start. Some people said, “What a good dad!”

Later, I walked to a game tent. There was an <abbr title="n. 谜">**enigma**</abbr> board. You had to guess three **enigma**s to win. The first **enigma** was, “What has an eye but cannot see?” I shouted, “A needle!” The next one was harder, but I solved it too. The prize was a brand-new box of <abbr title="n. 蜡笔">**crayon**</abbr>s. I love drawing rainbows with **crayon**s.

Mom stopped at a shiny jewelry booth. She tried on a silver ring with a small clear stone. “This diamond is exactly one <abbr title="n. 克拉">**carat**</abbr>,” the seller said. Mom held out her hand. The one-**carat** diamond sparkled in the sun. She looked very happy.

Near the safety tent, an old man held a broken thermometer. Silver liquid shone inside the glass. “This is <abbr title="n. 水银">**mercury**</abbr>,” he said. “It is dangerous if it leaks. You must <abbr title="v. 处理">**dispose**</abbr> of it carefully. Put any old **mercury** thermometer in a special box. Never throw **mercury** in the normal bin.” I made a note to tell my teacher.

Then the sky turned gray. Rain began to fall. The weather was a little <abbr title="adj. 令人沮丧的">**dispiriting**</abbr>. Puddles formed on the paths, and people ran under tents. But my mom said, “A little rain can’t spoil our fun!” So we stayed positive.

At the prize table, I went to get my **crayon**s. The helper gave me two boxes by mistake. “Oh, we have <abbr title="adj. 多余的">**redundant**</abbr> prizes today,” she said. I felt <abbr title="adj. 矛盾的">**ambivalent**</abbr>. Getting extra **crayon**s made me glad, but it also felt unfair to keep two. So I gave one box to the little boy who had fallen. He smiled, and my **ambivalent** feeling went away.

As the dark came, we all sat around a large <abbr title="n. 篝火">**campfire**</abbr>. The **campfire** crackled and sent up orange sparks. We roasted marshmallows and sang silly songs. The **campfire** kept us warm and dry. Even the rain could not put it out. The cozy **campfire** ended our day with a golden glow.

### 02

It was a <abbr title="adj. 沉闷的">**dreary**</abbr> <abbr title="n. 黄昏">**dusk**</abbr>, the sky was gray and the air felt wet. Tommy, a young <abbr title="n. 男孩">**lad**</abbr> with messy hair, sat on the porch with a pen and paper. He had to write a <abbr title="n. 作文">**composition**</abbr> for his English class, but he just stared at the blank page. The topic was “My Family,” and he found it boring. He <abbr title="v. 倚靠">**leaned**</abbr> back in his chair, trying to think, and rocked on the back legs. Suddenly, with a loud crack, the chair broke! Tommy fell hard and landed on his <abbr title="n. 屁股">**butt**</abbr>. His brand new <abbr title="n. 裤子">**pants**</abbr> got a big mud stain. His mother came out and said, “Oh Tommy, you are so <abbr title="adj. 鲁莽的">**reckless**</abbr>! Now look at your **pants**.” Tommy felt embarrassed.

Later that evening, Tommy complained of a toothache. His mother took him to the <abbr title="n. 牙医">**dentist**</abbr>. The **dentist**’s office was white and clean. The **dentist** checked his mouth with a small mirror and said, “You have a minor <abbr title="n. 小病">**ailment**</abbr>—just a small cavity. Nothing serious. But you must brush better.” Tommy asked, “Is it as bad as the <abbr title="n. 麻疹">**measles**</abbr>?” The **dentist** laughed. “No, the **measles** is a terrible <abbr title="n. 祸害">**scourge**</abbr>. It gives you fever and red spots all over. Your friend Jack has it, right?” Tommy nodded. He remembered Jack had been sick for a week because of the **measles**.

On the way home, Tommy saw Jack’s house. He stopped and saw Jack looking out the window, still pale with a few spots. Jack’s mother let them talk from afar. “I’m so bored!” Jack called. “Let’s <abbr title="v. 交换">**swap**</abbr> something. I’ll give you my comic book, and when I’m better, you give me your new toy car.” Tommy agreed and promised to do the **swap** the next day.

When Tommy got home, his mother was in her study. She was an anthropologist and did <abbr title="adj. 人类学的">**anthropological**</abbr> research. She was a <abbr title="adj. 开创性的">**pioneering**</abbr> scientist in a special field: the <abbr title="adj. 嗅觉的">**olfactory**</abbr> sense in different cultures. She told Tommy stories about how some people use smell to greet each other, or how they use flowers to tell stories. Tommy was amazed. He decided to write his **composition** about his mother’s work. “That’s so interesting,” he said, and began writing happily.

A few days later, Jack recovered. He came over to Tommy’s house. Everything seemed to <abbr title="v. 达到顶点">**culminate**</abbr> in a perfect afternoon. The two boys went to the park and sat on two <abbr title="adj. 平行的">**parallel**</abbr> swings. Jack said, “Your mom’s job sounds cool. My dad sometimes drinks <abbr title="n. 白兰地">**brandy**</abbr> when he has a sore throat—does that affect the **olfactory** sense?” Tommy laughed. Then Jack, still a bit weak, tried to stand up on the swing. The swing began to shake and almost <abbr title="v. 倒下">**toppled**</abbr> over! Tommy yelled, “Don’t be **reckless**, Jack! You just got over your **ailment**!” Jack sat down, grinning. The sky turned dark blue, and they walked home together, happy and safe. Tommy thought about the **dreary** **dusk** when he began his **composition**, and now the fun afternoon made him smile.

### 03

Tom has a strange **<abbr title="n. 爱好">**hobby**</abbr>**. He likes to collect **<abbr title="n. 矿石">**ore**</abbr>** — small, colorful stones from the river. His mum thinks it is a dirty **hobby**, but Tom does not stop. Every weekend, he puts on old shoes and walks to the river with a little hammer.

One Saturday, Tom picks up a **<abbr title="n. 传单">**leaflet**</abbr>** on the street. The **leaflet** has a picture of a **<abbr title="n. 麻雀">**sparrow**</abbr>**. It says, “Help save the **sparrow**! Their number is going down.” Tom loves birds, so he folds the **leaflet** and puts it in his pocket. He thinks, “I will read it later.”

On the way, he walks past a **<abbr title="n. 理发店">**barbershop**</abbr>**. The **barbershop** is small and old, but it smells like soap. The **<abbr title="n. 店主">**proprietor**</abbr>**, a kind old lady, stands outside and gives **leaflet**s to people. She is the **proprietor** of the **barbershop**. She smiles at Tom and says, “Good morning!” But this morning, the **proprietor** looks a little worried because the weather is **<abbr title="adj. 多变的">**fickle**</abbr>**. One minute the sun shines, the next minute dark clouds come. The **fickle** weather makes people stay home, so she has no customers today. Tom says sorry for her and continues walking.

Soon, Tom arrives at the river. He sees a **<abbr title="n. 露营者">**camper**</abbr>** by the water. The **camper** has a tent and a fishing rod. A small bag **<abbr title="v. 悬挂">**dangle**s**</abbr>** from the tent. Inside the bag is an **<abbr title="n. 工具">**implement**</abbr>** — a small knife. The **camper** uses the **implement** to cut a rope. Suddenly, the **camper** shouts, “Ouch! My leg!” He falls down. He has a **<abbr title="n. 抽筋">**cramp**</abbr>**! The **cramp** makes him unable to stand. His face turns red and he **<abbr title="v. 吐口水">**spit**s**</abbr>** on the ground from the pain. Tom runs over quickly. He is an **<abbr title="adj. 敏捷的">**agile**</abbr>** boy — he can jump over rocks easily. He holds the **camper**’s leg and helps him stretch it. Soon, the **cramp** goes away. The **camper** thanks Tom and says, “You are so **agile** and kind!”

Then, the **camper** points to the water. “Look! A **<abbr title="n. 螃蟹">**crab**</abbr>**!” Tom turns and sees a little **crab** crawling on a stone. The **crab** has a shell with many **<abbr title="adj. 有刺的">**spined**</abbr>** edges. If you touch the **spined** shell, your finger may bleed. Tom watches the **crab** carefully. The **crab** waves its claws and then **spit**s some water bubbles. Tom laughs. He likes the **crab**, but he does not pick it up because of the **spined** back.

After helping the **camper**, Tom remembers his **hobby**. He needs to find some **ore**. He walks along the riverbank and sees a **<abbr title="n. 五金店">**hardware**</abbr>** store not far away. The **hardware** store sells many things — nails, hammers, and boxes. Tom needs a better **implement** to break rocks for **ore**. He goes inside.

The **hardware** store has a new **proprietor**, a young man. The **proprietor** is a little **fickle** — first he smiles, then he frowns because no one buys expensive tools. Many **implement**s **dangle** from hooks on the wall. Tom chooses a small pickaxe, a perfect **implement** for digging **ore**. The **proprietor** takes his money with a quick nod.

Just as Tom leaves the **hardware** store, a **<abbr title="adj. 响亮的">**resounding**</abbr>** thunder cracks above. Boom! The **resounding** noise makes Tom jump. The **fickle** weather turns very dark. Rain begins to fall. Tom must **<abbr title="v. 缩短">**curtail**</abbr>** his trip. He planned to search for **ore** all day, but now he has to **curtail** it and run home. He does not want to get wet, but he has to **<abbr title="v. 忍受">**endure**</abbr>** the cold rain. He **endure**s the wind and the water running down his neck. Even his **enduring** **<abbr title="adj. 持久的">**enduring**</abbr>** love for **ore** cannot stop him from feeling tired. But the **enduring** rain finally stops when he reaches his door.

Inside, Tom dries himself and takes out the **leaflet**. A **sparrow** flies past his window — a fast, **agile** little bird. The **sparrow** lands on a branch and sings a **resounding** song. Tom smiles. Even though the day had a **cramp**, a **spined** **crab**, a **fickle** **proprietor**, and a **resounding** storm, he still found a new **implement** at the **hardware** store and helped a **camper**. And he will go back to find more **ore** next week — that is his **enduring** **hobby**. He will always **endure** a little rain for the things he loves.

### 04

The Genial Census

Lily’s grandpa was a <abbr title="adj. 和蔼的">**genial**</abbr> old man. He always wore a kind smile. One sunny Saturday, he said, “Lily, I need your help with the <abbr title="n. 人口普查">**census**</abbr>. We must count all the <abbr title="n. 民众">**populace**</abbr> in Green Village.” Lily felt <abbr title="adj. 无知的">**ignorant**</abbr> about the word ‘census’. Grandpa laughed, “It just means counting people who live here. Easy!”

They packed a yellow <abbr title="n. 手袋">**handbag**</abbr> with a small bottle of <abbr title="n. 洗发水">**shampoo**</abbr> and a few <abbr title="n. 谷物棒">**cereal**</abbr> bars. The map showed the village like a <abbr title="n. 迷宫">**labyrinth**</abbr>—tiny paths twisting in and out. Lily felt a thrill. This was a new <abbr title="n. 努力">**endeavor**</abbr> for her.

Soon, they walked along the <abbr title="adj. 无尽的">**endless**</abbr> streets. The sun was hot, and Lily began to feel a strong <abbr title="n. 口渴">**thirst**</abbr>. “Don’t worry,” Grandpa said. He took out a small white thing from his pocket. “I <abbr title="v. 压缩">**compress**</abbr>ed a towel. Now watch.” He put it into a cup of water. The tiny towel grew big and soft. “We can use it to clean our faces,” he smiled. They drank water and ate the **cereal** bars. The bar had a hard, crunchy <abbr title="n. 外皮">**crust**</abbr> on top, but Lily liked it.

As they walked, a boy ran up to them. “What are you doing here?” he asked. Grandpa asked his name for the **census**. The boy stuck out his tongue. “I won’t tell you!” He was **ignorant** of basic <abbr title="n. 礼仪">**etiquette**</abbr>. His noise began to <abbr title="v. 惹恼">**aggravate**</abbr> Grandpa. But Grandpa stayed <abbr title="adj. 坚定的">**steadfast**</abbr>. He said softly, “Young man, good **etiquette** means being polite and helpful.” At last, the boy gave his name and ran away.

Soon they stopped at an old wooden house. A strange, loud chirping filled the air. “Crickets!” Lily shouted. The house was <abbr title="v. 侵扰">**infest**</abbr>ed with small brown <abbr title="n. 蟋蟀">**cricket**</abbr>s. They jumped everywhere—on the floor, on the walls. Lily saw a rusty <abbr title="n. 锯子">**saw**</abbr> in the corner. It looked <abbr title="adj. 破旧的">**worn**</abbr> and forgotten. Next to the **saw**, a pile of bread **crust**s was covered with busy **cricket**s. “This place needs cleaning,” Grandpa said.

They marked the house empty and moved on. Lily felt her feet ache, but she remembered Grandpa’s **steadfast** spirit and kept going. By evening, they had knocked on every door and finished the **endeavor**. Grandpa wrote down the numbers: the **populace** was one hundred and five. “Good work, Lily,” he said. “You helped with the **census**! I’m proud of you.”

Walking home, the sky turned orange. Lily thought about the **endless** day, the **labyrinth** of streets, the rude boy, and the **cricket**-**infest**ed house. Even the **crust** on the **cereal** bar tasted better now. “I learned so much,” she said. “And you were **genial** even when that boy **aggravate**d you.” Grandpa chuckled. “It’s all part of the job. Next time, you can bring your own **handbag** with more **shampoo** and snacks.”

That night, Lily dreamed of **cricket** songs and the old **saw**, feeling happy about her first **census**.

# 第4周

## 06月23日

### 01

Last Saturday, Lily and her brother Tom went on a trip with their parents. Their dad is a <abbr title="n. 儿科医生">**pediatrician**</abbr>, but he had a day off. They visited a small <abbr title="adj. 殖民地的">**colonial**</abbr> town. The old <abbr title="n. 国会">**Congress**</abbr> Hall stood on a hill. Its big wooden door was <abbr title="adj. 半开的">**ajar**</abbr>, so they walked in. The floor was made of red <abbr title="n. 瓷砖">**tile**</abbr>. Inside, there was a <abbr title="adj. 回顾的">**retrospective**</abbr> show about the town's history. A friendly man, the <abbr title="n. 副手">**deputy**</abbr> director of the museum, said hello. He showed them a very old clock that needed <abbr title="n. 校准">**calibration**</abbr> to tell the right time.

Lily loved a glass case with a fish <abbr title="n. 化石">**fossil**</abbr>. Next to it, there was the skeleton of a <abbr title="n. 响尾蛇">**rattlesnake**</abbr> that once lived in a nearby <abbr title="n. 沼泽">**marsh**</abbr>. The curator explained that the snake’s <abbr title="n. 肌腱">**sinew**</abbr> was still visible in the fossil. Lily also saw a small <abbr title="n. 囊">**sac**</abbr> from a frog. Nearby, an old <abbr title="n. 小提琴">**fiddle**</abbr> and a wooden <abbr title="n. 木槌">**mallet**</abbr> were on display. A stone <abbr title="n. 凿子">**chisel**</abbr> used by early builders lay next to them. Mom said, ‘In colonial days, <abbr title="n. 纺织品">**textile**</abbr> making was an important job.’ She knows a lot because she is a **textile** designer. She pointed to a <abbr title="adj. 猩红色的">**scarlet**</abbr> banner on the wall, but the color was now a little <abbr title="adj. 较差的">**inferior**</abbr> because of age.

Tom tried to draw the fossil, but his drawing looked funny. Lily laughed, and Tom felt it was an <abbr title="n. 冒犯">**affront**</abbr>. He said, ‘That <abbr title="v. 使疼痛">**hurt**</abbr> my feelings.’ Dad told Lily not to laugh. Tom, being an <abbr title="n. 乐观的人">**optimist**</abbr>, soon smiled again.

Dad’s phone beeped. It might be an <abbr title="adj. 紧急的">**urgent**</abbr> call from the hospital, but it was just a message. As they left the museum, Mom looked at Dad. ‘Don’t forget our <abbr title="n. 周年纪念日">**anniversary**</abbr> next week,’ she said. They walked to a park. The park had a <abbr title="adj. 清澈的">**limpid**</abbr> blue lake. The view of the hills was <abbr title="adj. 令人惊叹的">**breathtaking**</abbr>! There was a <abbr title="n. 跷跷板">**seesaw**</abbr>, and Tom and Lily played on it. Lily also loved to <abbr title="v. 旋转">**spin**</abbr> around until she got dizzy. After playing, they felt very thirsty. Mom bought cups of cold <abbr title="n. 苹果酒">**cider**</abbr>. Lily <abbr title="v. 大口吞">**gulped**</abbr> her cider to <abbr title="v. 解渴">**quench**</abbr> her thirst. Her little <abbr title="n. 同伴">**companion**</abbr> Tom drank slowly.

Nearby, a woman sold fresh <abbr title="n. 樱桃">**cherries**</abbr>. But the price was <abbr title="adj. 过高的">**prohibitive**</abbr>—too high for a small bag! Lily tried to be <abbr title="adj. 明智的">**judicious**</abbr> and didn’t buy any. ‘We can get cherries at home,’ she said.

Tom ran around and then said his <abbr title="n. 胸部">**chest**</abbr> **hurt**. Dad checked him. ‘It’s just a stitch. The pain will <abbr title="v. 减弱">**abate**</abbr> soon,’ he said. Dad himself felt a strange feeling in his <abbr title="n. 肠道">**gut**</abbr>, maybe from worrying about the <abbr title="n. 流行病">**epidemic**</abbr> on the news. The flu **epidemic** was beginning to **abate**, the report said. Mom, an <abbr title="adj. 勤劳的">**industrious**</abbr> woman, planned to finish a new **textile** design that evening.

They walked past a playground with many <abbr title="adj. 娱乐的">**recreational**</abbr> games. Dad mentioned he was about to sign a new <abbr title="n. 合同">**contract**</abbr> for work. Suddenly, they heard cheers from a field. A football game was going on. They stopped to watch, and just then, a player made a quick <abbr title="n. 达阵">**touchdown**</abbr>! Everyone clapped. Dad said, ‘I don’t <abbr title="v. 宽恕">**condone**</abbr> violence, but football is exciting.’

On the way back, they passed the **marsh**. The water looked <abbr title="adj. 浑浊的">**murky**</abbr>, not clear like the lake. ‘**Rattlesnakes** <abbr title="v. 占多数">**predominate**</abbr> in some parts, but not here,’ Dad said.

Back in the car, Lily remembered her English test on Monday. She had to study <abbr title="n. 介词">**prepositions**</abbr> like ‘in, on, under.’ She needed a good <abbr title="n. 资格">**qualification**</abbr> to pass to the next level. Tom cheered her up, ‘You are **industrious**, Lily. You will do great!’

At a souvenir shop, they saw a funny <abbr title="n. 外星人">**alien**</abbr> toy. Mom bought a small **textile** purse. Lily thought the day was a <abbr title="n. 意外好运">**bonanza**</abbr>—full of new things. She had seen a **fossil**, learned about **colonial** times, and even watched a **touchdown**. She knew she would not feel **inferior** about her test, because she was ready.

### 02

The Winter Survival Rite

Mr. Thompson, a retired <abbr title="n. 政治家">**statesman**</abbr>, led the Survival Club. For the final <abbr title="n. 仪式">**rite**</abbr>, he announced a <abbr title="adj. 乌托邦的">**Utopian**</abbr> challenge: survive a night in the mountain during a <abbr title="n. 暴风雪">**blizzard**</abbr>. His advice was always <abbr title="adj. 实用主义的">**utilitarian**</abbr>: take only what is truly useful. “We must <abbr title="v. 最小化">**minimize**</abbr> risk,” he said <abbr title="adv. 冷静地">**dispassionately**</abbr>. “Every <abbr title="n. 缺点">**demerit**</abbr> on your score could mean failure.” He showed us how to use a <abbr title="n. 铲子">**shovel**</abbr> to build a snow shelter and a <abbr title="n. 测量器">**gauge**</abbr> to measure snow depth. We packed <abbr title="n. 货物">**cargo**</abbr> on sledges, including a cooking <abbr title="n. 用具">**utensil**</abbr>, a <abbr title="n. 泛光灯">**floodlight**</abbr>, and a <abbr title="n. 绷带">**bandage**</abbr> for injuries. Even a small <abbr title="n. 舵">**rudder**</abbr> was added to steer the sledge.

My partner Max and I received an <abbr title="n. 电子邮件">**e-mail**</abbr> with the route. We had to <abbr title="v. 聚集">**converge**</abbr> at the old cabin before dark. The <abbr title="adj. 气候的">**climatic**</abbr> conditions were terrible, like an <abbr title="n. 大西洋">**Atlantic**</abbr> storm. Mr. Thompson once told us about his <abbr title="n. 短途探险">**foray**</abbr> into politics; he dreamed of a <abbr title="adj. 社会主义的">**socialist**</abbr> community, but he learned that nature does not care about ideals. “<abbr title="n. 生存">**survival**</abbr> is the final test,” he said. “You must <abbr title="v. 综合">**synthesize**</abbr> all your skills for the <abbr title="n. 执行">**execution**</abbr> of the plan.”

We set off. Almost immediately, deep snow forced us to <abbr title="v. 趟过">**wade**</abbr> through it. I had to <abbr title="v. 紧握">**clench**</abbr> my fists to keep my fingers warm. Max noticed a <abbr title="n. 通知">**notice**</abbr> on a tree: “Old <abbr title="n. 矿坑">**mine**</abbr> — keep out.” But it was the perfect <abbr title="n. 领土">**territory**</abbr> to shelter. Inside, a <abbr title="n. 蝙蝠">**bat**</abbr> flew past me, making me scream. Max laughed, then used <abbr title="n. 摩擦">**friction**</abbr> to start a fire with sticks, rubbing them <abbr title="adv. 狂热地">**feverishly**</abbr>. When flames grew too big, we had to <abbr title="v. 闷熄">**smother**</abbr> them with a blanket. Our clothes had become <abbr title="adj. 潮湿的">**damped**</abbr> from sweat, so we dried them near the fire. Water dripped into a <abbr title="n. 排水管">**drain**</abbr> in the floor.

Suddenly, a <abbr title="n. 路霸">**roadhog**</abbr> on a snowmobile roared outside, almost hitting our sledge. “That guy is a real **roadhog**!” Max shouted. ### 01

### 02

The old **<abbr title="n. 城堡">chateau</abbr>** stood on a hill. Wind and rain caused **<abbr title="n. 侵蚀">erosion</abbr>** of its stone walls, leaving deep lines. Inside, it was now a secret lab. His words were an **<abbr title="n. 攻击">assault</abbr>** on Dr. Aris’s rules. Dr. Aris decided to **<abbr title="v. 谴责">condemn</abbr>** Jax’s actions in front of everyone. “You must not **<abbr title="v. 诱惑">tempt</abbr>** the creatures with ideas of freedom!” he said. In the **<abbr title="n. 闪光">flash</abbr>** of a dying light, Drake saw Jax holding a fake creature. A tiny piece broke off, a **<abbr title="adj. 离散的">discrete</abbr>** bit of soap, separate from the rest. The two factions began to **<abbr title="v. 竞争">vie</abbr>** for control of the door. ### 01

The cloak had a <abbr title="adj. 有纹理的">**textured**</abbr> pattern; it felt bumpy, not smooth. Her dog, Buddy, showed great <abbr title="n. 忠诚">**loyalty**</abbr>. He never left her side.

One day, the <abbr title="n. 院长">**dean**</abbr>, the head of the school, showed a <abbr title="n. 纪录片">**documentary**</abbr>. It was a film about a <abbr title="adj. 热带的">**tropical**</abbr> island near the <abbr title="n. 赤道">**equator**</abbr>. The island was hot and full of rain. After the film, Lily found an old map in the cloak’s pocket. The map would <abbr title="v. 揭示">**reveal**</abbr> a secret <abbr title="n. 火山口">**crater**</abbr>—a big round hole on the mountain.

Her parents said, “That’s just a <abbr title="adj. 普通的">**generic**</abbr> story. No <abbr title="n. 例外">**exception**</abbr>! You cannot go.” This news <abbr title="v. 使沮丧">**depressed**</abbr> Lily. But Buddy’s happy bark <abbr title="v. 使宽慰">**relieved**</abbr> her. She took some <abbr title="n. 点心">**refreshment**</abbr>, like cookies, and a few <abbr title="n. 美元">**buck**</abbr>s.

She rode a <abbr title="n. 电车">**tram**</abbr>, a kind of city train, to the airport. At the airport, <abbr title="n. 海关">**customs**</abbr> officers checked bags. An officer <abbr title="v. 评估">**assessed**</abbr> her bag and tried to <abbr title="v. 阻止">**inhibit**</abbr> her from taking a strange stone. Lily tried to <abbr title="v. 交流">**communicate**</abbr> with him. They made a <abbr title="n. 妥协">**compromise**</abbr>. She could keep the stone if she didn’t take other things. On the plane, she felt a short <abbr title="n. 分离">**separation**</abbr> from home. The plane crossed the equator, and it got very hot.

She arrived on the tropical island. The first village was <abbr title="adj. 贫困的">**impoverished**</abbr>; the houses were small and old. An <abbr title="adj. 专制的">**autocratic**</abbr> <abbr title="n. 地方法官">**magistrate**</abbr> asked for <abbr title="adj. 过度的">**excessive**</abbr> money. Lily shook her head. A kind woman at the tourist <abbr title="n. 办事处">**bureau**</abbr> gave her a free map. This was a great <abbr title="n. 恩惠">**boon**</abbr>! Lily walked around. She saw a boy on a <abbr title="n. 秋千">**swing**</abbr>, going back and forth. She saw an old wooden <abbr title="n. 马车">**wagon**</abbr>. A man could <abbr title="v. 雕刻">**sculpt**</abbr> a bird from wood. A woman in a long <abbr title="n. 长袍">**gown**</abbr> walked by. Lily stopped at a <abbr title="adj. 浑浊的">**turbid**</abbr> pond; the water was so muddy you couldn’t see the bottom. Fish in the pond used <abbr title="n. 鳃">**gill**</abbr>s—openings on their heads—to breathe. Nearby, she saw a deep <abbr title="n. 坑">**pit**</abbr>. Inside, an <abbr title="n. 蚯蚓">**earthworm**</abbr> crawled on a <abbr title="adj. 腐烂的">**rotten**</abbr> log.

Lily asked the villagers about their <abbr title="adj. 民族的">**ethnic**</abbr> ways, like their songs and dances. Then she followed the map toward the crater. An <abbr title="adj. 明显的">**overt**</abbr> sign, big and red, pointed the way. She had to <abbr title="v. 使用">**wield**</abbr> a stick to push aside some rocks. Inside the crater, she saw a <abbr title="adj. 复杂的">**sophisticated**</abbr> lock with many tiny parts. She used the key from her cloak. Click! <abbr title="adj. 立即的">**instant**</abbr> joy filled her heart. The lock was part of a <abbr title="n. 修复">**restoration**</abbr> project to fix an old treasure box. Inside, she found a bright stone—a <abbr title="n. 纪念品">**souvenir**</abbr> from her grandma. The stone had a <abbr title="adj. 混合的">**hybrid**</abbr> color, like blue and green mixed. This was the <abbr title="n. 前奏">**prelude**</abbr> to her grandma’s secret past. Lily understood her family’s <abbr title="n. 所有权">**ownership**</abbr> of this treasure.

Just then, she saw a little bird in a cage. She opened the cage door. The bird’s <abbr title="n. 解放">**emancipation**</abbr> made her smile. Lily went home. She showed the stone to the dean. “This is <abbr title="adj. 相关的">**pertinent**</abbr> to our lesson about family history,” said the dean. “Your trip is a special **exception**!” Lily felt proud. She hugged Buddy, whose **loyalty** had never wavered. In the market, she even used a **buck** to buy a cold drink. She was happy.

## 02

Tim arrived at the <abbr title="n. 海边">**seaside**</abbr> on a <abbr title="adj. 潮湿的">**damp**</abbr> morning. His Aunt Mary ran a small <abbr title="adj. 租赁的">**rental**</abbr> cottage in the <abbr title="adj. 偏远的">**outlying**</abbr> area. A thick <abbr title="n. 薄雾">**mist**</abbr> made everything look <abbr title="adj. 模糊的">**indistinct**</abbr>. The cottage was on the <abbr title="n. 边缘">**verge**</abbr> of a cliff, and Tim heard the <abbr title="n. 海浪">**surf**</abbr> crashing below. Aunt Mary was a <abbr title="n. 天主教徒">**Catholic**</abbr> and had a little chapel. She had kept a <abbr title="n. 预订">**reservation**</abbr> for Tim.

A <abbr title="n. 寄宿者">**boarder**</abbr> named Mr. Finn, who had a <abbr title="adj. 热衷的">**keen**</abbr> interest in <abbr title="adj. 植物学的">**botanical**</abbr> gardens, was studying a <abbr title="n. 目录">**catalogue**</abbr> of plants. Another boarder, Mrs. Down, was a musician who once dreamed of <abbr title="n. 明星地位">**stardom**</abbr>. She played the <abbr title="n. 羽管键琴">**harpsichord**</abbr>. Her <abbr title="n. 表演">**rendition**</abbr> of a song could <abbr title="v. 唤醒">**rouse**</abbr> even the sleepiest guest. But the old <abbr title="n. 水龙头">**faucet**</abbr> in the kitchen would <abbr title="v. 嘶嘶作响">**hiss**</abbr> and drip. Its <abbr title="n. 过度分泌">**over-secretion**</abbr> of water drove the handyman mad. He mopped the floor with a <abbr title="n. 拖把">**mop**</abbr>, leaning on his <abbr title="n. 拐杖">**cane**</abbr>.

Tim went to the town. There was a <abbr title="n. 监狱">**jail**</abbr>. A <abbr title="n. 暴乱">**riot**</abbr> had broken out. The townsfolk were on the verge of panic. Stories were <abbr title="adj. 矛盾的">**contradictory**</abbr>. A man had died, and some called him a <abbr title="n. 烈士">**martyr**</abbr>, but others said he followed a dangerous <abbr title="n. 教条">**dogma**</abbr>. Tim felt an <abbr title="adj. 情感的">**affective**</abbr> connection to the injustice. He wanted to <abbr title="v. 面对">**confront**</abbr> the truth, but the police were <abbr title="adj. 冷漠的">**impersonal**</abbr>. They could not <abbr title="v. 精确指出">**pinpoint**</abbr> the riot's cause. Everything was shrouded in <abbr title="n. 模糊不清">**obscurity**</abbr>.

Back in the garden, Tim saw a <abbr title="n. 鹌鹑">**quail**</abbr> hiding behind a <abbr title="adj. 湿润的">**moist**</abbr> bush. The sun's <abbr title="n. 强光">**glare**</abbr> <abbr title="v. 使目眩">**dazzled**</abbr> Tim, making him squint. Mr. Finn showed him a plant with a <abbr title="n. 触手">**tentacle**</abbr>-like root. He planned an <abbr title="n. 挖掘">**excavation**</abbr> to study the soil, talking about nature's <abbr title="n. 公平">**equity**</abbr>. Tim helped <abbr title="v. 踩踏板">**pedal**</abbr> his bike to the site, but fell and hurt his <abbr title="n. 鼻孔">**nostril**</abbr>. An indistinct figure helped him—it was the handyman with his <abbr title="n. 小笔记本">**pocketbook**</abbr>. He recorded every <abbr title="n. 交易">**transaction**</abbr> with great <abbr title="n. 细节">**nicety**</abbr>, even small ones. Tim <abbr title="v. 品尝">**savoured**</abbr> a sweet he offered.

That night, Tim had a dream of a <abbr title="n. 食腐动物">**scavenger**</abbr> bird and a <abbr title="n. 瘟疫">**pestilence**</abbr> of bad luck. But awake, he remembered the <abbr title="adj. 形成的">**formative**</abbr> day. The harpsichord's music, Mrs. Down's lost stardom, the martyr's idea of equity—all had <abbr title="n. 优点">**merit**</abbr>. Tim learned not to be impersonal. He would savour these seaside memories, even if some truths remain in obscurity.
